# S1 progress (ESA backend behind ISuffixTree, managed, no native code)

Rules: [PROCESS.md](PROCESS.md). Gates: [PLAN.md](PLAN.md) section S1 plus **peak RSS during build <= 24 B/char** (about 240 MB at 10 Mbp; Ukkonen peaks at 1.76 GB).
Pre-registered S1a gate (10 Mbp random DNA, 1 thread, this machine class): SA+LCP build <= 3.3 s (<= 10x libsais 0.328 s), index <= 16 B/char incl. text.
Targets: ceiling goal <= 1.0 s (3x), aspiration <= 0.5 s (1.5x); libsais reference 0.328 s, libdivsufsort 0.662 s, Go 0.73 s.

Decisions already taken by the owner: managed only; positions are `long` in the new contract from the start; contract-first (stable contract, replaceable engines);
Ukkonen stays untouched (oracle, online); ESA persistent container is a separate format kind; native accelerator not allowed.

**Owner requirement 2026-11-04 (categorical, supersedes ADR-003 / ADR-002 wherever they say "build in RAM, then write the file"):** the persistent index (SESA) must be built by **streaming straight into the memory-mapped files**; "build in RAM, then write" is rejected. Only a small fixed buffer is allowed; working memory must not grow with n. The input text may be read through a read-only mapping (it is the input, not the index). The in-memory backend is unaffected (its index lives in RAM by definition). Consequence: an external-memory / streaming construction algorithm must be chosen first; the S1a in-RAM kernels stay as the in-memory builder and as candidate building blocks, but are not the persistent builder. Selection: [ADR-004](adr/ADR-004-external-memory-construction.md) (research step, 2026-11-04; E0-E3 measured 2026-11-09 and B-DCS decided by the owner the same day, ADR-004 sections 5a and 6b): primary "B-DCS" = Karkkainen blockwise suffix sorting with a difference-cover sample in lexicographic buckets, LCP by a chunked external Phi/PLCP pass, child table by one scan with a spilling stack, navigation pack with Psi instead of ISA; fallback SAscan/pSAscan; prototype gates E0-E9 decide, owner questions Q1-Q9 open. S1b work packages 1b.7 (container writer) and anything that assumes an in-RAM build of the persistent file wait for ADR-004.

Answers to the S1-0 open questions (orchestrator, 2026-11-03; reversible, none touches a published format):
1. The 16 B/char gate applies to **Full** (index with the navigation pack); Core and Full are both reported.
2. The 3.3 s gate applies twice: S1a = SA+LCP <= 3.3 s; S1b = whole pipeline (SA+LCP+child table+navigation pack) <= 3.3 s.
3. Kind/extension name `SESA` / `.sesa` accepted as provisional (nothing is published yet, rename is free until the first release).
4. `NativeMemory` (BCL API, not P/Invoke, no native binary) is allowed from S1 on where it helps (large aligned arrays, no GC zeroing), always wrapped in an owning `IDisposable`/`SafeHandle`.

Design (S1-0, 2026-11-03): [ADR-001 index contract + suffix-link answer](adr/ADR-001-index-contract.md), [ADR-002 SA-IS, LCP, memory, optimization roadmap, JIT/AOT](adr/ADR-002-sa-construction.md), [ADR-003 SESA container](adr/ADR-003-esa-container-format.md).
Summary of the recommendations they contain: SA has n+1 entries (virtual `$`, `SA[0]=n`) so the ESA is node-for-node the Ukkonen terminated tree; the generic algorithms need only one navigator (suffix link called at exactly one place, `SuffixTreeAlgorithms.cs:477`, internal nodes only) and `Traverse`; suffix links are computed lazily as `LCA(Psi(lb), Psi(rb))` from an optional navigation pack (ISA + RMQ/PSV/NSV), not stored; SA-IS int32 with int64-ready generics; LCP by Phi/PLCP into a compact U8+overflow array; container kind SESA (magic `89 53 45 53 41 0D 0A 1A`).

Each work package: failing test first, implement, the three existing suites unchanged and green (commands in PROCESS.md "Environment"), commit `ST-S1: <id> ...`, push, update this file (Log). A reviewer (never the implementer) runs after 1a.9, after 1o, and after 1b.9.

## STATE 2026-11-09 (later): ADR-004 prototypes E0-E3 DONE - DECIDED by the owner: B-DCS primary, page-cache floor fixed, F1 fallback

**Owner decision 2026-11-09 (after the measured E0-E3, ADR-004 section 6b):** B-DCS is the construction algorithm of the persistent SESA builder; the minimum page cache "about 1x (text + DCS rank file)" is accepted and fixed as a documented requirement; F1 (SAscan family) stays the fallback. E4-E9 (external-memory PH1, PH4-PH7, 500 Mbp) were NOT authorised by that decision and needed a separate yes; 1b.7 stayed blocked until then. **Owner decision 2026-11-11: that yes is given - E4-E9 first, then 1b.7 (after 1b.6, which is done); one agent at a time, cost and time stated before each launch; details and the E4 label slip in ADR-004 section 6c.** The text below is the state as reported before the decisions.

**Owner decisions 2026-11-16 (after E7 and E9; ADR-004 section 6c has the same list):** (1) no switch to F1; B-DCS stays. (2) 1b.7c implements PH4 in base mode only; the bounded mode may be a separate package after E8, only if needed. (3) Minimum machine as a documented requirement (ESTIMATES for 3x10^9): page cache about 1x (text + rank file) = 1.1-1.5 GB; disk 60-70 GB with fast sequential write (scratch about 36 GB, outputs about 28-31 GB); the page cache PH4's 12 B/char scratch needs beyond that is not known until E8 (not measured at 3 Gbp). (4) E8 package: a Linux container image (x86-64 and arm64) and scripts for the owner's Mac (Docker Desktop), after E9; no Docker daemon in the orchestrator's session, so the image is delivered unbuilt and untested; cost stated before launch. (5) N_base: decided 2026-11-18 (16 Mi).

**Owner decisions 2026-11-18 (after the E8 run on the Mac; ADR-004 section 6c):** (1) A1 relaxed: the builder's anonymous working set is bounded by about 320 MB (measured 309.7 / 309.2 MB at 3x10^9 with four threads); the gate G2 of the builder is RssAnon <= 320 MB at every n with the default parameters and four threads; no tuning package. (2) N_base = 16 Mi symbols. (3) 1b.7a authorised (forecast 3-5 h, 0.8-1.2 M tokens, +-50 %); 1b.7b-e each need their own stated cost and yes.

**Owner decisions 2026-11-18, the container format (the four ADR-003 gaps of 1b.7b; ADR-004 section 6c, ADR-003 amended):** (1) TEXT codec 4 layout as the verified prototype (`PARAM0` = b, `ELEM_WIDTH` = 0, `LENGTH = ceil(n*b/8) + 64`); (2) `TEXT_SHA256` = hash of the code units as bytes for codecs 1, 2 and 4, UTF-16LE for codec 3; (3) own optional section kind 13 `COUNTS`; (4) `b = ceil(log2 sigma)`. 1b.7e implements the amended ADR-003.

**Owner decisions 2026-11-19 (given in the session; not yet in ADR-002 / ADR-003; items 2 and 3 are recommendations for S2, not approvals):** (1) **Scope of "continue without stopping": every remaining package of S1b (1b.7f, 1b.8, 1b.9, 1b.11, 1b.10); CORRECTED later on 2026-11-19 by the owner: before S2 the approval is manual again, S2-S8 are NOT approved** (the first answer, "S2-S8 including S7", is withdrawn); the orchestrator stops only to ask a question and gives the cost of each package in one line before it starts. (2) Defaults taken for the open decisions of PLAN.md section 7 (reversible): positions stay `long` in the API with additive overloads (no breaking change); the hand-off runs of S2 and S5 are delivered as a package for the owner's Mac, like E8; S7 is in scope. (3) **Width of stored positions** (the owner asked whether the hybrid of the Ukkonen v6 tree, short entries first and long ones after, can be used): decided by measurement at the start of S2. The width (`POS_WIDTH` 4 or 5 bytes) is chosen once from n, which PH0 knows, so no promotion in mid-build is needed; SA, ISA and PSI hold random positions, so a hybrid by value would need escapes for about 31 % of the entries at n = 6.2x10^9 (both strands of a human genome), about 6.5 B per entry against 5 B for a fixed 40-bit entry, plus a second read per access; LCP is already a hybrid (U8 or U16 with an overflow list); a delta-from-index form with escapes is a candidate for CHILD only. A hybrid is adopted only where the measurement shows it clearly smaller and not slower. (4) **2026-11-20, the owner: no debts may remain before S2; `Resume` (ADR-004 crash safety) is to be implemented (package 1b.9e); every item of the debt register below is closed or handed to the owner before the approval for S2 is asked.**

(The policy below is to be pasted into every 1b.7 agent brief.)

**Test policy for 1b.7a-e (owner yes 2026-11-16).** Aim: during development only the tests that the change can affect; the rest only at the end of a package, and then only the tests that touch what the change could have reached. Three levels:

1. **During development (every edit loop):** only the test classes of the changed production classes, tests first (red before green). No project-wide run.
2. **At the end of a package (before the package commit that reports "done"):** the *affected set* = every test project that references a changed project, directly or through project references (reverse closure), run in its default build, plus the category rules: Exhaustive and Fuzz of `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` only if `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed; Vectors only if the SA or LCP kernels changed; the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) only if code that it compiles differently changed; Ukkonen, Persistent, Mcp.Core and EmProto only if their own projects, `SuffixTree.Core` or a shared `.props` changed. For 1b.7 the affected set always contains `SuffixTree.Persistent` (the dispatcher opens both kinds, the v6 tests stay unchanged, the v6 loader must reject an ESA file) and `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests`; `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (about 11 minutes) only when `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` or code moved out of it changed.
3. **Full run of all categories:** once at 1b.9 and once before 1b.10 (the S1 gate and the review). Nothing else needs it.

**Widening triggers (run the full affected set of level 2 plus the neighbours at once, in the same package):** a change to contract types of `SuffixTree.Core`, to the container format or the section table, to a shared `.props`, to a test helper or a shared base class, to a data file that tests read (for example `sa_vectors.tsv`), or to anything loaded by reflection or started as a process.

**Residual risk (stated, not hidden):** behavioural couplings that go through data files, reflection or child processes are not visible in project references; a miss of that kind would first show at 1b.9. Each package report lists the test sets it ran and the sets it skipped with the rule that allowed skipping.

**E0-E3 done; every gate is met at its stated definition; the E3 rule "above 5x: the design needs a minimum page cache" is reached for tie-rich DNA below an effective cache of about 1x (text + rank) and is documented, not decided** (one implementer context; code `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`, tests `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (58, about 16 s), results `analysis/em` (RESULTS.md, TSVs, scripts), ADR-004 section 5a).
E0 sampler 0.64 % overhead, counters within 0.05 % of `/proc/self/io`. E1: `dna_10m` 1.006 s on 1 thread, but a^n / tandem / N run are 117x-502x slower (a^n 10 M above 10 minutes): DCS needed. E2: with the difference-cover sample every family is <= 10x `dna_10m` (largest 7.9x), DCS costs 7.4 % on `dna_10m`, covers proved by test for X = 2..2300, no tuning round. E3 (100 Mbp, 4 threads, K = n/3000): RssAnon 74 MB (cap 256), identical output for 1/2/4 threads and three K values, 14.6 s, 19.7 B/char I/O, 4.2 B/char scratch; slowdown with the page cache limited to the nominal 0.5x (rank + text): 1.43x (random DNA) and 1.65x (genome-like), but **a 11-14x cliff on tie-rich DNA when the file cache falls below about 1x (text + rank)**; the true 0.5x could not be reached at this scale (the anonymous footprint shares the cgroup limit; one OOM kill at the lowest limit).
Was open for the owner (answered above: B-DCS, floor accepted, F1 fallback): B-DCS versus F1 (the numbers favour B-DCS up to 100 Mbp; the cache cliff and everything beyond 100 Mbp, PH1 in external memory, PH4-PH7 (E4-E9) are unmeasured), and whether the "minimum page cache about 1x (text + rank)" is acceptable (Q4). Nothing of the ADR recommendation text was changed. Not started: E4-E9, S1b work.

State: branch `claude/stoic-maxwell-0olr2z` pushed, working tree clean, nothing running, no agent alive. Done in this session (details in the Log, section "S1b 2026-11-09"): 1b.0 contract types of ADR-001 section 6 in `SuffixTree.Core/Indexing` + conformance suite + Ukkonen oracle adapter; 1b.1 in-memory ESA (`EsaIndex`, internal in `SuffixTree.Esa`): stack scan over an LCP stream, three child-table layouts, header statistics, `Traverse`; 1b.2 search (descent, `Contains`, `CountOccurrences`, `FindAllOccurrences`, lazy `Locate`); fresh-mutant audit. Verification at the end of the session (HEAD of the audit commit `25d1ceb5`): Esa default 371/371 in the normal and in the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`); explicit categories Exhaustive 38/38, Fuzz 24/24, Vectors 632/632 (`SEQERON_BENCH_DIR=/tmp/stbench`); older suites 494 / 672 / 137; build warning-free; Ukkonen and persistent v6 not modified.

What 1b.3 starts from: `EsaIndex` (text, `PreparedText`, `int[] SuffixArray` with n + 1 entries, `CompactLcp`, `ChildTable`, `EsaStatistics` with `LrsDepth` / `LrsRank` / `NodeCount` as `long`), `LcpIntervalScan` (the reusable bottom-up scan: events `LIndex` / `Close`), `Traverse` (diagnostic path, 4 B/char transient for the leftmost occurrences), `TryMatch` -> rank interval. The default child layout is `EsaIndex.DefaultLayoutFor(n)`. Still NOT done in S1b: native analysis operations (1b.3), navigator and suffix links (1b.4a, 1b.4), contract implementation `EsaSuffixIndex` (1b.5; the conformance suite has only been run against the Ukkonen adapter), facade `EsaSuffixTree : ISuffixTree` and backend factory (1b.6; `NodeCount` > int must be refused there, review MAJ-7), container (1b.7), full differential fuzz (1b.8), gate measurement (1b.9), review and README (1b.10). `BUCKET` and `NEXTSYM` were not built: the gates are met without them.

Open points handed to the next package: (a) a corrupt child table can make the descent loop forever (eight mutants of the layouts did), so the `Checked` mode of 1b.7 must bound every walk (strictly increasing boundaries); (b) the scan stack is in RAM (16 B per entry, up to n entries: 16 MB for a^n at 1 Mbp, i.e. 16 B/char in the worst case): the streaming builder of ADR-004 PH5 spills it, the scan interface does not change; (c) the container codec for `CHILD` is open: `Tagged` is 8-18 % faster but holds ranks in 30 bits, `Packed` is the only 4-byte layout that reaches the 3 x 10^9 positions of ADR-004 in a uint entry; (d) the Esa default suite grew from 287 to 371 tests and takes about 40 s.

## S1a-opt iterations (one per roadmap item of ADR-002 section 5; each accepted or reverted by the PROCESS.md stopping rule)

Accept only if the 10 Mbp SA+LCP time improves by >= 3 % (median of 5, JIT numbers only) and everything passes (unit, exhaustive, `Vectors`, fuzz v1 vs `SaisReference`); otherwise revert and keep the measured number in the Log. Stop after 3 consecutive iterations < 3 % or at <= 0.36 s.

## Test strategy (all work packages)

Oracle hierarchy (a mismatch at any level stops everything, PROCESS.md rule 3): L0 definitions and brute force (n <= 2000; exhaustive small alphabets); L1 the existing `...ByDefinition` implementations; L2 the in-memory Ukkonen tree; L3 the persistent v6 tree; L4 libsais output hashes (outside the repo); L5 `SuffixTreeSerializer.CalculateLogicalHash` and the `Traverse` event stream (equal across backends if H-OCC holds).

Differential on **every public operation** against L2 (and L3 for a subset): all members of `ISuffixTreeSearch`, `ISuffixTreeAnalysis`, `ISuffixTreeDiagnostics`, `ISuffixTree` (`ISuffixTreeSearch.cs`, `ISuffixTreeAnalysis.cs`, `ISuffixTreeDiagnostics.cs`, `ISuffixTree.cs:44-80`) with positive, absent, empty, single-character, longer-than-text and boundary patterns/queries; MEM/MUM/LCS/anchors with random and mutated queries (substitutions, indels, repeats), minLength 1..n. **Results the contract leaves implementation-specific are compared by property, not equality** (ADR-001 section 7): LRS representative on ties, `PositionInText` of `LongestCommonSubstringInfo` and of anchors (must be a valid occurrence; the match must be maximal), order of `FindAllOccurrences`.

Adversarial strings (deterministic and identical in the C# tests and the outside generator; PRNG = SplitMix64 with the seed in the name, because `System.Random` sequences are not stable across versions): `a^n`; runs (alternating letters, run lengths from the PRNG in 1..L, L in {2, 16, 256, 65536}); periodic with period p in {2, 3, 5, 7, 64, 65}; Fibonacci word (`f1 = "a"`, `f2 = "ab"`, `f(k) = f(k-1) f(k-2)`, truncated to n); Thue-Morse (`t[i] = popcount(i) mod 2`); de Bruijn sequences B(2,k), B(4,k) (FKM Lyndon-word construction, truncated or full); `a^k b a^k b ...`; random binary; random DNA (ACGT); random protein (20 letters); skewed alphabets (one rare symbol); all-distinct symbols (sigma = n up to 65,536); UTF-16: BMP characters above 255, valid surrogate pairs, lone surrogates, U+0000, U+FFFF; n = 0, 1, 2, 3; texts that maximise LMS count (`abab...`) and recursion depth.

Exhaustive small: all strings over sigma 2 up to length 14, sigma 3 up to 9, sigma 4 up to 7 (SA, LCP, tree statistics, `Traverse` stream, count of every substring).

Differential fuzz harness (built in 1a.7, extended in 1b.8): seeded generator families x alphabets x sizes, time/iteration budget from the command line, every failure prints `family seed n op` and is minimized (shrink by deleting/merging characters while the mismatch persists) into a regression file that becomes a permanent test; the harness is the same code that the S8 CI job will run.

Machine rule: tests may run only when no other build or test is running (`uptime`, `ps`, PROCESS.md:35); heavy categories (`Vectors`, `Performance`, long fuzz) are run explicitly.

## Measurement protocol for the gate (1a.8, 1a.9, 1b.9, 1o.*)

* **Inputs**: `dna_10m` (the gate), `dna_1m`, `rep_10m`, `prot_1m`; `dna_20m`, `dna_50m` for scaling. Regenerate with `analysis/bench/gen/gen_inputs.py` and check `sha256sum -c MANIFEST.sha256` first (`HANDOFF.md` section 0). Queries: the `present20`, `absent20`, `locate8`, `memq100k` files of the same manifest.
* **Harness**: a console project that loads the input into memory first (excluded from timing), then calls the build entry point and prints `R<TAB>op<TAB>metric<TAB>value<TAB>unit<TAB>notes` lines, driven by `lib/benchlib.py measure --raw RAW --tool seqeron-esa --version <git short hash> --input dna_10m --repeats 5 -- CMD` (`HANDOFF.md` section 1). Until S0 work package 0.7 fixes a repository home for the harness, it lives next to `analysis/bench/seqeron/SeqeronBench`; afterwards in the S0 harness project.
* **Metrics**: `build_sa_s`, `build_lcp_s`, `build_child_s`, `build_total_s` (internal `Stopwatch` around the library call only, the same convention as the libsais row), process wall time and CPU, **peak RSS from `wait4`/`ru_maxrss` of the fresh process** (as in the other rows), index bytes per char by section (sum of container section lengths, text included; Core and Full profiles reported separately), `Checked` and `Trusted` access, count 20-mers (present/absent, 1 and 4 threads), locate 1000 8-mers, MEM/MUM/anchors on `memq100k`, cold and warm `Open`.
* **Runs**: 5 **fresh processes** per row (JIT warm-up included, which is what a user gets), report median and min/max; in addition one steady-state row (5 builds in one process, the last 3 reported). Release build, `DOTNET_` runtime settings at defaults (tiered compilation and dynamic PGO on), 1 thread (`DOTNET_gcServer=0`), no other process running; check `uptime` before and after.
* **Machine drift control**: run the libsais reference harness (`competitors/sa_c/sa_bench.c` flow, `HANDOFF.md` section 2) at the start and end of each session; if its `dna_10m` time differs from 0.328 s by more than 10 %, annotate the session "drift" and report both the absolute time and the ratio to the same-session libsais time.
* **Acceptance arithmetic**: gate values are the medians above; an optimization iteration is accepted per PROCESS.md:26-29 on the JIT numbers only. The NativeAOT row (1o.10) is recorded under a separate tool tag and never mixed in.
* **Reporting**: every Log entry contains commit hash, input, median (min/max), peak RSS MB and B/char, index B/char (Core/Full), and the libsais same-session reference.

- 2026-11-03 1o.1a [rejected, reverted] experiment ade9e441, revert commit follows it. **LCP pointer kernel** (`LcpKernel`, int-only, behind `Buf<T>`, selected by `--lcp-kernel`; the reference untouched): Phi over a zeroed native block with every SA entry range-checked, PLCP in place with the escape counting fused into the loop, pass 3 straight into the compact arrays through `CompactLcp.FromParts`, escape-list overrun checked in the escape branch. **First attempt measured 1.113 (SA+LCP) and 1.52 (LCP phase), i.e. 11 % SLOWER** (15 pairs, `abA`): the PLCP loop took 0.33 s against 0.14 s. Two causes found with the profile and the JIT listing: (1) the 8-byte word compare with a trailing-zero count makes the carried length `l` depend on the loaded data, so the cache misses of consecutive iterations are serialised, while the reference's byte loop with a branch lets the CPU speculate `l` and overlap them; fixed by comparing the first 8 symbols one by one with a branch and using words only for long matches; (2) `Math.Max(j, p)` was compiled to a conditional JUMP on a coin flip (j < p), costing about 0.03 s of mispredictions; fixed by a branchless maximum. `AggressiveOptimization` (tried on the loops, and the Stopwatch calls moved out of the hot method, because an OSR frame kept the address-exposed `Buf` locals in the Tier0 frame) made no measurable difference and was dropped. **Final A/B session** (2026-11-03 14:26-14:28 UTC, `dna_10m`, load 0.20, nothing else running; libsais SA+PLCP+LCP 0.572 s before / 0.565 s after = -1.2 % drift; A = reference LCP, B = pointer LCP, 9 A/A pairs, 15 A/B pairs, order alternating, every child reproduced the libsais vectors): A/A median 0.9915 (0.9628-1.0476), noise band [0.9559, 1.0441]; **SA+LCP total: A median 1.375 s (1.311-1.523), B 1.362 s (1.296-1.430), paired B/A median 0.9937 (0.9045-1.0532), 95 % interval of the median [0.9824, 1.0021]**; LCP phase 0.318 s -> 0.302 s, ratio 0.9298 (0.8414-1.0169), interval [0.9006, 0.9729] (a real -7 % on the LCP phase, 1.2 % of the total); SA phase 1.0150 (same code on both sides: the spread of one session); peak RSS (VmHWM) +6.2 % (156.6 -> 166.4 MB: the native Phi cannot reuse the memory that the GC freed from the SA-IS temporaries, the managed Phi can); ESA/libsais 2.43x (A) and 2.41x (B). **Verdict REJECT** (bar: median <= 0.97, outside the band, interval below 1.0; the result is 0.6 % faster). The profile of one warm build shows where the LCP phase went: pass 3 0.095 -> 0.065 s, escape counting 0.015 -> 0 (fused), PLCP 0.134 -> 0.15-0.17 s (slower), Phi unchanged 0.065: LCP is memory-latency bound (random text and PLCP reads), bounds checks were not its cost, so the lever left for the LCP phase is memory-level parallelism (prefetch, roadmap R3), not pointer code. **Evidence for the kernel before the revert:** default 216 tests in both build modes (raw and `ESA_BOUNDS`), `Category=Exhaustive` 9/9 (74 s; binary strings <= 20 through SA reference + LCP kernel against Kasai, the five 10^6 texts with expected kinds, the 38 families at 10^5 and sigma = n = 65,536 with all four poison patterns), `Category=Vectors` 158/158 (79 reference + 79 pipeline, libsais hashes), `Category=Fuzz` 6/6 (pipeline tiers 10^6 / 10^5 / 10^3 cases, seed 1: naive / verifier / Kasai AND entry-by-entry equality with the reference), older suites 494 / 672 / 137; `jit_check.sh` on the optimized listings (Tier1-OSR of `ComputePlcp`, `WriteU8`): 0 range-check helpers, 0 accessor calls, and the negative control (the reference's safe `CountSymbols`) reports 1 range-check helper, exit 1. **Mutants** (`analysis/mutation/lcp_kernel_mutants.json`, runner `run_kernel_mutants.py`, 21 mutants K01-K21 = the reviewer's M21-M28/M55 mapped to the kernel plus its own): 18 killed by the default tests in the Release configuration; **K14 (escape-list bound check removed: writes past the managed lists) and K15 (Phi not zero-initialised: reads poison) survive Release and are killed only by the checked build** - so the checked configuration is a required acceptance step, not an extra; K20 (head loop falls through instead of returning) is equivalent (the word loop re-finds the mismatch at offset 0). One real test gap was found by mutant K13: texts with up to 256 distinct symbols are remapped to BYTES even when the units exceed 255, so the 16-bit kernel instantiation only runs for alphabets above 256 and none of the existing texts had a long common prefix there (test `SixteenBitSymbols_WithLongCommonPrefixes_PlaceTheMismatchInsideTheWord` added, K13 now killed). - 2026-11-04 1o.3b [x] (commit follows this entry). **LCP in suffix-array order: one sequential scan with one prefetched random access per rank, three-pass fallback** (roadmap R3/R7 in spirit; fuses and removes passes). **Why:** the profile of 1o.3a showed the LCP phase bound by random accesses into two 4-byte-per-character arrays (Phi, PLCP: 40 MB each at 10 Mbp); a prefetch does not help scatter/gather passes limited by the line-fill buffers (about 12 outstanding lines, about 6 ns per random line). **Code:** `LcpKernel.Direct.cs` (`BuildDirect`, `TryDirect`, `ScanDirect`, `ResolveLong`, `EqualPrefix`), `Buf<T>.LoadVector128`, `LcpKernelKind.Direct`, `--lcp-kernel direct`; reference and the 1o.3a three-pass kernel untouched. **Scan:** for r = 1..n the 16 bytes (8 symbols for 16-bit texts) at `SA[r-1]` and `SA[r]` are compared with one `vpcmpeqb` + `vpmovmskb` + `tzcnt` (`Vector128.Equals(...).ExtractMostSignificantBits()`, portable): no branch on the data, no loop-carried dependency, the text line of `SA[r+32]` (and the line of its 16th byte) is prefetched, the result goes straight into the final byte array. A value below the vector width is exact; a full match is a "long" rank (rank in a list, the width as placeholder): on random DNA 0.1 %. Vector loads only when `n - max(a, b) >= width` (the scalar compare covers the last 15 positions), every SA entry is range-checked when read (`ArgumentException` like the reference). **Resolution:** each long rank continues from the vector width, vector by vector (not amortised: Kasai needs text order), writing the exact value or the escape 255 plus a list entry. **Bounded, then linear in the worst case:** the scan gives up when more than n/4 ranks are long, or after `max(n/32, 1024)` ranks when more than half of them are long (early exit for repetitive texts); the resolution gives up after 2n vector steps or when 16 * escapes > n (the kind would not be U8); every give-up falls back to the three-pass kernel, so the result is always the reference result (kind, escape list, every entry) for a valid suffix array and the time is O(n). For a non-permutation the result is unspecified but memory safe (tested in a guarded arena and in the checked build). **Distances:** scan 8/16/32/64 -> 68/64/64/61 ms in the warm profile (flat from 16; 32 kept), the second prefetch (the line of byte 16 of the vector, which straddles two lines in one case of four) 69 -> 63 ms; the scan costs about 6 ns per rank, the resolution about 1 ms. **Evidence:** default Esa tests 257 in both build modes (raw and `ESA_BOUNDS`; - 2026-11-04 1o.3c [x] (attempt 1 `cc40165b` rejected and reverted in `73f97abd`; attempt 2 `169f1236` superseded; attempt 3 `ee950b45` + hardening `73964ec6` accepted). **The direct LCP scan survives long repeats** (fix of REVIEW-S1a-opt MAJ-1 and MIN-4; condition C3: judged on `dna_10m`, `dna_1m`, `rep_10m` and eleven repeat-bearing rows). **Mechanism (MAJ-1):** the per-rank resolution of 1o.3b charges a repeat of length L about L^2 / 32 vector steps against a budget of 2 n, so one repeat longer than 8 sqrt(n) symbols made the attempt give up after the scan and the budget were spent, and the three-pass kernel ran from the start. **Design (final):** (1) the scan is unchanged (one prefetched vector compare per rank, rank list of the long ranks, give-up when more than a quarter, or after n / 32 ranks more than half, are long). (2) *Per-rank resolution first* (the 1o.3b loop, compiled and run exactly as before): its allowance is cumulative, 4,096 vector steps plus 4 per long rank processed so far (`ResolveBaseSteps`, `ResolveStepsPerLong`), checked after every full-vector step; random DNA needs 0.0-0.1 steps per long rank. (3) *Text-order resolution* when the allowance is exhausted, when the escape list of the byte kind overflows, or at once when there are at least 65,536 long ranks (`TextOrderMinLongs`): gather text position and predecessor of every long rank (`sa[r]`, `sa[r-1]`), counting sort by text position with a bitmap (set the bits, prefix counts per 64-bit word, scatter the (predecessor, k) records to the popcount index: no comparison sort, n/8 bytes of bitmap), then walk the positions in increasing order with the Kasai carry: the LCP of position j with its suffix-array predecessor is at least PLCP[j-1] - 1, so after the long position j-1 the comparison starts at max(PLCP[j-1] - 1, w), after any other position at w (the scan has seen w symbols; a position whose text predecessor is not long has PLCP < w, and a run of long positions ends only where the length is back at w). Total work O(n / w + long ranks) vector steps for every text; a repeat of L symbols costs L / 16 steps once and one step per position. The values are stored by long-rank index, the kind (`U8`/`U16`/`U32`) is chosen from the exact counts of values >= 255 and >= 65,535 (`CompactLcp.SelectKind`) and the long entries are written in rank order into the byte array (+ escape list) or into a widened `ushort`/`int` copy, so the kind no longer forces a fallback. 20 bytes per long rank (rank, position/value, predecessor, 8-byte sorted record), hence the quarter cap. (4) The three-pass kernel stays the fallback for the scan's give-up (more than a quarter long, or the n / 32 probe sees more than half): `rep_10m` (86 % long), `random-binary` (99 %), `two-copies`, `a^n`. **MIN-4:** the give-up allocates nothing that is touched (the rank list is virtual memory, the byte array is touched for the first 1,024 ranks only), the 3 n/16-int escape/rank lists of the old code are released before the text-order resolution allocates, and the dead workspace is released before the output arrays are allocated. **Attempts.** *Attempt 1* (text order for every long rank, a stratified 4,096-rank sample before any allocation, three lists in the scan): correct (default 281, Vectors 474, Fuzz 18, Exhaustive 24 raw and checked; 60 corpus rows equal libsais), and fast on the repeat rows (cold single runs: LCP phase 0.075-0.16 s against 0.45-0.58 s), but **rejected**: JIT. `build_lcp_jit_s` (new harness row, `JitInfo`) went from 3.7 ms (13 methods) to 7.5 ms (19 methods) in the LCP step, the whole step at 1 Mbp is 6.8 ms. Paired A/B against the 1o.3b chain (fresh processes, 15 pairs, 9 A/A): `dna_1m` SA+LCP **1.0580** [1.0458, 1.0725], A/A band [0.977, 1.023], LCP phase 0.00665 -> 0.01102 s; `dna_10m` 1.0245 [0.984, 1.056], band [0.935, 1.065], LCP phase 1.1105 [1.055, 1.174]; `rep_10m` 0.9876 [0.962, 1.023], band [0.92, 1.08]. *Attempt 2* (per-rank loop first with a budget of 4 steps per long rank of the whole list, text order after, no sample; JIT back to 12 methods / 3.8 ms): `dna_10m` 1.0021 [0.949, 1.012] (band [0.969, 1.031]), `dna_1m` 1.0066 [0.991, 1.028] (band [0.978, 1.022], LCP phase +0.17 ms), `rep_10m` 1.0097, embedded repeats 0.67-0.78, but `dup400-p20/p25` LCP phase 0.238 / 0.281 s (three-pass 0.25-0.28): a per-rank attempt costs about 100 ns per processed long rank (two random cache lines, however short the match) and the budget was a share of the whole list, so it processed up to a third of 2.4 million ranks (0.11 s) before giving up. *Attempt 3* (cumulative allowance, text order at once from 65,536 long ranks, `AggressiveOptimization` on the two text-order methods: -3 ms of JIT, `Materialize` through pinned `Buf` accessors) is the result below. **Rows not changed on purpose:** the three-pass kernel for heavy texts. `rep_10m` (86 % long ranks, mean LCP 112): the text-order resolution with the cap and the early exit switched off took 0.37 s in-process (scan 0.10, resolution 0.24, output 0.03) against 0.21 s for the three passes, `random-binary` 0.46-0.52 s against 0.28 s; the prefetch distances and hints of the three passes (8-64, `t0`/`t1`/`t2`/`nta`, a prefetch in the Phi scatter, in-process sweep on `rep_10m`) are all within the +-5 % drift of one session (0.200-0.232 s for the three passes). The 3 % target for `rep_10m` is **not met**: paired 0.9876, 1.0097, 1.0098, 0.9797 [0.956, 1.015] in four sessions (A/A bands +-4 to +-10 %), i.e. unchanged. **Tests** (default suite 273 -> 287, raw and checked; the failing explicit test `Direct_Succeeds_OnRandomDnaWithOneEmbeddedRepeatOfOnePercent` is now a default test with its assertion unchanged, plus a 1 / 5 / 20 % equality test on n = 10^6): `MoreEscapesThanTheU8Rule_GiveUp...` and `TheStepBudget_GivesUp...` asserted the removed give-ups and became `TheKindRule_IsTheReferenceRule_OnBothSidesOfTheU8Limit` (same texts, success and exact kind on both sides of 16 * escapes <= n), `TheEscapeThresholds_At255_And65535_AreExact` (L - 254 / L - 65,534 escapes for 253..257 and 65,533..65,537), `ALongRepeat_ExhaustsThePerRankAllowance...` and `ThePerRankAllowance_IsCumulativeAndExactToTheStep` (a replay of the per-rank loop on the reference data predicts outcome and step count for 363 texts, and a text tuned to end exactly on the allowance), `ManyLongRanks_GoStraightToTextOrder...` (n = 3 M over three letters), `ThePerRankResolution_GivesUpAtTheEscapeCapacity_AndNotBefore`, `RepeatArrangements_AreResolvedExactly_WhenTheyFitTheCap` (repeat lengths around the vector widths, at the text start and end, tandem, substituted copies, three copies, byte and 16-bit; every text also through the forced text-order path), `CorruptSuffixArrays_WithLongRanks_StayInsideTheBuffers` (400 corrupt arrays, both resolutions, guarded arena), `ARepeatedTextPosition_KeepsTheMinimum_AndIsCountedOnce`; `AssertAttempt` checks the canaries, the arena block count and the forced text-order result; a sixth registry engine `LcpKernel.DirectTextOrder` (the per-rank loop switched off through `DirectReport.ForceTextOrder`) runs through every corpus, so Vectors 474 -> 632 and Exhaustive 24 -> 30, Fuzz 18 -> 24. Final code: default 287, `Vectors` 632, `Fuzz` 24, `Exhaustive` 30 in the raw and in the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`), older suites 494 / 672 / 137, build warning-free; 57 corpus files (the 1 / 10 Mbp families of the review, `dna_20m`, `prot_1m`) and, on the final binary, `dna_50m` and five more rows equal the libsais SA and LCP hashes (every child of every A/B session with a libsais vector was verified as well); `jit_check` clean (`ScanDirect`, `ResolveLong` Tier1-OSR; `ResolveInTextOrder`, `Materialize` FullOpts for `U8`, `U16`, `U32` results: 0 range-check helpers, 0 accessor calls; the checker's accessor pattern now counts only calls INTO `Buf<T>`, a call that takes or returns a `Buf` such as an arena allocation is not an accessor call). **Fresh mutant set** (`analysis/mutation/lcp_direct_1o3c_mutants.json`, 41 mutants F01-F41 of `LcpKernel.Direct.cs`, filter `Kernels|Review`, Release then checked build for the survivors): **28 killed in Release** (F10/F12/F13 take the test host down), **3 more by the checked build** (F22-F24: min instead of max of the positions, scalar tail one symbol too far, vector step with one symbol too few: the read-past-the-end class), **10 survive and are equivalent or performance-only:** F06 (the escape lists of the failed attempt stay allocated: peak RSS only), F17 (carry across a gap in the positions: at the end of a run the carried length is w, so max(w - 1, w) = w; also survives `Fuzz` + `Vectors`), F18 (carry minus 2: one more comparison per position, same values; survives `Fuzz` + `Vectors`), F21 (start length not clamped: valid arrays never exceed the limit and a corrupt one reads nothing because both loops test `l < limit`; survives `Fuzz` + `Vectors`), F28/F29 (the second range check of the gathered position and predecessor: the scan has checked the same entries, contract of `Build(PreparedText, sa)`: the array is not modified during the call), F37/F38 (look-ahead prefetch one record / rank too far: in-bounds read of an allocated slot, used as a prefetch address only), F40 (threshold 65,537: identical output), F41 (no `AggressiveOptimization`: JIT time only). Two survivors of the first run needed tests: F05 (escape capacity: killed by the direct `ResolveLong` test) and F14 (stale value of a displaced record: the exact value 16 is now asserted); F01, F15, F25 and F35 failed to compile in their first form (analyzer errors) and were rerun and killed. **Lessons:** (1) in a cold process the JIT is part of the kernel: 3.7 of the 6.8 ms of the 1 Mbp LCP step are compilation, and about 1,000 bytes of IL more on the common path (a second resolver, a sample, a 64-bit buffer instantiation) cost 4 ms = 6 % of the 1 Mbp build; the rare path must live in methods the common path never calls (`JitInfo.GetCompilationTime`, harness rows `build_lcp_jit_s`, `build_lcp_jit_methods`); (2) the cost of resolving a long rank on its own is two random cache lines (about 100 ns), not its steps, so a give-up rule must be cumulative and checked early; (3) Kasai restricted to a subset of the positions is exact because every run of subset positions ends where the carried length is back at the threshold.

### S1b 2026-11-09 (work packages 1b.0-1b.2; owner "yes" to S1b given the same day; one implementer context)

- 2026-11-09 1b.0 [x] `29047acc`. **Index contract types, engine conformance suite, oracle engine.** Additive files in `SuffixTree.Core/Indexing` (namespace `SuffixTree.Indexing`, positions `long`): `IndexOperation` (the 14 values of ADR-001 section 6), `IndexSupport` (a plain enum: analyzer CA1028 forbids the `: byte` of the ADR sketch; the values 0 / 1 / 2 are kept), `IndexLocus`, `IIndexText`, `ISuffixIndex`, `ISuffixTreeNavigation`, `ISuffixArrayAccess` (with `Lca(rankA, rankB)`, the member that review MIN-6 found missing); documented, warning-free. Rules fixed beyond the ADR text: `TryExtendRight` with a symbol outside 0..65535 (the terminator -1 included) returns false and leaves the result default; `TryExtendRight(pattern)` with an empty pattern returns the locus; `Support` / `SupportNote` of an undefined operation throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; `Locate` with a negative skip throws; `IIndexText.SymbolAt` is -1 from position n on. Tests (`Esa.Tests/Contract`, 23): `IndexConformanceTests`, a `[TestFixtureSource]` fixture over `IndexEngineFactories.All` (a new engine joins by one list entry): metadata, `SymbolAt` / `CopyTo` bounds, root count, ExtendRight against brute force for every substring up to length 8 of 15 texts and absent patterns (pattern overload equal to symbol-by-symbol, from an inner locus, empty pattern, failed extension leaves default, out-of-range symbols, empty text), `Locate` paging with page sizes 1 / 2 / 3 / 1000 covering every position once, skip past the end, partial pages, Support / SupportNote rules (`Degraded` needs a note, the four core operations are never `Unsupported`), ExtendLeft answered or `NotSupportedException`, locus equality, 4 concurrent readers, random and adversarial texts; `IndexContractTypesTests`. Oracle engine: test-only `UkkonenIndexAdapter` over the public `ISuffixTree` API (locus = leftmost occurrence + length; Count, ExtendRight, LazyLocate, TextAccess Native, the rest `Unsupported`). Red first: the test files did not compile before the adapter existed. Esa default 287 -> 310; 494 / 672 / 137 unchanged.
- 2026-11-09 1b.1 [x] `8ece833a`. **Child table, lcp-interval scan, `Traverse`, statistics; H-OCC and P3.** New internal types in `SuffixTree.Esa`: `LcpIntervalScan` (one pass over an `ILcpStream`, a stack of open intervals, sink events `LIndex(previous, k)` and `Close(ell, lb, rb, first, lastChild)`; it reads no SA and no text, so the streaming builder of ADR-004 PH5 can feed it from a sequential LCP file and spill the stack; the in-memory stack is four int arrays, peak height 1 + max LCP), `ChildLayouts` (`Reference3`, `Packed`, `Tagged`), `ChildTable` (build + `EsaStatistics`: NodeCount, InternalCount, LeafCount, MaxDepth, deepest internal node, stack peak; n = 0 gives `(1, 0, 0)` as Ukkonen), `ChildWriteTrace`, `LeftmostOccurrence`, `EsaIndex` (prepare + SA-IS pointer kernel + direct LCP kernel + child table; `FromArrays` / `WithLayout` reuse SA and LCP) and an iterative `Traverse` (a^n 50,000 does not recurse). Child-table scheme (own derivation, checked against the definition on all strings): the first l-index of an interval sits at its right boundary `rb` when the interval is not the last child of its parent (`LCP[rb+1] >= LCP[lb]`) and at its left boundary `lb` when it is (and for the root); the next-l-index link sits at the l-index; a link to `k+1` is implied by `LCP[k+1] = LCP[k]` in `Packed` (explicit in the other two); the three fields never meet in one slot (a next link needs `LCP[k] <= LCP[k+1]`, an up field `LCP[k] > LCP[k+1]`, a last child has no next link). **H-OCC: CONFIRMED on the real engine, no fallback needed.** The ESA `Traverse` stream (`VisitNode(start, end, leafCount, childCount, depth)`, `EnterBranch` keys, `ExitBranch`) is identical to the Ukkonen tree\'s on every string of the exhaustive sets (all binary strings <= 14, ternary <= 9, 4-ary <= 7: 84,136 strings, two layouts each), to the persistent v6 tree\'s on the strings up to 11 / 7 / 5 (8,740 strings) and to the L0 naive model on about 780 random and adversarial texts up to 2000 symbols (three layouts each); `SuffixTreeSerializer.CalculateLogicalHash` is equal across ESA (through a test-only `ISuffixTree` shim), Ukkonen and v6 on 8 fixed strings, all binary strings up to 7, ternary up to 4 and about 65 adversarial texts up to 200 symbols; NodeCount, LeafCount and MaxDepth equal Ukkonen\'s; the mutant that uses `SA[lb]` instead of the leftmost occurrence (T01, the ADR-001 fallback) is killed by 8 tests, so these oracles do detect a failure of H-OCC. Tests `Esa.Tests/Tree` 43 at the commit (47 at the end: scan input validation, stack growth, the LRS tie rule and the field encoding were added later; red first: the files did not compile before the implementation): scan events and first / next l-index of every interval against `BruteLcpTree` (the tree from the definition by recursion on the LCP array, no child table), `LeftmostOccurrence` against `min SA[lb..rb]`, write-trace invariants (no slot written twice in the single-array layouts), statistics, n = 0 (single event `(0,0,0,0,0)`), Traverse streams. Explicit `Exhaustive`: 4 more tests.
- 2026-11-09 1b.1 / 1b.2 **P3 outcome (child-table packing against the 3-array reference).** The single-array child table stays; the RMQ-only replacement of ADR-001 section 5 is not needed for the Core profile. MAJ-4 rule: a single array must be <= 4.1 B/char **and** within 20 % of the reference on count. Result: 4.0000004 B/char (Packed and Tagged) against 12.0 (Reference3), and both are FASTER than the reference, not slower. Count / locate throughput of the 1b.2 descent, median of 3 fresh processes per cell (interleaved; raw `analysis/s1b/p3_layouts_repeats.tsv`), 20-mer count of present patterns (absent), `FindAllOccurrences` of 1000 8-mers, scan time:

| text | layout | count present q/s [min-max] | count absent q/s | locate8 FindAll patterns/s | child scan (s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dna_10m | Reference3 | 460k [445k-472k] | 480k | 516k | 0.241 |
| dna_10m | Packed | 584k [584k-622k] | 615k | 845k | 0.204 |
| dna_10m | Tagged | 653k [643k-664k] | 659k | 960k | 0.198 |
| rep_10m | Reference3 | 395k [381k-407k] | 551k | 536k | 0.216 |
| rep_10m | Packed | 547k [543k-600k] | 848k | 859k | 0.213 |
| rep_10m | Tagged | 592k [543k-612k] | 780k | 919k | 0.224 |
| prot_1m | Reference3 | 989k [988k-1056k] | 1016k | 1223k | 0.025 |
| prot_1m | Packed | 1193k [1089k-1207k] | 1208k | 1297k | 0.023 |
| prot_1m | Tagged | 1410k [1280k-1500k] | 1499k | 1639k | 0.023 |

`Tagged` (two tag bits, no LCP comparison on a read) is 8-18 % faster than `Packed` (field kind told apart by LCP comparisons, which cost an LCP read per step and an escape lookup on repeat-rich text) but holds ranks in 30 bits (n <= 2^30 - 1, which is `SuffixTreeLimits.MaxTextLength` of the Ukkonen and v6 trees). Decision: `EsaIndex.DefaultLayoutFor(n)` = `Tagged` up to 2^30 - 1 symbols, `Packed` above (the only 4-byte layout that reaches the 3 x 10^9 positions of ADR-004 in a uint entry); `Reference3` remains as the test oracle and as the baseline. The container codec for `CHILD` is not decided here (1b.7, ADR-003 row updated with a pointer). **Write pattern (ADR-004 section 3.2), measured, not proven.** `ChildWriteTrace` replays the scan events as the writes of each layout; a write is at the cursor (rank cursor - 1 or cursor) or behind it (the left boundary of the interval being closed, or the previous l-index of the open top: both are stack entries). `dna_10m`: Reference3 10,000,000 writes (5,723,643 at the cursor, 4,276,357 behind), Packed 8,555,144 (the implied links are not written), Tagged 10,000,000; page loads of an LRU cache of 16 pages of 4,096 bytes (64 KB): Packed and Tagged 10,026 for a table of 9,766 pages (1.027x), Reference3 30,327 for 3 x 9,766 pages (1.035x); with 1,024 pages 9,771; the largest distance behind the cursor is the whole text (the root, one write). Synthetic 1 Mbp texts, Packed, 16 pages (table 977 pages): a^n one backward run and 977 loads (a single sequential backward sweep at the end), abab 3 runs and 980, Fibonacci 1,002, Thue-Morse 1,034, N runs 996 (Tagged 1,231, Reference3 2,508 for 2,931 pages). So on all tested texts the writes are sequential in one direction or LIFO-local with a 64 KB window; no strict bound is proven (it follows from the stack discipline that every behind write hits a boundary of an open interval, but the number of distinct far pages alive at once is not bounded by an argument here). Scan stack peak: a^n 1,000,000 entries, abab 500,000, N runs 125,002, Fibonacci 32, Thue-Morse 24, `dna_10m` 15 (16 B per entry in RAM: the a^n case needs the spill of ADR-004). Child scan 0.19-0.22 s at 10 Mbp, next to 0.85 s SA + LCP in the same harness (cold JIT): SA + LCP + child about 1.07 s against the 3.3 s gate of 1b.9 (which also needs the navigation pack and is not measured here). Raw data: `analysis/s1b`.

- 2026-11-09 1b.3 [x] `30a09b7d` (code, tests, bench command), `debab845` + `b4b24e1c` (mutation audit). **Native analysis operations of the in-memory ESA** (`EsaIndex.Analysis.cs`, internal, SA + LCP + header statistics only; the child table is not read). Signatures are those of `ISuffixTreeAnalysis` / `ISuffixTree` (a reflection test pins them; the facade of 1b.6 forwards 1:1): `LongestRepeatedSubstring()` (the header statistics `LrsDepth` / `LrsRank`: O(L) copy, no search), `LongestRepeatedSubstringMemory()` (zero-copy slice of the shared text; Ukkonen overrides it too), `FindAllLongestRepeatedSubstrings()` (the deepest lcp-intervals are exactly the maximal runs of the maximum LCP value, so ONE sequential pass over the LCP array plus an SA slice per run; positions sorted, ties ordered by first occurrence), `CountDistinctSubstrings()` (`long`: `n (n + 1) / 2 - sum LCP`, SA is a permutation so no SA read), `CountDistinctSubstringsByLength(maxLength)` (`long[]`: a count of length i is `n - i + 1` minus the number of ranks with LCP >= i; a histogram of LCP values clamped at `min(maxLength, n)` and its suffix sums, built in place in the result array), `GetAllSuffixes()` / `EnumerateSuffixes()` (SA walk; the second is a lazy iterator). `FindMaximalRepeatedPairs` and the k-common operations got NO new code: through the test view `EsaNativeTreeShim` (an `ISuffixTree` whose `Traverse` is the ESA's; the 1b.6 facade will be the same) the interface default and `SuffixTreeAlgorithms.FindLongestCommonSubstrings` / `LongestCommonSubstringLengthsBySupport` (factory = ESA view) give lists identical to the definition and to Ukkonen. **Semantics read from Ukkonen and its tests and matched:** the terminator never counts (distinct substrings are non-empty substrings of the text, suffix lists exclude the empty suffix, lengths are 1..n); `n = 0`: `LRS = ""`, `FindAll = []`, distinct total 0, `ByLength(m) = [1]` for every `m >= 1`, no suffixes; `maxLength < 1` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (param `maxLength`) before the text is looked at, `maxLength` above n is clamped (`n + 1` elements), element 0 is 1; counts are `long`, positions `int` (the `ISuffixTree` surface; the long contract of ADR-001 section 6 has no analysis operation, so no `long` variants exist); suffix order is ordinal and a prefix sorts before its extensions (equal to the Ukkonen order, tested on all strings). **LRS tie rule (contract: implementation-specific, `ISuffixTreeAnalysis.cs:13`):** the ESA returns the lexicographically smallest tied substring (the deepest interval that closes first); Ukkonen returns "the first deepest in child-storage order"; compared by property (maximal length, member of the tie list, equal strings when the tie is unique, ESA rule pinned). The LRS string is not cached (Ukkonen caches it); the Memory variant is the zero-allocation call.

Process peak RSS (VmHWM) 1.61 GB on `dna_10m` with all checks and the generic operations (the generic collectors and `Traverse` cost 4 B/char transient plus per-node frames), 0.90 GB on `rep_10m` without them. 1 Mbp synthetic rows (warm): `FindAll` 1.6-2.7 ms, `CountDistinctSubstrings` 1.3-2.1 ms, `ByLength(n)` 5.9-15 ms for a^n, abab, Fibonacci, Thue-Morse, N-runs. **Quadratic places, all bounded and inherent to the return type:** `GetAllSuffixes` returns n strings of average length n / 2 (Theta(n^2) characters; use `EnumerateSuffixes`, whose cost is the copy of the strings and which holds O(1) beyond them); `FindAllLongestRepeatedSubstrings` outputs k ties of length L with k <= (n - L + 1) / 2, so at most about n^2 / 8 characters (typical: 1 tie), while its positions total at most n (the intervals are disjoint) and nothing else is allocated; `ByLength` allocates exactly the `long[]` result (8 B per length, proportional to `min(maxLength, n)`, not n^2). Outcome in one paragraph:** by the pre-registered rule of ADR-001 section 5 the link kernel is **(c), the range-minimum / previous-smaller / next-smaller family on a block-minima pyramid over the LCP array**: the outward scan (a) is within 1.2x of (c) on all six inputs (0.43-0.81x, it is faster) but not bounded on Fibonacci (exact worst case over all nodes 236,064 LCP reads at 1 Mbp, growing linearly: 29,504 / 59,013 / 118,027 / 236,064 at 125k / 250k / 500k / 1M) nor on Thue-Morse; the child-table descent (b) is 1.4-3.1x slower than (c) on every real input and unbounded on `a^n` (3 n steps, 2.0 ms per link at 1 Mbp). (c) costs 410 ns (ISA) / 415 ns (Psi) median per link on `dna_10m`, below the 600 ns trigger, so O1 (stored link table) is not evaluated. The value-parameterised form (c2) (`L = PrevLess(Psi(l), depth-1)`, `R+1 = NextLess(Psi(r)+1, depth-1)`, no range-minimum query) is faster than the literal form on every input (0.45-0.79x of its median on the six inputs, 0.84-0.95x at 50 Mbp) and bounded by 87 steps (F = 16) on every input and size; 1b.4 should implement (c2). ISA against Psi: no consistent difference per link (paired ratio Psi / ISA 0.86-1.11 on the six inputs), ISA 6-24 % faster end to end, so the navigation pack keeps ISA (4.0 B/char) and needs no Psi. P2 on `dna_10m`: the throwaway driver with the ADR kernel (c), Psi, two loci (the cost of the generic algorithm) takes 0.130 s cold and 0.097 s steady (proposal <= 0.20 s met, stretch <= 0.10 s met warm), with (c2) 0.103 / 0.073 s (ISA: 0.090 / 0.066 s); the in-memory Ukkonen tree in the same process 0.071 / 0.048 s, persistent v6 0.163 / 0.143 s (`BENCHMARK.md`); on `rep_10m` the ESA is 2.4x faster than Ukkonen (0.211 / 0.160 s against 0.494 / 0.386 s). The MEM set of the driver equals that of Ukkonen's `FindMaximalExactMatches` in 30 of 30 comparisons. No change in `src/`, `SuffixTree.Core`, Ukkonen, v6, Mcp, CI, wiki, ADR text.
- **What was built** (all in `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench`; `Links/` is compiled into the bench app and, by one `Compile Include` in `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests.csproj`, into the test assembly, which holds the oracle tests in `Links/`): `LinkTables` (ISA; Psi[i] = ISA[SA[i]+1], Psi[0] = -1); `LcpRangeHelper` (pyramid of block minima over the compact LCP, fanout F a power of two, level k holds the minimum of F^k entries; `Min`, `ArgMin` (leftmost minimum), `PrevLess` / `NextLess` (largest / smallest position at or before / after `pos` with LCP below a bound), `PreviousSmaller` / `NextSmaller` (strict PSV / NSV of a position), generic step-counting variants; LCP entries are read through the `CompactLcp` indexer, nothing is copied); `LinkKernels` (four interval searches for `SL(v) = LCA(Psi(l), Psi(r))`, each with ISA or Psi endpoints and a step counter / step budget: (a) `Scan`, (b) `Descent`, (c) `RmqPsvNsv`, (c2) `PsvNsv`; result = target rank interval, parent depth and last-child flag, i.e. what a child-table walk needs; depth 1 returns the root); `LinkNodeSet` (all internal nodes with string depth and node depth by one stack scan, seeded sample, k largest keys); `MemDriver` (throwaway matching-statistics driver: node handles `{Lb, Rb, Depth, Pos, Last}`, extension through the child table exactly as `EsaIndex.Search` descends, suffix link of the last node by the chosen kernel, rescan by skip/count, MEMs of one query start by an outward LCP scan from the rank interval of the longest match while the running minimum stays >= minLength, left-maximality check; `loci = 2` also follows the second locus of the generic algorithm (its links and rescans are paid, its result is not used) so that the link count equals what `FindMaximalExactMatches` would do). Bench commands `links` (P1) and `mem` (P2), README section, `analysis/s1b/{run_p1.sh, run_p2.sh, aggregate.py, RESULTS-1b4a.md}` and the raw rows `p1_links_raw.tsv`, `p1_fanout_raw.tsv`, `p2_mem_raw.tsv`.
- **Tests (red first, with the corrections made on the way).** Test files and stubs (every member `NotImplementedException`) were committed first (`216a2172`): **33 of 33 new default tests red** (`LcpRangeHelperTests` 9, `LinkTablesTests` 3, `LinkNodeSetTests` 5, `LinkKernelTests` 8, `MemDriverTests` 8). Implementation order and what the first runs showed: tables + helper -> 12 tests green at once (helper 9 + tables 3; the helper had been written blind, no correction); node set + kernels -> the first run had 6 of 13 red: five from my direction error in the descent (the walk goes down through values below `depth-1` until it meets it; the check had been inverted), one from a wrong premise in a test (below); driver -> 8 of 8 green on the first run. **Wrong premise found and corrected:** the budget test first assumed that the outward scan is expensive on the deepest node of `a^n` (the ADR sketch says "quadratic on `a^n`-like texts"); it is not: `[Psi(l), Psi(r)]` is already almost the whole target there (1-2 steps). A temporary exploration over ten families (not committed) showed where it is long (Fibonacci 4,717 of 20,000, Thue-Morse, `a^(p-1) b`, `a^k b` with k = 100: 19,407 of 20,000) and the test was rewritten on a Fibonacci word (descent: `a^n`). **Oracles (definition first):** for every internal node (lcp-interval of value l >= 1, taken from `BruteLcpTree` of the naive LCP) the link target is the rank interval of `Y = text[SA[lb]+1 .. +l-1)` (the root for l = 1), found twice and independently, by a scan of the naive suffix array and by `EsaIndex.TryMatch(Y)`, and checked to be an lcp-interval of value l-1; all four kernels x both sources x fanouts 2 and 16 must return exactly that interval with the parent depth and last-child flag: all strings over sigma 2 up to 10, sigma 3 up to 7, sigma 4 up to 5 (default), the explicit `Exhaustive` fixture runs sigma 2 up to 14, sigma 3 up to 9, sigma 4 up to 7 (84k strings); every adversarial family at ten sizes 1..400; 150 random strings (sigma 1, 2, 3, 4, 20, 256); nine texts of 3,000-30,000 symbols with the scan as oracle of the others on 400 sampled + 60 worst nodes and 60 of them against the pattern search. The helper is checked against brute force (range minimum with leftmost position, previous / next smaller strict and by bound) on all ternary arrays up to length 8, all binary arrays up to 11 (explicit: 12-14, ternary 9), random arrays over seven value ranges, all-equal, strictly increasing / decreasing, saw-tooth, single dip / bump, zig-zag, values around the escape boundaries 255 / 65,535 stored as U8, U16 and U32, 5,000- and 40,000-entry arrays, for fanouts 2, 4, 16, 64. The driver is checked against `FindMaximalExactMatchesByDefinition` and the Ukkonen tree: all binary texts up to length 6 x all queries up to length 6 x minLength 1-4, all ternary pairs up to 4 with all 16 (kernel, source, loci) combinations, mutated windows of the adversarial families, foreign symbols, empty and over-long queries, `a^n`. **Counts:** Esa default **429 -> 462** (normal build) and **462 / 462 in the checked build** (`-p:EsaBounds=true`); explicit `Exhaustive` **42 -> 47, 47 / 47 in 8 m 27 s** (the 5 new ones included); `Vectors` and `Fuzz` not run (nothing they cover was touched); older suites unchanged and green: Ukkonen 494, persistent 672, Mcp.Core 137. **Mutation sanity of the prototype code** (not the formal audit of a product kernel): 22 hand-written mutants (`analysis/s1b/mutation_p1_runner.py`, `mutation_p1_result.txt`): 20 killed (one of them, H5, by a hang), 2 survivors K2 and K5 (the scan / PSV-NSV search starting at `Psi(r)` instead of `Psi(r)+1`) are equivalent: every endpoint inside the target has `LCP >= depth-1`, so only the step count differs by one. Large-scale agreement inside the `links` command (66 processes, 42 of them with the pattern-search check): all kernels give the same interval for every sampled and worst-node link where both finished (1,470 cross-check rows, all 0 differences), and 300 sampled links per process equal `TryMatch` of the label without its first symbol (42 x 300, 0 mismatches).

  On `dna_50m` (31.2 M internal nodes; about 900 MB of structures, beyond the 480 MiB L3 that the VM reports) the medians are (a) 579 / 607 ns, (b) 2,188 / 1,985, (c) 681 / 667, (c2) 574 / 633 (ISA / Psi), steps unchanged, ISA build 0.63 s, Psi 0.72 s, pyramid 0.14 s; the ranking is the same and ISA against Psi is still a tie. **Caveat:** at 10 Mbp every structure (about 180 MB) fits the L3 of this VM, so a link costs L3 latencies (about 4-8 dependent reads at 50-70 ns); on a machine with a small L3 the same links cost DRAM latencies and the ratios between kernels with the same number of reads stay, the absolute ns grow.
- **Decision rule of ADR-001 section 5, applied mechanically** ("choose (c) unless (a) or (b) is within 1.2x of (c) on ALL inputs AND bounded on `a^n`/Fibonacci; if (c) is > 600 ns median on `dna_10m`, evaluate O1"). Ratios of the median ns per link (net) to (c), for both endpoint sources:

| input | source | (a)/(c) | (b)/(c) | (c2)/(c) | (c) median ns |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dna_10m | isa | 0.72 | 3.12 | 0.79 | 410 |
| dna_10m | psi | 0.76 | 2.81 | 0.75 | 415 |
| rep_10m | isa | 0.70 | 2.77 | 0.77 | 580 |
| rep_10m | psi | 0.64 | 2.82 | 0.66 | 550 |
| prot_1m | isa | 0.57 | 1.39 | 0.61 | 268 |
| prot_1m | psi | 0.56 | 1.41 | 0.61 | 274 |
| a^n | isa | 0.45 | 5691.65 | 0.45 | 358 |
| a^n | psi | 0.43 | 5568.25 | 0.44 | 366 |
| fibonacci | isa | 0.73 | 2.63 | 0.66 | 193 |
| fibonacci | psi | 0.81 | 2.61 | 0.75 | 187 |
| thue-morse | isa | 0.64 | 2.30 | 0.69 | 182 |
| thue-morse | psi | 0.71 | 2.38 | 0.73 | 187 |

  Step 1, "within 1.2x on all inputs": (a) passes on all six inputs and both sources (0.43-0.81); (b) fails on all six (1.39-3.12 on the real inputs and Fibonacci / Thue-Morse, 5,568-5,692 on `a^n`). Step 2, "bounded on `a^n` and Fibonacci": (a) is bounded on `a^n` (1-2 steps) but **not on Fibonacci**: its exact worst case over all nodes is 29,504 / 59,013 / 118,027 / 236,064 steps at n = 125k / 250k / 500k / 1M, i.e. 0.236 n, linear (Thue-Morse: n / 12); (b) is bounded on Fibonacci (96-114 steps) but **not on `a^n`**: 374,994 / 749,994 / 1,499,994 / 2,999,994 steps, i.e. 3 n (node depth n - 1, one child-table walk per level). Step 3: neither (a) nor (b) satisfies both conditions, therefore **(c)**. Step 4: the median of (c) on `dna_10m` is 410 ns (ISA) / 415 ns (Psi), below 600 ns, so O1 is **not triggered**; nothing about O1 was measured (its size stays the ADR estimate: 4-8 B per internal node, 2.5-5 B/char). On `dna_50m` (c) is 681 / 667 ns, above 600 ns; the rule names `dna_10m`, so this is information for the owner only: it is the memory-latency regime where a stored table (about 1-3 reads) would be competitive, to be revisited with the 1b.9 gate numbers, not a trigger. Inside the (c) family the literal ADR form (`RMQ(a+1..b)`, then PSV and NSV of the minimum's position) is dominated by (c2): same pyramid, the node depth gives `t = depth-1` so the range-minimum query is not needed; (c2) / (c) = 0.45-0.79 in ns per link on the six inputs (0.84-0.95 at 50 Mbp) and 0.69-0.74 end to end; the RMQ of the pyramid stays useful for the public `Lcp(i, j)` / `Lca(i, j)` of PLAN.md:74-76.
- **P2 table** (MEM of the benchmark query: 100,000 bp window with 1 % substitutions, minLength 20, `queries/<name>.memq100k.txt`; 10 calls per fresh process, the first call is cold (JIT of the driver and the kernels included), the steady value is the median of the last five; the Ukkonen rows are the in-memory tree of the same process, built after the ESA measurements, 15 processes; ESA structures built by `EsaIndex.Build` (about 1.0 s) + ISA / Psi / pyramid (0.19 s)):

  Counters (100,000 query starts): two loci 200,000 links followed, 200,000 extension steps, `dna_10m` 21,707 child lookups to extend from a node and 342,090 rescan lookups (3.4 per start), `rep_10m` 70,165 and 186,061; one locus half of the links (100,000), 10,852 / 171,047 and 21,537 / 114,312. The link kernel takes 32-54 % of the time in the (a) / (c) / (c2) variants (timer cost of 2 x 22 ns per link included) and 67-74 % with the descent. 817 and 160,979 matches are the counts of `BENCHMARK.md`; **the MEM set (position in text, position in query, length) of the driver equals the Ukkonen tree's in all 30 comparisons** (3 headline variants x 5 processes x 2 inputs) and the checksum of the set is the same in all 60 processes of an input. **Against the proposal:** <= 0.20 s on `dna_10m` is met by every variant except the descent on the cold call (0.208 s; steady 0.160 s); the stretch <= 0.10 s is met warm by every (a) / (c) / (c2) variant (0.053-0.097 s) and cold by (c2) with ISA (0.090 s) and (c2) with one locus (0.086 s), not cold by the literal (c) (0.119-0.130 s). With the ADR kernel and the generic algorithm's two loci the ESA is 1.8x (cold) / 2.0x (steady) slower than the in-memory Ukkonen tree on `dna_10m`; with (c2) 1.4x / 1.5x; with (c2) and one locus (ESA-native matching statistics) 1.2x / 1.1x; and 0.8x / 0.7x (with (c)) or 0.6x / 0.5x (with (c2)) of the persistent v6 time published in `BENCHMARK.md` (0.163 / 0.143 s; a different session). On the repetitive text the ESA is 2.4x faster than Ukkonen (0.211 against 0.494 s cold, 0.160 against 0.386 s steady; (c2): 0.177 / 0.113 s). Paired ratios of the steady median per repetition: (c2) / (c) 0.74 and 0.69; ISA / Psi 0.85 [0.80..1.06] and 0.94 with (c), 0.88 and 0.76 with (c2); one locus / two loci with (c2) 0.71 and 0.80.
- **Deviations from the ADR sketch and choices made** (ADR text not edited; the orchestrator decides): (1) **(b) is not a parent climb.** The child table of 1b.1 stores, for an interval, its first l-index at `rb` or `lb`, but no parent link, and neither the parent's bounds nor a way to reach them follows from a child's bounds without an outward search, so a climb from leaf `a` is not O(1) per step on this layout; (b) is the child-table descent along the rank path of `Psi(l)` from the root until the node of value `depth-1` (the walk of `EsaIndex.Search` without text comparisons; cost = children walked on the path to the target, about 3 table reads per level). A true climb needs the pyramid ((c)). (2) **(c2) added** as a fourth kernel (see above). (3) **R-SL point 2 of the ADR is not confirmed:** "use (a) in the first working version (WP 1b.4) because it is the simplest and is the oracle for (c)" would put an unbounded kernel (Fibonacci, Thue-Morse, periodic and `a^k b` texts) into the product; (c2) is as simple (two helper calls) and bounded, (a) stays what it is here, the oracle. The ADR line is for the orchestrator to amend; I did not touch the ADR. (4) The pyramid is not a sparse table over blocks: RMQ costs O(F x levels) sequential reads, 0.267 B/char at F = 16 (0.57 / 0.13 / 0.06 at F = 8 / 32 / 64; the ADR estimate "0.3-0.5 B/char, UNVERIFIED" is verified in order of magnitude); measured speed does not depend on F in 8-64, so 1b.4 can take F = 32 (0.13 B/char, (c2) steps max 123 on `rep_10m`). (5) The kernels read the LCP through `CompactLcp`'s indexer (a range check and a kind switch per read), not through pointers: the scan loops pay more for it than the pyramid does, all numbers are therefore a safe-code upper bound; the pointer kernels of 1b.4 will be somewhat faster, probably most for (a), which does not change the boundedness result. (6) The 1 locus driver is not the generic algorithm: `FindMaximalExactMatches` walks `GetChildren` / `CollectLeaves` from the minLength locus down to the longest-match locus; the driver replaces that by the outward LCP scan (same output, same left-maximality rule), so P2 measures the cost of matching statistics + links + rescans + emission, and the walk of the generic algorithm is the one cost it does not contain (on random DNA at minLength 20 the minLength locus is already inside a leaf edge, so it is nearly empty there; on `rep_10m` it is not, so the `rep_10m` figures are the less certain ones). (7) Process headers say `71c5dabaf3-dirty`: the data was measured with `71c5daba` plus the two-line fix of `cd6f63d2` (links not computed are skipped by the cross-check; a first set of runs showed exit code 3 only in the processes where the budget-limited descent was the comparison reference, the timing rows are not affected; that set was discarded and everything re-run).
- **Not verified / limits:** machines with a small L3 (only `dna_50m` leaves the L3 here); more than one thread (the structures are read-only, nothing concurrent was run); alphabets above 256 symbols in the driver (`NotSupportedException`, the kernels are alphabet-free); texts above 2^30 symbols (tagged child table); the end-to-end time of the real navigator through `SuffixTreeAlgorithms.FindMaximalExactMatches` (1b.4); MUM and anchors (proportional to the number of links, not measured); the cost of the `Pos = SA[Lb]` read that completes the target handle is inside the driver numbers (one SA read per link) but not in the P1 ns.
- **Open points handed to 1b.4:** (a) implement `GetSuffixLink` as (c2) with ISA and an `LcpRangeHelper` of F = 32; the node handle already carries the depth, the target's parent depth and last-child flag come out of the same two LCP reads, `Pos = SA[Lb]` is one more read; (b) keep (a) in the tests as an oracle only, with the definition oracle of `Links/LinkKernelTests` (target = interval of the label minus its first symbol) as the first-level check; (c) the generic algorithm pays two links and two rescans per query start (the minLength locus and the longest-match locus); a dedicated ESA matching-statistics path (one locus, links from (c2), emission by the outward LCP scan) saves 25-30 % on `dna_10m` (0.053 against 0.073 s steady) and is the S3 `ms[]` candidate of ADR-001 O4; (d) the rescan after each link costs 3.4 child lookups per start on `dna_10m` (two dependent reads, `SA[a]` then `text[...]`, per candidate child): the optional `NEXTSYM` byte array would cut them to one read; (e) the pyramid is 6 levels at F = 16 over 10 M entries; its build (0.027 s) and ISA (0.071 s) are 10 % of the SA + LCP + child build (1.0 s), Psi (0.077 s more) is not needed; (f) texts with many LCP escapes select U32 (`a^n`, Fibonacci, Thue-Morse: 4 B/char) so the scans read 4-byte entries there; the pointer kernel must keep the escape-aware reader for U8 / U16; (g) `Checked` mode of 1b.7 must bound the descent-like walks (open point (a) of the 1b.2 hand-over) but kernel (c2) has no table walk at all, which is one more argument for it.

### S1b 2026-11-09 work package 1b.4 (navigator, navigation pack, suffix links and the matching operations; one implementer context)

- 2026-11-09 1b.4 [x] `d7e81df3` (code), `9bbd9347` (tests first, red), `d4c3a578` (tuning, bench command), `4300daa4` (measurement data), `fd58c181` (mutation audit, gap tests, exhaustive fixture of the kernel). **Outcome in one paragraph:** the generic algorithms of `SuffixTree.Core` (`FindAllLcs`, `FindAllDistinctLongestCommonSubstrings`, `FindExactMatchAnchors`, `FindMaximalExactMatches`, `FindMaximalUniqueMatches`) run UNCHANGED on `EsaNavigator : ISuffixTreeNavigator<EsaNode>` over the in-memory `EsaIndex`, and their answers equal the definition-level oracles and the Ukkonen tree on every exhaustive small pair, adversarial family, random pair, fuzz case and MUMmer 3.23 vector tried (MEM / MUM lists and the LCS lists exactly; anchors as (query position, length), their text position and the text position of `LongestCommonSubstringInfo` as "a valid occurrence", the contract leaves them open). The suffix link is kernel (c2) of 1b.4a on a block-minima pyramid of fanout 32 (0.129 B/char) with ISA as the endpoint source (4.000 B/char), in a navigation pack that is built lazily, once, thread-safely, heap-resident; the core operations never touch it (tested), `EsaIndex.SuffixLinkSupport` is `Degraded` until it exists. **MEM of the 100 kbp query (1 % substitutions, minLength 20) through the GENERIC algorithm on `dna_10m`: 0.109 s cold [0.099 .. 0.111], 0.066 s steady (5 fresh processes), the proposal <= 0.20 s is met cold and warm, the stretch <= 0.10 s warm and, narrowly missed, cold (median 0.109 s, best 0.099 s); the in-memory Ukkonen tree in the same process takes 0.068 / 0.037 s (ESA 1.6x / 1.8x slower), on `rep_10m` the ESA takes 0.195 / 0.153 s against 0.535 / 0.387 s (2.7x / 2.5x faster); MUM 0.075 / 0.045 s and anchors 0.076 / 0.044 s on `dna_10m`; the LCS family on two 1 Mbp texts 0.22-0.33 s (2-16 % faster than the Ukkonen tree, about 10 % typically). The first suffix-link user also pays the pack once: 0.098 s at 10 Mbp (0.65 s at 50 Mbp), so a cold first MEM in a fresh process costs 0.109 + 0.098 = 0.207 s.** Production link kernel 428 ns (`dna_10m`) / 364 ns (`rep_10m`) per link on random internal nodes, 0.82x / 0.69x of the prototype with the same work. Mutation audit: 111 fresh mutants built, 100 killed (20 of them by non-termination), 1 more killed in the checked build, 10 equivalent survivors argued, one gap found and closed. No change to `SuffixTree.Core`, the generic algorithms, Ukkonen, v6, Mcp, CI, wiki, the S1a kernels, EmProto, ADR texts, `BENCHMARK.md` (`git diff` of those paths is empty); the only edit outside the new files is the additive read access to the base arrays and the overflow list of `CompactLcp` (five `internal` properties).
- **What was built** (all new files in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa`, about 1,000 lines; tests in `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.Esa.Tests/Navigation` and `Fuzz/MatchingFuzzTests.cs`; bench command `nav`, README section, `analysis/s1b/{run_nav.sh, run_nav_all.sh, aggregate_nav.py, RESULTS-1b4.md, nav_raw.tsv}`): `EsaNode` (`record struct {Lb, Rb, Depth, ParentDepth, Pos, LastChild}`, 24 bytes against the 20 of the ADR sketch: the last-child flag is needed to find the child-table field of an interval); `PinnedArray<T>` (an array whose address is fixed: allocated on the pinned object heap or pinned by a handle that `Dispose` or the finalizer frees; viewed as `Buf<T>`); `LcpPyramid` (pyramid of block minima over the compact LCP, fanout a power of two, default 32: `Min`, `PrevLess`, `NextLess`, strict `PreviousSmaller` / `NextSmaller`, the combined `Borders` of the link kernel, `ValueAt`; the LCP base array is read through a pointer by kind (U8 / U16 / U32); an escaped entry is resolved by binary search in the overflow list only when a comparison needs it, i.e. only for bounds above the escape value, which no node of depth up to 255 does; every loop runs inside one block or level group, so a search reads at most F x (levels + 1) entries and an inconsistent pyramid ends in `InvalidOperationException`); `EsaNavigationPack` (ISA scatter, pyramid, pinned view of the suffix array, `SuffixLink(node)`: internal node = kernel (c2) with `Psi(l)` and `Psi(r)` from ISA, depth-1 node = root, root = root, leaf = the leaf of the next suffix with parent depth and last-child flag from the two LCP values around its rank, the terminator-only leaf = root); `EsaNavigator` (struct; `TryGetChild` and `GetChildren` walk the l-indices of the layout in use (Tagged, Packed or Reference3, three specialisations of one generic body) and compare the symbol at the node's depth in the first suffix of each candidate; `CollectLeaves` is a slice copy of the suffix array without the empty suffix; `GetEdgeSymbol` reads the original string at `Pos + ParentDepth + offset` and returns -1 from the end of the text; every walk checks that the l-indices strictly increase and stay inside the interval, so a corrupt table raises instead of looping); `EsaIndex.Navigation` / `NavigationBuilt` / `NavigationBuildCount` / `SuffixLinkSupport` (double-checked lock, `Volatile`) and `CreateNavigator()`; `EsaIndex.Matching` (`LongestCommonSubstring` (string, span), `LongestCommonSubstringInfo` (string, span), `FindAllLongestCommonSubstrings`, `FindAllDistinctLongestCommonSubstrings`, `FindExactMatchAnchors`, `FindMaximalExactMatches`, `FindMaximalUniqueMatches`; same signatures and argument rules as `ISuffixTreeAnalysis`, arguments are validated BEFORE the pack is built). `[MethodImpl(AggressiveOptimization)]` is set on the hot members (below).
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** Test files and stubs (every member `NotImplementedException`) were committed first (`9bbd9347`): **64 of the 66 new default tests red** (59 `NotImplementedException`, 2 of them as `AggregateException` from threads, 3 `Assert.Throws` tests that got `NotImplementedException` instead of the argument exception), 1 green (`Contract_IsAValueTypeNavigatorOfEsaNode`: `EsaNode` is a logic-free record struct and was written complete in the stub commit), 1 skipped (checked-build-only); **10 of 10 explicit tests red** (Exhaustive 8, Fuzz 2). Implementation order and what the first runs showed: `PinnedArray` 5 of 5 green at once; `LcpPyramid` 11 of 11 green at once (written blind, no correction; the structured-array test was then cut from 9.7 s to 0.13 s by sampling positions and bounds above 96 entries); pack, kernel, navigator and walk tests all green at once (46 of 46; afterwards trimmed for run time: the larger-text walk 12 s -> 2 s by sampling long leaf edges and shortening eager assertion messages, the concurrency test from 8 pool tasks to 8 threads); matching operations 18 of 19 green, the one red was MY test (the layout test compared an index of the whole text with the oracle of a 300-symbol slice), and the larger-text test first took 27 s because minLength 1 on random DNA has 11 million MEMs (now minLength >= 8). **So the tests found no defect of the implementation at the first run; the evidence of their sensitivity is the mutation audit.** **Written after the implementation, NOT red first:** `CorruptTableTests` (2), the pin-release and finalizer tests (2), the null-node, `Borders`-argument and "invalid arguments do not build the pack" tests (3; all found while drafting the mutant list), the exhaustive fixture `SuffixLinkExhaustiveTests` (3), and the run-time trims above. **Oracles:** the walk of the ESA through the navigator is compared record by record (path label, leaf flag, leaf count, child count, branch key) with the public `Traverse` of the Ukkonen tree, node by node it also checks parent depth, edge length and symbols, `TryGetChild` for every present and several absent keys, `CollectLeaves` (the union of the children, brute force for n <= 64) and `FindAnyLeafPosition`, on all strings over sigma 2 up to 10, sigma 3 up to 7, sigma 4 up to 5, all three layouts, adversarial families (sizes 1-257), random strings over sigma 1, 2, 3, 4, 20, 256, the edge corpus (surrogates, NUL, U+FFFF) and six larger texts; a hand-built "banana" tree pins the handle values. The suffix link of every node is compared with (1) the definition (the rank interval of the label without its first symbol, from the naive suffix array AND from the pattern search), (2) the node found by descending that label with `TryGetChild` alone, (3) the prototype kernels (a) and (c2), for pyramid fanouts 2 and 32, on the same exhaustive sets, the adversarial families up to 400, random strings, the edge corpus and nine larger texts (sample + worst nodes); links of leaves and the root against the leaf of the next suffix. The pyramid against brute force and the prototype `LcpRangeHelper`: all binary arrays up to length 10, ternary up to 7, the escape palettes {0, 1, 254, 255, 256, 300} (U8) and {0, 1, 65534, 65535, 65536, 70000} (U16) up to length 4, random arrays, structured arrays, a 40,000-entry array with four levels. MEM / MUM (both modes) / anchors / LCS family: all binary text and query pairs up to length 6 and ternary up to 4 with minLength 1-3, text over {a, b} with queries over {a, b, c}, the MUMmer 3.23 literal outputs of `MaximalMatchTests`, the LCS literature cases, adversarial texts with mutated, reversed and foreign queries, random pairs over six alphabets, six larger texts, concurrent queries on one index. Fuzz: `MatchingFuzzTests` (20,000 cases up to n = 200 against definition and Ukkonen, 2,000 cases up to n = 4,000 against Ukkonen, texts from the 1a.7 families).
