  The answers equal the Ukkonen tree's in 5 / 5 processes for every operation. ESA build 1.0 s (`dna_10m`), pack **0.098 s** at 10 Mbp (`rep_10m` 0.116 s, `dna_50m` 0.647 s; the pyramid alone 0.018 s warm), **4.129 B/char** (ISA 4.000 + pyramid 0.129 at fanout 32, 5 levels at 10 Mbp). The "second text" of the LCS rows is a deterministic copy of `dna_1m` with 1 % substitutions and 0.1 % indels, or an independent random text of the same letters (the benchmark generator has no second 1 Mbp text); both are 1 Mbp. **Link kernel against the prototype (same nodes, paired halves, ns net of the timer floor, the prototype WITH the extra `SA[Lb]` read that completes the production handle):** `dna_10m` 428 [420 .. 460] against 511 [500 .. 558] ns (0.82x), `rep_10m` 364 [355 .. 397] against 500 [485 .. 543] ns (0.69x), 0 differences in 5 x 200,000 links per input; the 292 / 444 ns of 1b.4a were not reproduced in these conditions (the prototype without the extra read takes 437-480 ns here; the paired same-process comparison is the figure to read). Decision on the escape-aware pointer reader: it pays (18-31 % per link at equal work), the kernel stays bound by a handful of dependent cache misses.
- **Where the MEM time goes** (`nav --op split`; three views because fine-grained timers distort a memory-bound pipeline and the members overlap in the processor, so the shares are brackets, not a sum): `dna_10m` (plain call 61 ms, 200,000 links, 368,000 child lookups of which 359,000 follow a link, 274,000 edge symbols, 82,000 leaves collected, 33 KiB allocated per call): by replay the link kernel is about 93 ns per call in place (19 ms, 30 %), the child lookups 87 ns each (32 ms, 52 %; two dependent reads per candidate child), edge symbols and lengths 15 %, `CollectLeaves` 4 %, the sum is 104 % so the generic algorithm and its allocations are below the resolution; in the timed run the link kernel and the child lookups each take about a third of the timed total and the timers the rest. `rep_10m` (plain call 147 ms, 8.4 MB allocated per call for the 160,979 results and the lists of the generic algorithm): the enumeration of sibling subtrees dominates (1.39 million `GetChildren`, 1.45 million child lookups off the rescan path, 1.77 million `CollectLeaves` with 5.5 million positions, 3.3 million edge symbols), the link kernel is about 11 % by replay (16 ms). The generic algorithm's cost is therefore in the navigator members it calls, the remainder is not measurable separately; the S3 candidate (one locus per query start, the O4 of ADR-001) would remove half of the 200,000 links and rescans on `dna_10m`.
- **Experiments that were run and not adopted** (each measured, details in `RESULTS-1b4.md`): lazy `Pos` of the link target (saves the `SA[Lb]` read, 120 ns on a random node, end-to-end unchanged); prefetch in the ISA scatter (0.098 s against 0.100 s); **a link from `Psi(l)` alone** (mutant P10 survived because the second endpoint is mathematically redundant once `t = depth - 1` is known: the maximal run of LCP >= t around any rank of the target is the target; two dependent random reads fewer, but MEM was 5-8 % slower in 5 paired runs, the two ISA reads overlapping in the memory system), so the LCA form of the ADR stays. **Adopted:** `AggressiveOptimization` on the navigator members, the pack and the pyramid cores: cold MEM on `dna_10m` 0.145 s -> 0.098 s (6 processes each), steady unchanged. **A measurement error caught and corrected:** the first complete campaign built the Ukkonen tree before the ESA calls; the 1.9 GB heap of small objects made the ESA 20 % slower on `rep_10m` MEM (0.182 s against 0.153 s, probably the gen-2 collections that the large result lists provoke; not checked by counting collections), the ESA is now measured first; the discarded rows are kept in `nav_raw_discarded_ukkonen_first.tsv`.
- **Mutation audit** (`analysis/mutation/esa_s1b_nav_*_mutants.json`, `results_s1b_nav.tsv` merged with notes, `run_s1b_nav_audit.sh`; three parallel worktrees, tests of `Esa.Tests/Navigation` in the default run, Release first and the survivors again in the checked build; a 110 s watchdog kills a test host that never ends): **121 hand-written mutants of the new production code only** (`LcpPyramid` 37, `EsaNavigationPack` 20, `EsaNavigator` 28 + 4 more on the corrupt-table guards, `EsaIndex.Navigation` 5 + 3 replacements, `EsaIndex.Matching` 8, `PinnedArray` 6 + 6 replacements, `EsaNode` 4: boundary shifts, off-by-one, swapped operands, wrong comparison, dropped guard, wrong table slot, inverted flags); **10 did not compile** (analyzer errors; four of them were replaced by compiling forms, `A05` (no `GC.SuppressFinalize(this)`) could not be built in three forms and is argued equivalent), **111 were built and run: 100 killed in Release (20 of them because the generic algorithm then never ends and the watchdog killed the host), 1 more killed only in the checked build (Y20, a read one past the pinned array), 10 survivors, all argued equivalent beforehand** (M05 info asks for all positions: the first of the ascending list is a valid occurrence; N09 `>=` for `>` after the equality branch; N18 `<= 0` for `< 0` (an l-index is never 0); N24 `AddRange` of an empty span; P07 root test also on `Lb == 0`; **P10 the second link endpoint is `Psi(Rb - 1)`: any rank of the target gives the same run**; Y22 early return of `NextLess` at the end (no block exists to the right of the last rank); Y29 U16 reader resolves from bound 255; Y31 corrupt-input guard of the overflow lookup; Y36 left border rank 0 cannot occur since `LCP[1] = 0 < t`); P10, Y22 and Y36 also survive the exhaustive kernel fixture (`results_s1b_nav_survivors_exhaustive.tsv`). **Two gaps were found and closed:** N28 (the guard that the first l-index of an interval lies inside it) survived because no test fed a corrupt child table, now `CorruptTableTests` (all five guard mutants N28-N32 are killed, N30 by non-termination); A02 (a `PinnedArray` that never frees its handle) because no test looked at the release, now two weak-reference tests.
- **Deviations from the ADR sketch and choices made** (ADR text not edited): (1) the node handle has 24 bytes (six fields); (2) the link of a leaf is the leaf of the next suffix and the link of the root is the root, where the Ukkonen navigator returns the root for a leaf (it has no link); the generic algorithms never ask for either; (3) the pack is built when a navigator is created (`CreateNavigator()`), not at the first `GetSuffixLink`; invalid arguments never create it; `NavigationBuilt` / `SuffixLinkSupport` are index-level indicators for 1b.5; (4) `GetEdgeSymbol` reads the original string (2 B/char for DNA) instead of the dense symbols (1 B/char) because the algorithms compare with the query's characters; (5) the pyramid also offers `Min`, `PreviousSmaller`, `NextSmaller` (tested, no caller yet: they give `Lcp(i, j)` and the parent computation to 1b.5); (6) `CompactLcp` got five read-only `internal` properties; (7) `EsaIndex` stays non-`IDisposable`: the pins are released by the finalizers of `PinnedArray`, `EsaNavigationPack.Dispose` exists for a facade that wants it; (8) the occurrence that anchors and `LongestCommonSubstringInfo` report is the first one the navigator offers; (9) suffix links were NOT compared with the links stored inside the Ukkonen tree (they are reachable only from `SuffixTree.Tests` and the project may not be touched): the definition, the descent and the prototypes replace them, and every generic result that depends on links is compared with Ukkonen's; (10) the minLength guards of `FindExactMatchAnchors` follow Ukkonen (`minLength <= 0` is an empty list, not an exception).
- **Not verified / limits:** texts above 2^30 symbols (the `Packed` layout is exercised on small texts only); machines with a small L3 (the figures are for 480 MiB L3; at 50 Mbp the MEM steady time is 0.082 s, which is the only size beyond the cache that was run); more than one query thread beyond the tests (the structures are read-only); alphabets above 256 symbols only in the unit tests; the kernel's boundedness is structural (loops inside blocks and level groups) and was not re-counted with step counters on Fibonacci / Thue-Morse at 1 Mbp as in P1 (the unit tests run every node of Fibonacci 1,500 and a^500 and the larger texts); the cold figure depends on the JIT of the generic algorithms in `SuffixTree.Core`, which this package may not mark for optimisation; the hang-kills of the audit prove that a wrong navigator makes the generic algorithms loop, nothing in the product guards against a navigator that is wrong.
- **Open points handed to 1b.5 / 1b.6 / 1b.7:** (a) 1b.5: `SuffixLink` capability = `index.SuffixLinkSupport` (`Degraded` with the note "builds ISA and the LCP pyramid on first use, O(n), 4.13 B/char, 0.1 s at 10 Mbp"); `Lcp(i, j)` = `Navigation.Pyramid.Min(i + 1, j)`, `LcpAt` = `Pyramid.ValueAt`, `RankOf` = `Navigation.Isa` (both `Degraded` until the pack exists), `ExtendLeft` = ISA at `p - 1` plus min / max rank over the interval; `TryParent` has no primitive yet: the parent depth is `max(LCP[lb], LCP[rb + 1])` (already in every handle) and the parent's borders are `PrevLess(lb, parentDepth)` / `NextLess(rb + 1, parentDepth)`; (b) 1b.6: the facade creates `CreateNavigator()` per call (one `StringTextSource` allocation and a struct copy), forwards the seven matching methods of `EsaIndex` unchanged, refuses `NodeCount` above `int` itself, and may dispose the pack in its own `Dispose`; the navigator is a value, the pack is shared and immutable, concurrent readers are tested; (c) 1b.7 (`Checked` mode): the navigator walks already refuse non-increasing l-indices, the pyramid raises `InvalidOperationException` on an inconsistent block; a corrupt LCP or SA still makes ISA / the pyramid wrong silently, to be validated at load; (d) not done and left for S3: the one-locus matching-statistics path (half of the links and rescans of MEM), the optional `NEXTSYM` array (one read instead of two per candidate child in the rescans, which are the largest single cost on `dna_10m`); the cold figure would profit from a ReadyToRun / tiering setting of the host, not from this package.

### S1b 2026-11-11 work package 1b.5 (contract implementation `EsaSuffixIndex`; one implementer context)

- 2026-11-11 1b.5 [x] `387c3951` (code), `b65e8f95` (tests first, red), `69594952` (validation / corrupt-table tests, written after the code), `a68d9827` (mutant lists, runner), `cb6b53a5` (audit gap tests, second-round lists). **Outcome in one paragraph:** `EsaSuffixIndex` (internal sealed, `SuffixTree.Esa`, about 500 lines in `EsaSuffixIndex.cs`, `.Tree.cs`, `.Arrays.cs` and `EsaIndexText.cs`) implements `ISuffixIndex`, `ISuffixTreeNavigation` and `ISuffixArrayAccess` over the in-memory `EsaIndex`; every operation on every kind of locus (root, internal node, leaf, terminator leaf, the leaf of the empty suffix, point inside an edge) equals an oracle that was written from the definitions (naive SA / LCP, occurrence sets of strings in `text$`); the engine conformance suite of 1b.0 runs against the ESA with its three child-table layouts (`esa`, `esa-packed`, `esa-reference3`) and passes; the capability matrix equals the table of ADR-001 section 6 (below); the core operations never build the navigation pack; no existing production class was changed.
- **What was built.** A locus is `(Lo, Hi, Length, Aux)`: the rank interval of the suffixes that start with the locus string, the number of matched symbols (a leaf counts its terminator), and the depth of the lowest explicit node at or below, so a locus is an explicit node exactly when `Length == Aux`. `Root = (0, n, 0, 0)`; the terminator child of the root (the leaf of the empty suffix) is `(0, 0, 1, 1)` and does not exist for the empty text (one node, as the Ukkonen tree). `Count` and `Locate` ignore rank 0 (`Count(Root) = n`, the children of a node add up to its count; `Locate` is in suffix-array order and reuses `EsaIndex.Locate`). `TryExtendRight` walks the l-indices of the child table at nodes and compares the text at `SA[Lo] + Length` inside an edge (own generic child walk over the three layouts: the navigator builds the pack in its constructor, the contract's `Child` must not). `TryChild` / `GetChildren` (count pass, then a write pass; nothing is written when the span is too small) / `EdgeSymbol` / `SuffixAt` / `LcpAt` need no pack. The last-child flag and the parent depth of a node are recomputed from the two LCP values at its borders (`parent depth = max(LCP[lb], LCP[rb + 1])`, last child exactly when `LCP[lb] > LCP[rb + 1]`). Pack operations: `TryParent` = parent depth plus `PrevLess` / `NextLess` on the pyramid; `TrySuffixLink` = kernel (c2) of the pack; `TryExtendLeft` = ISA of `p - 1` over the positions in the interval that are preceded by the symbol, min / max rank, `Aux` = leaf depth or pyramid minimum (the empty string is `TryExtendRight` of the root: no pack); `RankOf` = ISA; `Lcp(i, j)` = pyramid minimum over `(min + 1 .. max)` (equal ranks: the length of the suffix, no pack); `Lca` = value `m` of that minimum, borders `PrevLess(lo, m)` / `NextLess(hi + 1, m) - 1`, the root for `m = 0`, the leaf for equal ranks, the root for the empty text. `Support(op)` is state dependent (`EsaIndex.SuffixLinkSupport` is the pack indicator). Loci are validated: a tuple that cannot be a locus of this text is an `ArgumentException` (O(1) shape rules for `Count` / `Locate`, plus the LCP values at both borders and the child-table field for the node operations).
- **Capability matrix (result).** `CapabilityMatrix_EqualsAdr001Section6_OncePackExists` pins all 14 operations: Count, ExtendRight, LazyLocate, TextAccess, Child, StringDepth, SuffixArrayAccess, LcpAccess **Native**; ExtendLeft **Degraded** always ("O(width of the locus interval) ... builds ISA and the LCP pyramid on first use"); Parent, SuffixLink, InverseSuffixArrayAccess, Lcp, Lca **Native once the pack exists** and **Degraded before it** (note "builds ISA and the LCP pyramid on first use, O(n), 4.13 B/char, about 0.1 s at 10 Mbp"); nothing is Unsupported. This equals the ESA column of ADR-001 section 6 in the steady state (the table says "SuffixLink D until the pack exists, then N", "ISA N (pack)", "Lcp, Lca D without the RMQ helper, N with it", "ExtendLeft D"). Differences from the table that I chose and declare: **Parent is Degraded before the pack** (the table says N: `TryParent` needs `PrevLess` / `NextLess` and the child table of this implementation stores first l-indices, not the PSV / NSV `up` / `down` fields of the original paper, so there is no pack-free O(1) parent); **LcpAccess is Native without the pack** (`CompactLcp` random access, binary search over the escapes only; the 1b.4 hand-over said `Pyramid.ValueAt`, Degraded). `CapabilityMatrix_BeforeThePack_...` pins the fresh state, `CoreOperations_NeverBuildTheNavigationPack` and `InvalidArguments_NeverBuildThePack` the laziness, `EachPackOperation_BuildsThePackExactlyOnce` and the 8-thread first-use test (`NavigationBuildCount == 1`) the once-only build. The rule "an operation reported Unsupported throws `NotSupportedException`" is a generic conformance test (`UnsupportedOperations_ThrowNotSupportedAndNeverFakeAnAnswer`, every engine, every interface the engine implements); it is **vacuous for the ESA** (nothing is Unsupported) and exercised only by the Ukkonen adapter (`ExtendLeft`).
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** Committed first (`b65e8f95`): the oracle (`TestSupport/IndexContractOracle.cs`, itself checked by `IndexContractOracleTests`: the hand-derived tree of "banana", and for every string of sigma 2 up to 8, sigma 3 up to 5, sigma 4 up to 4 its nodes equal the lcp-intervals of the definitional `BruteLcpTree` plus one leaf per suffix), `EsaContractChecker` (every operation on every locus), `EsaSuffixIndexTests` (hand-derived "banana", empty text, n = 1, matrix, laziness, validation, three layouts, concurrency), the Exhaustive and Fuzz classes, three conformance tests, the three ESA factories, and a stub whose constructor throws `NotImplementedException`. **93 of the 122 default Contract tests were red**, 27 green (the Ukkonen adapter fixture, the contract-type tests, the 3 oracle self-tests; the oracle had one bug of its own, found by its self-test before the commit: the empty string was matched at position n + 1), 2 skipped (the Ukkonen adapter has no navigation or SA access). The 8 explicit tests were compiled against the stub but not run red. After the implementation (`387c3951`): **115 of 122 green on the first run, 2 skipped, 5 red results, all of them test bugs, not engine bugs (three distinct ones; 120 green after they were fixed)**: the 8-thread test asked for rank 300 + i beyond n = 400; the navigation conformance test summed the children of a leaf; the "fabricated loci" test claimed that exactly the valid tuples are accepted (see the validation limit below). Those three were changed after the implementation; the last became the documented-rules test of `69594952`. **Written after the implementation, before the audit:** `ForgedLoci_...` (box of tuples against the documented rules), the corrupt-child-table tests, `InvalidSymbolWithAMalformedLocus_StillThrows`. **Written after the audit:** the wrapping-position assertions of the text view and `CorruptChildTable_FirstLIndexBehindTheInterval_IsNoLocus`.
- **Counts.** Esa default **535 -> 638** (635 passed + 3 skipped: the checked-build-only test and the 2 Ukkonen skips; 1 m 09 s; +103: 3 oracle tests, 31 in `EsaSuffixIndexTests`, 69 conformance cases = 3 new tests x 4 engine fixtures + the 19 existing tests x 3 new fixtures); **checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`) 636 passed + 2 skipped of 638** (1 m 12 s); explicit categories: the new classes only (no shared production code was touched): **Exhaustive 6 / 6** (all binary strings up to 10, ternary up to 7, four letters up to 5, default layout; binary up to 8 with `Packed` and with `Reference3`; ternary up to 6 with both; 31 s together with Fuzz at the first seed), **Fuzz 2 / 2** (3,000 cases up to n = 45 at every locus, 250 cases up to n = 300 sampled, layout drawn per case; seed 1, then seed 7 at scale 2, 1 m; the Fuzz class also once in the checked build, 26 s). The older explicit suites (Exhaustive 58, Fuzz 26) and Vectors were not re-run: no kernel, scan, child-table or navigator code changed.
- **Mutation audit** (`analysis/mutation/esa_s1b_index_{core,tree,arrays,text}_mutants.json`, second round `..._{core_b,tree_b,text_b}_...`, `results_s1b_index.tsv` with notes, `run_s1b_index_audit.sh`, `run_index_mutants.py`: every build and every test run has a watchdog (the whole process group is killed after 300 s / 150 s; a hang counts as killed); three parallel worktrees, tests of `Esa.Tests/Contract` in the default run, Release first, survivors again in the checked build): **127 fresh hand-written mutants of the new production code only** (`EsaSuffixIndex.cs` 61, `.Tree.cs` 39, `.Arrays.cs` 19, `EsaIndexText.cs` 8: wrong comparison, off-by-one, swapped or dropped operands, dropped guards, wrong table slot, inverted flags, wrong ranges of the symbol, wrong capability); **5 did not compile** (analyzer errors: C07 unused constant, C08 / C09 / C40 unused local, T39 unused parameter) and were replaced by compiling forms (C07b, C08b, C09b, C40b, T39b: all killed); of the 122 that compiled, **117 were killed** (3 of them by the watchdog or a host crash: C25, T26, T38) and **5 survived**: **3 equivalent** (A17 and A18, the two borders of `Lca` may start one rank inside the range because the minimum guarantees `LCP >= value` there; C54, `first >= lb` against `first > lb` in the node check, rejected anyway by the value comparison that follows) and **2 real gaps, both closed with tests and killed on re-run** (X06: without `ThrowIfNegative`, `CopyTo(-(2^32), ...)` cast the start to int 0 and copied - the wrapping 64-bit positions were added to the text-view test; C55: no forged tuple of the box can tell a first l-index behind its interval (exhaustive search over binary strings up to 9 and ternary up to 6 in the three layouts found none), so the table is corrupted through reflection in `CorruptChildTable_FirstLIndexBehindTheInterval_IsNoLocus`). In total **127 compiled mutant sites, 124 killed, 3 equivalent**, 134 rows in the result file (the 5 replacements and 2 re-runs included).
- **Deviations from the ADR sketch and choices made** (ADR text not edited): (1) the capability differences above (Parent Degraded before the pack, LcpAccess Native without it); (2) a leaf locus counts its terminator (`Length` of the leaf of suffix p is `n - p + 1`), so `Aux` and `Length` of a leaf are one more than the text it spells, and a leaf is reached only through `TryChild` / `GetChildren` (`TryExtendRight` stops one symbol short, at a point inside the leaf edge); (3) `Count` and `Locate` ignore rank 0 also for the leaf of the empty suffix (`Count = 0`, `Locate` writes nothing), so `Count(Root) = TextLength` and the counts of the children add up; this is the library's convention for `CountOccurrences("")`; (4) `TryChild`, `GetChildren`, `TrySuffixLink`, `EdgeSymbol` take explicit nodes only and throw `ArgumentException` for a point inside an edge; `TryParent` (the node above the node below the point), `IsExplicitNode`, `TryExtendRight/Left`, `Count`, `Locate` take any locus; (5) `EdgeSymbol(node, k)` is the text at `SA[Lb] + parentDepth + k`, -1 from the end of the text on and for every offset at or beyond n (no 64-bit overflow), -1 for the root; behind the incoming edge the first suffix of the interval is read, as the navigator does, so only offsets inside the edge are occurrence independent; (6) `Lca` of equal ranks is the leaf (with its terminator), of the empty text the root; `Lcp(i, i)` is `n - SA[i]` and builds no pack; (7) ranks and positions are `long` and validated as `long` (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; a position of 2^32 + 3 is refused, not truncated); `Locate` with a negative `skip` is refused before the locus is looked at; (8) the child walk of `EsaNavigator` was duplicated (own generic code, own tests, corrupt tables end in `InvalidOperationException` as there); the navigator was not refactored; (9) `EsaIndexText` is a new internal `IIndexText` (the tests had their own); (10) conformance suite: three tests added (`UnsupportedOperations_...`, `SuffixArrayAccess_EqualsTheDefinitions`, `Navigation_ChildrenPartition...`), the ESA factories added, the optional-interface tests are skipped for engines without the interface; (11) small additive members of existing Esa classes: none.
- **Not verified / limits.** (a) **Locus validation is O(1) and not exact**: it rejects every tuple that breaks the shape rules, every leaf tuple whose depth is not that of its suffix, and every interval whose borders or first l-index do not fit, but an interval that merely looks like a node (the interior is not scanned) can pass; in the box of forged tuples around "banana" / "mississippi" / "abababa" 4 / 17 / 6 look-alikes pass (reported by the test); a locus that the index handed out is never one, a forged locus has no defined result; an exact check needs the pyramid (state dependent) or an O(width) scan and is left to the `Checked` mode of 1b.7; (b) nothing was timed: no per-call cost of the contract surface was measured (a `Resolve` is three reads, `TryExtendLeft` is O(width of the interval) and was not run on a large text); (c) texts above 2^30 symbols (the `Packed` layout is exercised on small texts only) and alphabets above 256 symbols only in the unit tests; (d) the audit ran the `Contract` tests only (default categories); the explicit categories were not run per mutant; (e) the generic `Unsupported` test never meets a navigation or SA-access engine that reports Unsupported (there is none); (f) thread safety was tested with 8 threads on one text and the once-only pack build; the structures are read-only.
- **Open points handed to 1b.6 / 1b.7.** (a) 1b.6: the facade wraps `EsaSuffixIndex` (`new EsaSuffixIndex(EsaIndex)` or `EsaSuffixIndex.Build(text, layout)`) for `ISuffixTreeNavigation` / `ISuffixArrayAccess`, keeps its own `int` surface over `EsaIndex`, refuses `NodeCount > int` itself, and registers the backend factory next to the conformance factories (`IndexEngineFactories.All`); `Count(Root)` is n and the empty text has one node; disposal of the pack stays with the facade (`EsaIndex` is not `IDisposable`); (b) 1b.7: `EsaSuffixIndex` reads `EsaIndex.SuffixArray` (`int[]`), `EsaIndex.Lcp` (`CompactLcp`) and the child-table structs directly, so a memory-mapped index needs either the same three abstractions or a second implementation behind `ISuffixIndex`; the `Checked` mode can make `Resolve` exact with the pyramid (minimum over `lb + 1 .. rb` equals `Aux`) and should keep the corrupt-table tests of this package as its pattern; (c) `TryExtendLeft` is the only O(width) operation: the S3 / S7 backward-search structures (Psi, BWT) would make it native; (d) the facade's `ISuffixTreeNavigation.GetChildren` is a two-pass walk (count, then write): a caller that passes a span of the right size pays two walks of at most sigma' l-indices.

a `StringTextSource` is used without a copy). **Backend factory:** the production factory is `EsaSuffixTreeFactory` (the counterpart of `PersistentSuffixTreeFactory`); a factory that selects among Ukkonen, v6 and the ESA must reference all three projects, so it cannot live in `SuffixTree.Esa` and `SuffixTree.Core` cannot reference the engines: the cross-backend seam is `SuffixTreeBackends` in the Esa test project (name, `Func<string, ISuffixTree>`, `Func<ITextSource, ISuffixTree>`), and the contract engines are registered where the instructions put them, `IndexEngineFactories.All` (`esa-facade`, `esa-facade-packed`: the 22 conformance tests of 1b.0-1b.5 now also run on the facade object itself, 44 new test cases).
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** Committed first (`e9c83d09`): the stub (every member and the constructor throw `NotImplementedException`), the backend seam, the derived copies, the hand-written classes (`FacadeSurfaceTests`, `FacadeKnownAnswerTests` (literal answers of the Mcp.Core tests), `FacadeDifferentialTests` (definitions + Ukkonen + v6, three layouts, named texts, adversarial families at n 1-150, random strings, 4,000-5,000-symbol texts, logical hash), `FacadeLifetimeTests` (v6 and the three ESA layouts), `FacadeInt32LimitTests`, `FacadeNavigationTests` (the 1b.5 contract checker on the facade object, int and long surface consistency), `FacadeConcurrencyTests`, `FacadePrintTreeTests`) and the explicit `FacadeExhaustiveTests` / `FacadeFuzzTests` (compiled against the stub, not run red). **Default run on the stub: 2,768 tests, 877 red, all of them new and all of them an ESA fixture (esa 648, esa-packed 86, esa-reference3 86, esa-facade 22 + 22, 13 class-level tests); the other 1,253 new tests are the Ukkonen (616) and persistent-heap (628 + 3 skipped) fixtures of the same assertions, which pass (the evidence that every derived assertion is backend-agnostic), plus 6 that need no tree.** First run after the implementation (Facade + conformance filter): 2,211 passed, 6 skipped, **1 red, and it was a defect of the implementation, not of the test: the Ukkonen test `LRS_IsCached_ReturnsSameInstanceOnSecondCall` (ported) found that the ESA built a new substring on every call; the facade now caches the string.** Everything else turned green at once. **Written after the implementation, honestly not red first:** the LRS cache, the three tests that came out of drafting the mutant list (zero-copy `StringTextSource`, 70,001-symbol custom text source across the chunk boundary, the layout backends build their layout), the null-index assertion, and after the audit the four gap tests below, `LongestRepeatedSubstring_IsComputedOnce_...`, the `PrintTree` of the empty text and the parameter-name assertions of the factory. The Persistent-origin and Mcp-origin copies and the generator config were written before the code (in the red commit).
- **Counts.** Esa default **638 -> 2,777** (2,769 passed + 8 skipped; +2,139 = 1,701 derived copies (1,341 from `SuffixTree.Tests`, 360 from `Persistent.Tests`; each ported method runs for ukkonen, persistent-heap and esa: 447 + 120 test cases per backend) + 394 hand-written + 44 conformance cases of the facade; 1 m 48 s against 1 m 09 s before); **checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`) 2,770 passed + 7 skipped of 2,777** (1 m 49 s); explicit categories: the new classes only (no shared production code was touched): **Exhaustive +7 (sigma 2 up to length 10, sigma 3 up to 7, sigma 4 up to 5 with the default layout; sigma 2 up to 8 with Packed and with Reference3; sigma 3 up to 5 with both; the logical hash and the `PrintTree` lines of every binary string up to 9 against the Ukkonen tree), 7 / 7 in 3 s; Fuzz +2 (2,500 cases up to n = 120 against definitions, Ukkonen and v6 with the layout drawn per case, 300 cases up to n = 3,000 against Ukkonen), 2 / 2 in 1 min**; the sensitivity of both classes was checked once with a deliberately wrong `FindAllLongestRepeatedSubstrings` (8 of the 9 tests fail at the first case, the ninth does not touch that member). The older explicit suites (Exhaustive 64, Fuzz 28 after 1b.5) and Vectors were NOT re-run: no kernel, scan, child-table, navigator or index class changed. The Ukkonen (494), persistent (672) and Mcp.Core (137) suites were NOT re-run: nothing they depend on changed (the diff outside the new files and the Esa test project is empty; the Esa test project already referenced Ukkonen and v6, the only project-file change is the `CsCheck` package reference).
- **Traceability (`analysis/s1b/facade_traceability.tsv`, generated by `port_facade_tests.py` from `port_facade_config.py`; `facade_member_coverage.tsv` lists for every member of the interfaces the new test classes that call it, no member is uncovered).** 765 rows, none blank: **391 ported** (330 Ukkonen-origin + 61 Persistent-origin methods), **40 covered by a new test** (12 Persistent-origin, 28 Mcp.Core: the literal answers are re-asserted in `FacadeKnownAnswerTests`, lifetime and guard tests in `FacadeLifetimeTests` / `FacadeInt32LimitTests`), **334 not backend-agnostic with the concrete reason** (42 Ukkonen: wall-clock guards of the Ukkonen builder, `StringTextSource` unit tests, the `ToString` and `EstimatePrintTreeCapacity` internals, the static `TryBuild` and `Empty`; 243 Persistent: v6 format, storage providers, loader validation, builder, serializer round trips, memory-mapped fixtures; 49 Mcp.Core: argument validation and DTO mapping of the MCP wrappers and the genomics helpers, which build their own tree). Inventory: 32 of the 33 Ukkonen test files with tests build the tree with `SuffixTree.Build` directly (344 call sites), the Persistent files through `PersistentSuffixTreeFactory.Create(new StringTextSource(...))`, `BuildTree` helpers over the memory-mapped builder, or the abstract `SuffixTreeTestBase.CreateTree`; the generator rewrites only those construction calls (and `using` / cast idioms around them), the namespace and the class header (a fixture per backend) and removes the methods that are listed with a reason, so the assertions of the derived copies are the original ones. Three things the derivation uncovered: (1) the two ParamNames of the length refusal differ by design (Ukkonen `value`, v6 and the ESA factory `text`: `backend_skip` in the config, asserted in `FacadeInt32LimitTests`); (2) `Build_TextExactlyAtLimit_...` is rejected earlier by the v6 heap mode (its storage estimate); (3) `PersistentSuffixTree.LongestCommonSubstringInfo(ReadOnlySpan<char>)` is a member of the class, not of `ISuffixTree` (the facade has it too, asserted in `FacadeSurfaceTests`).
- **Mutation audit** (`analysis/mutation/esa_s1b_facade_{core,diag,nav,factory}_mutants.json`, round 2 `..._{core_b,diag_b,nav_b,factory_b}_...`, round 3 `..._surv_..._...`, `results_s1b_facade.tsv` merged with notes and the raw `results_s1b_facade_*.tsv`, `run_s1b_facade_audit.sh` over `run_index_mutants.py`: every build and test run has a watchdog, a hang counts as killed; three parallel worktrees; round 1 with the hand-written classes and the conformance suite only (`Facade` without `Ported`), round 3 with the WHOLE default suite): **115 fresh hand-written mutants of the new production code only** (`EsaSuffixTree.cs` 52: the guards, constructor, lifetime, header values, search and analysis forwards, the LRS cache; `.Diagnostics.cs` 20: header, indentation, markers, link marks, terminator, labels, stack handling; `.Navigation.cs` 31: wrong forwards and dropped disposal checks; `EsaSuffixTreeFactory.cs` 12), **11 did not compile** (analyzer errors: unused parameter or field, identical method bodies, constant conditions) and were replaced by compiling forms, **104 compiled in round 1: 93 killed (1 by the watchdog: N07, dropping the skip of `Locate` makes the paged enumeration of the checker never end), 11 survived: 4 real gaps, closed with tests and killed on re-run (C34: the disposal check of `FindMaximalRepeatedPairs` was masked by the later read of `Text`, now `FindMaximalRepeatedPairs(0)` after `Dispose` in the order-of-checks test; C40: the span overload of the info is a member of the class that the interface-typed lifetime test never reached; D03: the empty text printed the same lines but built the pack for nothing; F09: a null `string` reached `StringTextSource` and named the argument `value`), 7 equivalent**; the 11 replacement forms: 8 killed, 3 equivalent. **Total: 115 compiled mutants, 105 killed, 10 equivalent** (arguments in the table: C09 the constructor guard cannot fire for a managed string (it becomes reachable with the mapped texts of 1b.7; its logic is pinned directly), C19 `LeafCount` and `MaxDepth` are both n, C31 / C32 the LRS string is returned from the cache or from the first publication (cost only, or two threads racing on the very first call), C39 the span LCS is defined through the span info, C50 `EsaIndex.Build` refuses null itself, D16b StringBuilder capacity hint, F08 read chunk size, N14b / N15b `Lcp` / `Lca` are symmetric). **A measurement caveat:** in round 3 the two symmetric mutants showed `killed(1)` in the checked configuration; it did not reproduce (6 unmutated checked runs under the same three-fold load passed, the mutants alone passed, an idle re-run of both mutants survived in both configurations, `results_s1b_facade_r3b_surv_nav.tsv`); the only wall-clock-bounded tests of `Esa.Tests` are the S1a review tests `ReviewS1aOptWorstCaseTests` (3 s and 5 s bounds), so the two were load flakes of an older timing assert and are not counted as kills.
- **Behaviours that differ from the other backends, on purpose, and how they are asserted.** (1) The order of `FindAllOccurrences` is suffix-array order (ADR-001 section 7; `ISuffixTreeSearch` allows it): compared sorted. (2) The representative of `LongestRepeatedSubstring` on a tie is the lexicographically smallest tied substring (1b.3) where the Ukkonen tree picks by child-storage order and v6 by its stored deepest node: compared by property (it must be one of the longest repeats, exact when the maximum is unique, `FindAllLongestRepeatedSubstrings` exactly equal). (3) The text position of `LongestCommonSubstringInfo` and of the anchors is the first occurrence the navigator offers: must be a valid occurrence, the (query position, length) pairs are exact. (4) `PrintTree`: the node lines equal the Ukkonen tree's exactly once the link marks are removed; the marks themselves differ between the old backends (Ukkonen prints the code of the first symbol, v6 the character) and the ESA prints the character like v6; every mark is checked against the definition of the suffix link on the printed tree (`FacadePrintTreeTests`); the ESA `PrintTree` builds the navigation pack once (4.1 B/char) for the marks and does not for the empty text. (5) The parameter name of the length refusal is `text` (v6 factory), Ukkonen says `value`; both `ArgumentException` from `SuffixTreeLimits`. (6) `NodeCount` above `int` is `NotSupportedException` (v6 has the builder's `InvalidOperationException` at its `NodeCountLimit`, Ukkonen has no such case). (7) There is no `TryBuild` and no shared `Empty` (static members of the Ukkonen class). (8) After `Dispose` every member throws `ObjectDisposedException`, as the v6 tree does (the Ukkonen tree is not disposable).
- **Deviations from the ADR sketch and choices made (ADR texts not edited; what an amendment would need):** (1) ADR-001 section 7 says `EsaSuffixTree : ISuffixTree, ISuffixTreeNavigation, ISuffixArrayAccess, IDisposable`; built so, with `ISuffixIndex.Text` explicit (name clash with `ISuffixTreeSearch.Text`); the amendment should say so and name `EsaSuffixTreeFactory`. (2) Sections 7 and 8 put the text-length guard at `n > int.MaxValue - 1` with `NotSupportedException("use the long API")`; the Ukkonen and v6 backends already refuse above `SuffixTreeLimits.MaxTextLength = 2^30 - 1` with `ArgumentException`, and "exceptions equal to the other backends" wins for the factory: the factory applies `SuffixTreeLimits` first, the ADR guard is the internal `EnsureTextLength` that the constructor calls for texts that do not come from a managed string (the mapped texts of 1b.7); through a string the ADR bound cannot be reached (a string has at most 2^30 - 33 symbols), and with it a node count above `int` cannot occur either, so `CheckedNodeCount` is a guard for the same future and is tested with a synthetic statistics value. (3) The section 7 table has no row for `PrintTree`: it needs the pack for the link marks (Degraded-style first-use cost, once). (4) Section 7 says nothing about lifetime beyond "disposable like `PersistentSuffixTree`": built as `ObjectDisposedException` for every member, idempotent `Dispose` that releases the pack and never builds it. (5) `LongestRepeatedSubstring` caches its string (4 bytes more per tree, the string stays alive for the life of the tree, as in the other backends). (6) No additive members of existing Esa classes were needed (the pack is disposed through the existing `NavigationBuilt` / `Navigation` / `Dispose`). (7) The facade is public; `EsaIndex`, `EsaSuffixIndex`, `ChildLayoutKind` stay internal and the test project reaches them through the existing `InternalsVisibleTo`.
- **Not verified / limits.** (a) Texts above 2^30 symbols and a node count above `int` exist only as seams (`EnsureTextLength`, `CheckedNodeCount`, the statistics override); no text of that size was built. (b) Nothing was timed: the facade is a thin forward (one disposal check per call, a `StringTextSource` and a struct copy per matching call); the S1 gate figures are 1b.9. (c) `Dispose` while another thread is inside a call is unsupported (as for v6); a pack built by a racing thread after the disposal would be released by its finalizers. (d) The derived copies cover the heap tree of v6; the memory-mapped, hybrid, reloaded and serialized variants of the persistent tests are v6 storage and were not ported. (e) The derived copies are regex rewrites of the original sources: if an original changes, the generator must be re-run (it fails loudly when a listed method or substitution no longer matches, and when a new test file appears that is in neither list). (f) `Fuzz` ran 2,800 cases once, not the 30-minute budget of 1b.8; v6 is compared in the differential classes only up to n = 150 (Ukkonen up to 5,000); `FacadeChecks` compares `PrintTree` and `Traverse` with Ukkonen and v6 on every default-corpus text. (g) The audit's checked-configuration column for round 1 ran the narrow filter; the survivors were re-run against the whole suite in round 3 in both configurations.
- **Open points handed to 1b.7 / 1b.8.** 1b.7 (container): (a) `EsaSuffixTree` holds an `EsaIndex`, whose `Search`, `Analysis`, `Traverse` and `Matching` read `int[]`, `CompactLcp` and the child-table structs directly, and whose text is a managed `string`; a memory-mapped facade needs the same three abstractions (or a second index class behind `ISuffixIndex`) and a text view of 64-bit length (`EsaIndexText` is string based; `ITextSource` has `int Length`); (b) disposal of mapped sections: today `Dispose` releases only the pack (a `GCHandle` on the managed suffix-array array plus the pinned ISA and pyramid arrays); with mappings the order must be pack, text view, sections, file handle, idempotent, and a read racing the disposal would touch an unmapped view (the per-member `_disposed` check does not close that race; document it or add a reader count); (c) the `Checked` mode: the facade lets `InvalidOperationException` from the corrupt-table guards of the walks pass through (tested at index level in 1b.4 and 1b.5, not through the facade); `NodeCount` takes the statistics value, so a container header value flows through `CheckedNodeCount`; `EnsureTextLength` is where a header `n` above `int.MaxValue - 1` must be refused; (d) `PrintTree` needs the pack; a container without the pack sections builds it in the heap as the in-memory index does. 1b.8 (full differential fuzz): `facade_member_coverage.tsv` is the operation coverage list (every member of the four interfaces and of the two contract interfaces maps to the classes that call it), `FacadeChecks.CheckEverything` is the shared per-text check (definitions, Ukkonen, v6 where given, properties for the open results) and is what the harness should call per case; `FacadeConcurrencyTests` (8 threads, one shared facade, once-only pack build checked through `NavigationBuildCount`) is the 4-thread test to extend with checksums; v6 should join the large-n cases (it is compared only up to n = 150 here).

### ADR-004 E3b 2026-11-11 (external-memory PH1; one implementer context; owner decision of the same day, ADR-004 section 6c)

- 2026-11-11 ADR-004 E3b [x] (commits `26109a36` tests first, `4a70bae0` implementation, `b1a079f7` command and component tests, `3b602571`, `b03223ab`, `50b3824c` rows, `043bfde6` audit round 1). **The difference-cover rank file (PH1) built in external memory.** `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`: `ExternalDcs.BuildRankFile(text, cover, rankPath, options)` = sample buckets (one scan of the sample positions per bucket, range `[lo, hi)` of the strict order (key, X-prefix, position), RAM sort, equal prefixes named, runs sorted by slot), scatter-window merge, rank file through 1 MiB mapped windows when the names are distinct, otherwise the reduced string as a 32-bit big-endian file written by |D| cursors, in-RAM SA-IS below N_base and the E3 `EmBuilder` plus a distribution-based inverse (`SaInverter`) above it, same builder one level down for the reduced string's own rank file; `SampleNamer`, `SampleGeometry`, `Runs`, `NameSinks`, `MappedWindowWriter`; commands `e3b` (rows, `--scale-n`, `--verify`, `--touch-buffers`) and `packrand` (streamed packed random DNA). - **Tests (red first, exact account).** Committed first (`2e056fd6`): the stub (every member with logic throws `NotImplementedException`; records and exceptions are complete) and **123 Esa tests: 120 red, 3 green against the stub** (two checks of defaults and of the tuple layout, which are data the stub already holds, and `ADeviceThatCannotBeSized_IsSurfaced_NotHung`, which accepted any exception and so accepted the stub's; it was tightened to `IOException` when the implementation showed what is thrown), and **10 differential tests: 10 red**. Written after the code, in response to the first measurements and the audit: 22 more Esa tests (the Lemire divider, the Phi mapper's checks, the limits of the 32-bit positions, `ScatterInto` on damaged chunks, the stage-by-stage release of the scratch, the shortfall of a read by one byte, a `/proc` that vanishes, a source that is open elsewhere, a sampler that must be woken, the message of a refused reservation, a missing slot at the first and last place of every chunk, the tuple sizes of other value types) and the two cost tests.
- **Counts.** Esa default **2,777 -> 2,922** (2,914 passed + 8 skipped, 1 m 47 s; +145); Esa Exhaustive **71 / 71** (9 m 9 s), Fuzz **30 / 30** (8 m 35 s), checked build **2,915 passed + 7 skipped** (1 m 48 s); `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 / 672**; `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **479 / 479** (469 + 10 differential tests, 14 m 7 s), its External subset in the checked build 6 / 6; `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` builds.
- **Choices made** (ADR-004 section 5a has the reasons; none is a public API, a format or a behaviour verified in E4-E9): the budget is a decorator next to `NativeArena`, not a parameter of it (cost: nothing stops a task from bypassing it, so 1b.7b-e construct `BudgetedArena` only); the counters are instances (no process-wide state); the `/proc` readers are injectable and degrade to "unavailable"; the mapped-window default is 1 MiB; keys and positions are 32-bit as in the prototypes; the pass-1 loop is a method of its own marked `AggressiveOptimization` (the first measurement showed +7 .. +19 % because the runtime compiled the loop as an on-stack replacement of an unoptimised frame) and the division by R is Lemire's multiplication (exact for all 32-bit keys, tested against `/`).
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.7a and what was done:** E3b(a) / E4 (a) the DIST primitive generalised in the tuple and the target: done (`Dist`, `DistScratch`); E5(d) / E6 the spill of a chain: `SpillStack` reproduces 16 B x (height - about 2 blocks) and the file bytes; E6(d) the explicit I/O counters: ported to the byte (the counters equal the prototype's on every run; the 5.5 % gap to `rchar + wchar` stays unattributed, it is not a property of the counting code); E9(a), (f) windows of at least 1 MiB: the default of `MappedWindowWriter` and `DistOptions`; E8-run (b) positions are `uint`: kept, refused above 2^32 - 2 with `NotSupportedException`; E8-run (d) arm64: `Math.BigMul` and `AggressiveOptimization` are standard, not run there. The rest belongs to 1b.7b-e (listed below) or to the owner.
- **Not verified / limits.** Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (only the simulated platform and the Linux file source; flush-on-dispose of the view accessor and sparse `SetLength` are Linux facts here); anything above 10^8 entries (3x10^9: P = 375, arena 56.6 MB, about 60 s and 24 B/char are ESTIMATES by x30); a full disk under a mapped window (the writer is created sparse, so it would show as a fault of a mapped page, not as an exception); `DistScratch` appended from several threads under the real PH4 load (8 threads on small data only); 40-bit keys; a legal value equal to the `Fill` marker is not detected by the exactly-once check; the scratch-release test samples counters from a polling thread (timing-based); the run-to-run noise of this disk is about +-8 %, so the counter overhead is bounded by count x cost (0.02 %), not isolated by a run. Defects found in the **prototypes** (not fixed, they are not touched): `ArenaBudget.Reserve` wraps at a cap of `long.MaxValue`; `SpillStack.Pop` lowers the height before a read-back that can fail; `BudgetedArena.ReleaseTo` / `Dispose` keep the bytes when the arena throws on a guard band; `RssSampler.Stop` waits up to an interval; `ProcIoSnapshot.Read` throws on a malformed file. In the **production code** the audit found no defect; the first G3 measurement found a performance defect of the first version (fixed).
- **Open points handed to 1b.7b-e:** (a) 1b.7b: `MappedWindowWriter` is the minimal port (`Append`, `WriteAt`, counters, 1 MiB default); section offsets, final-length-first, CRC pass and `MappedTextReader` are 1b.7b's; **a full disk shows as a fault on a mapped page**, `FileStreamOptions.PreallocationSize` (posix_fallocate) would raise ENOSPC at creation but changes the write pattern E9 measured: measure before deciding; (b) 1b.7c: PH4 B1 / B2 / B3 and PH5 move onto `DistScratch` / `DistWriter` / `DistReader` and `SpillStack` (the prototype's `Spool` and `SpoolWriter` are the same design; `ChunkDivider` replaces `LcpGeometry.ChunkOf`); `BuildPhi` writes a Phi file (n entries) which PH4's base mode does not need (a check, or the bounded mode's text-order file); the consumer of `SpillStack` must treat an `IOException` / `EndOfStreamException` as the end of the build (the stack stays consistent, a retry fails the same way); (c) every task that allocates native memory must construct `BudgetedArena` and the builder one shared `ArenaBudget` (caps of E6 as decided: 160 + 64 MiB) and one `IoCounters`; the gate **RssAnon <= 320 MB at every n with four threads** is read with `RssReader` / `RssSampler`; where `/proc` is missing the figure is "unavailable" and the builder must say so instead of passing; (d) 40-bit positions (both strands): the key of `DistEntry` and `ChunkDivider` are 32-bit; a 64-bit key needs a 128-bit multiplication or a hardware division (cost not measured); (e) 1b.7d: the rank file of PH1 is `Dist.Scatter` with a skipping mapper and a zero fill; the mapper needs `SampleGeometry.TrySlotOfRed` (a test-local struct in the differential test shows the shape); (f) 1b.7e: `ProcIoSnapshot` and `IoMeasurement` give the syscall and block-layer deltas next to the explicit counters for the report of the whole build; ISA stays optional (`Dist.BuildIsa`, 24 B/char); Psi from the ISA is a random-read pass, not a DIST.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default, Exhaustive, Fuzz and the checked build (`src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed; the new code goes through `Buf<T>`), `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (always for 1b.7), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (references Esa through EmProto and holds the new differential tests; its External subset also in the checked build), build of `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench`; **skipped, with the rule:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change), the checked build of Persistent and of the whole EmProto project (nothing they compile differently changed); no widening trigger fired. Residual risk as stated in the policy (couplings through data files, reflection or child processes) is not visible to project references; nothing of that kind was touched.
- **Cost of 1b.7a (wall clock between the first and the last commit; tokens are an estimate):** about 2.5 hours of wall clock in one session (07:42 to about 10:10 UTC on the clock of the machine), of which about 40 minutes of measurement (the G3 protocols), 40 minutes of mutant runs (256 builds + test runs, 11 s each) and 40 minutes of the affected-set tests; about 0.9 M tokens (an ESTIMATE, not measured); the cap of the brief (6 h, 1.6 M) was not reached.
- **Orchestrator verification of 1b.7a (2026-11-18):** pushed head `71ddfbb8`, only new files in `External`; the affected set run by me: Esa default 2,914 + 8 skipped, Exhaustive 71, Fuzz 30, Persistent 672, EmProto 479 (checked build, Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core skipped by the rule); my own G3 run (3 runs per cell, own SA file): production against prototype +9.6 / +1.7 / +1.2 / +8.3 % (cold A, warm A, cold B, warm B), I/O equal to the byte, 30 of 30 outputs equal to the reference ISA; the counters' <= 1 % overhead rests on the implementer's per-update count; session counter 2.45 h and 0.67 M tokens (the implementer estimated 0.9 M). Details in ADR-004 section 5a after the 1b.7a subsection.

### S1b 2026-11-18 work package 1b.7b (MappedTextReader, TEXT codec 4, the mapped writer and its reservation in `SuffixTree.Esa/External`; one implementer context)

- 2026-11-18 1b.7b [x] `9286bf27` (code; tests first `3e009b06`: 99 of 100 Esa tests red against a throwing stub; differential tests `b3472d8e`; cost harness `f4946bb6`; common-prefix prologue `01b88e8d`; G3 rows `3688384d`; audit rounds 1-4 `5de4d775`, `a489bb11`, `276dd6ef`, `8ce2133f`; this entry in the commit after them). **Outcome in one paragraph:** the text side of the external-memory builder exists as production code with its own tests in `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa/External` (namespace `SuffixTree.Esa.External`, 4 new files and one edited, all `internal`, no file outside `External/` changed, no csproj changed, no line of `apps/` changed): **`PackedTextFormat`** (the layout of TEXT codec 4: `b = max(1, ceil(log2 sigma))` bits per dense symbol, a big-endian bit stream, 64 zero bytes of padding; the layout of the prototype's `.pk` file, byte for byte), **`MappedTextReader`** (long-indexed random reads through a mapping: `Window`, `Key` = the first `64 / b` symbols as a `ulong`, `Symbol`, `ReadSymbols`, `Lce` with an AVX2 path guarded by `IsSupported` and the scalar path of arm64, `CompareSuffixes`, `Verify`; O(1) validation at open, `InvalidDataException` for damaged input, memory-safe for any argument), **`PackedTextWriter`** (the prototype's two passes: `Scan`, `Pack` into any range of a mapped file, `WriteFile` with `<target>.tmp`, `fsync` and rename), **`MappedOutputFile`** (a file of known final length with one shared mapping, range writers for several tasks or cursors, `Complete`, and the **reservation of the disk space**: `fallocate` through the BCL where the platform has it, an explicit free-space check where it has not, both behind an injectable source) and the extended **`MappedWindowWriter`** (ranges, `Flush`). Gates (fixed by the orchestrator before the work; the table is in ADR-004 section 5a "1b.7b"): **G1 contract PASS** (108 Esa tests: 100 written first, 99 red and 1 green against the stub, 8 added by the audit; reader against a bit-level oracle for b = 1 .. 8, exhaustive texts, alignment, 2^31 / 2^32 positions and offsets on sparse files, damaged input, writer round trip, determinism, atomic finish, failure modes incl. a real full file system)**, **G2 equality PASS** (packed files byte-identical to the prototype `Prepare` with the same alphabet, counts and SHA-256 on `dna_10m`, `prot_1m`, `rep_10m`, an N-containing text, a 256-symbol text and the `dna_100m` file of the E9 setup; keys and symbols equal at 10^6 random positions for b = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, common prefixes equal with equal step counts on the vector and the scalar path; mapped writer byte-identical, also above 2^32 bytes), **G3 cost** (key extraction **+2.2 %** over the prototype, symbol -0.4 %, the scalar common prefix on short matches +10.6 / +11.4 % (the price of the clamp), the vector one and the long matches +-4 %; writer: time -2 .. +5 %, device writes equal or +3.6 % for the shared file; `fallocate` against sparse: time -1.8 / -0.9 %, device writes +0.4 / 0.0 %, creation 1.5 ms against 0.8 ms for 400 MB: **`Auto` = `Preallocate`**, a full disk is an `IOException` at creation; RssAnon 1.0 / 3.2 MB above the baseline), **G4 portability PASS** (simulated platform without `fallocate`; scalar equals vector; not run on macOS, Windows or arm64), **G5 PASS** (Esa default 3,022 + 8 skipped, Exhaustive 71, Fuzz 30, Persistent 672, EmProto 497; checked build, Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core skipped by the rule). Mutation audit: **201 mutants, 179 killed, 22 argued equivalent, 0 open**, four rounds, no defect of the production code found, only gaps of the tests (ADR-004 section 5a). Open points: the four ADR-003 gaps for TEXT codec 4 (below).
- **What was built** (details and the design choices in ADR-004 section 5a "1b.7b"): `PackedTextFormat.cs`, `MappedTextReader.cs`, `PackedTextWriter.cs`, `MappedOutputFile.cs`, `MappedWindowWriter.cs` (edited); tests `Esa.Tests/External` (105 new: `PackedTextFormatTests`, `MappedTextReaderTests`, `PackedTextWriterTests`, `MappedOutputFileTests`, the oracle `TextOracle`, the `FakeReservationSource`) and `EmProto.Tests/External` (`TextDifferentialTests`: 18 differential tests; `TextCostTests` and `WriterCostTests`: `[Explicit]`, driven by `analysis/em/run_1b7b_g3.sh`); raw rows `analysis/em/rows_1b7b_g3.tsv` (and `rows_1b7b_g3_reader_v1.tsv`, the reader before the fix of the common-prefix prologue), table script `tables_1b7b_g3.py`; mutation lists, results, runner and summary `analysis/mutation/*1b7b*`.
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** Committed first (`3e009b06`): the stub (every member with logic throws `NotImplementedException`) and **100 Esa tests: 99 red, 1 green against the stub** (the constants of the layout, which are data the stub already holds). Implementation `9286bf27` (245 External tests green: the 145 of 1b.7a and the 100). Written after the code: 5 more Esa tests (the step counts at the edges of the vector loop, `CompareSuffixes` at and beyond the end, an input with a letter that was not there, a missing directory that must not read the input, the recreation of a file that held older content) in response to the first mutation analysis of the lists, and the 18 differential tests (they need the working production code to be written against; they are tests of equality with the prototype, not of a contract that a stub could fail).
- **Counts.** Esa default **2,922 -> 3,030** (3,022 passed + 8 skipped, 1 m 55 s; +108); External subset 145 -> 253; Esa Exhaustive **71 / 71** (9 m 18 s), Fuzz **30 / 30** (8 m 39 s); `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` **672 / 672**; `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **479 -> 497** (+18 differential tests; 14 m 38 s); `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` builds; the checked build was not run (rule below).
- **Choices made** (ADR-004 section 5a has the reasons; none is a public API; the layout is the prototype's, no format was invented): the reader is the prototype's `PackedText` plus the checks (damaged input is `InvalidDataException`, a negative position `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, a position at or beyond n reads the zero padding, the common-prefix limit is clamped, a read after `Dispose` throws, `UseVectors` is guarded by the hardware); the writer is the prototype's two passes split into `Scan` and `Pack` so that 1b.7e can aim `Pack` at the TEXT section, `WriteFile` is atomic (the prototype wrote in place) and writes the padding explicitly; `MappedOutputFile` = one mapping, several range writers (offsets relative to the range); the public constructor of `MappedWindowWriter` stays sparse so that `Dist` is untouched; `Auto` = `Preallocate`; the explicit fallback is a free-space check through `DriveInfo` (injectable) followed by a sparse file; the common-prefix prologue was slimmed after the first G3 (+21 % -> +10.6 %). No arena is constructed by this package (the buffers are managed arrays of 1 MiB), so the 1b.7a condition (`BudgetedArena` only) did not come into play.
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.7b and what was done:** 1b.7a (a) full disk under a mapped window, section offsets, final-length-first, `MappedTextReader`: done (the reservation measured and chosen, ranges, `Complete`, the reader; the CRC pass is 1b.7e); E9 (a), (f) windows of at least 1 MiB: the default of `PackedTextWriterOptions` and `MappedOutputFile.CreateWriter`, the harness reproduces the 64 KB finding (3.02 s against 1.8 s); E8-run (b) positions `uint`: the writer refuses n above 2^32 - 4 (`MaxSymbols`) with `NotSupportedException`, the reader is `long`-indexed and was tested above 2^32 symbols; E8-run (d) arm64 runs the scalar common prefix: the scalar path equals the vector one in every test (not run on arm64); E6 (e) CHILD at a section offset written at its final length: `CreateWriter(start, length)`, the CRC pass is 1b.7e. The rest belongs to 1b.7c-e or to the owner (below).
- **Not verified / limits.** Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the simulated platform only; `fallocate` through the BCL is a Linux fact here; the BCL's equivalents elsewhere were not run); a text of more than 10^8 symbols through the writer end to end (the reader and the mapped writer on sparse files with synthetic values at 2^31 and 2^32 symbols and above 2^32 bytes); the writer's time per symbol against the prototype's (the bytes are equal, the time was not compared; informational only); a file system without `fallocate` (the BCL ignores the request, the outcome says "asked"); a sparse file on a full disk (a signal); a read racing `Dispose`; `Verify` at 10^9 symbols; the AVX2 path on a machine without AVX2 (simulated by switching it off); the scalar common prefix on short matches is 10.6 / 11.4 % above the prototype (the clamp). Defects found: in the prototypes (not fixed, not touched): `UseVectors` can be switched on without AVX2, `CompareSuffixes(i, i)` returns 1, `Lce` compares the padding when `max` exceeds the text, `Prepare` writes in place and does not notice a grown input; in the production code of 1b.7a: a failed creation of a mapped writer left its file (now removed); in the new code: the G3 cost defect of the `Lce` prologue (fixed); the audit found no defect of the production code, only gaps of the tests.
- **Open points handed to the owner / ADR-003 (the container format is the owner's decision):** (1) TEXT codec 4 is under-specified in ADR-003 (where `bits_per_symbol` lives, the bit order, the tail and the padding, what `COUNT * ELEM_WIDTH == LENGTH` means for it): the prototype's layout is implemented and an amendment text is proposed in ADR-004 section 5a "1b.7b"; (2) `TEXT_SHA256`: ADR-003 says UTF-16LE code units, PH0 and the prototype hash the input bytes; (3) the symbol counts `C[]` have no home in ADR-003; (4) the width for DNA with N and IUPAC: Q3 says 4 bits for sigma <= 16, the prototype and every verified file use `ceil(log2 sigma)` (3 bits for sigma 5 to 8); `MinBits` can force the other reading.
- **Open points handed to 1b.7c-e:** (a) 1b.7c-d: `Lce` / `CompareSuffixes` are memory-safe for any arguments and clamp to the shorter suffix; `ReadSymbols` is the sequential read for PH1's scans; (b) 1b.7e: `Scan` gives the TEXT section's size and entry (`PackedTextInfo`), `Pack` writes into `MappedOutputFile.CreateWriter(sectionOffset, info.TotalBytes)`, `Complete(fsync: true)` ends the file, the CRC-32C of the section is a sequential pass over a reader's mapping (not built), `e8-verify` can open the TEXT section through `MappedTextReader.OpenRegion`; (c) create every big output (SA, LCP, CHILD, NEXTSYM, PSI, the container) through `MappedOutputFile.Create` with `Auto`, so that a full disk is an exception at creation (LCP files at the size known after PH4-B2); `Dist`'s targets still use the sparse constructor (their pattern, random-order windows, was not measured with `fallocate`); (d) the pre-flight of ADR-004 4.3 can use `IReservationSource.AvailableFreeBytes`; (e) `PackedTextWriter` refuses n above 2^32 - 4, the 40-bit positions for both strands remain out of the package; (f) the reader's `Verify` recomputes counts and SHA-256 and is the Structure-level check of the TEXT section.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default, Exhaustive and Fuzz (`src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed), `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (always for 1b.7), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (it holds the new differential tests), build of `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench`, all on the head `8ce2133f` (no source file changed afterwards; only documents); **skipped, with the rule:** the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`: no code that it compiles differently was added: no `ESA_BOUNDS` conditional, no `Buf<T>`, no arena), Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change); no widening trigger fired, **except that a new test starts a child process** (`mount` for a 4 MB `tmpfs`), which stays inside the project that ran in full. Residual risk as stated in the policy (couplings through data files, reflection or child processes are not visible to project references): the cost and differential tests read `/tmp/stbench` data files that no one changed; nothing else.

- **Tests (red first, exact account).** Committed first (`8cae48ec`): the stub (every member with logic throws `NotImplementedException`), **119 Esa tests (118 red, 1 green against the stub)** and the **8 differential tests (8 red)**. Implementation `9f706b94` (119 Esa tests and 8 differential tests green). Written after the code: 12 more Esa tests (boundary cases, the failure modes that the audit showed untested: the window size that is not a power of two, a failed `ChildScan.Create`, the deletion of the chunk files as soon as they are read, a failing B2 worker, the final-length check, the descriptors and mappings that a build or a failure must leave closed) and the 4 explicit classes (exhaustive, fuzz); the differential tests need the working code and the prototype's own files.
- **Counts.** Esa default **3,022 + 8 skipped -> 3,153 passed + 8 skipped** (+131; 1 m 28 s); Exhaustive and Fuzz, the checked build, Persistent, EmProto and the Bench build: see the sets below; heavy explicit runs of G1: exhaustive class 2 of 2 (10,658 strings through the whole stage with rotating tiny parameters; the families at 10^5 and 10^6 with three settings each), fuzz class 2 tests x six seeds at scale 10 = **102,000 cases**, all equal to the oracles.
- **Choices made** (ADR-004 section 5a has the reasons; none is a public API, a file format or a behaviour verified in E4-E9): path-based stage with range-based writers (1b.7e can aim them at container sections); a corrupt suffix array is refused (an entry outside `[0, n)`, a repeated entry, a wrong length: `InvalidDataException`; B2 checks that every position of a chunk occurs exactly once with a bit set of R bits per task, which the prototype's per-chunk counts do not (by reading)); the CHILD file is created at the first block, as in E6; the CHILD windows are counted in `IoCounters` (the prototype added them at the run level); `ChildCodec.Tagged` is a parameter (n up to 2^30 - 1); failure semantics (every output of a failed build is removed, the scratch subdirectories too, the scratch root only when the build created it); the RssAnon gate is a verdict (`Unavailable` / `Within` / `Exceeded`) and never an exception; the parameter rule is `ScaleRules` for these two stages (R = 8,000,000, two B2 workers of four under the 160 MiB cap, caps 160 + 64 MiB; `ScaleN` / `RuleN` kept for the 100 Mbp comparison); `MappedTextReader.Prefetch` instead of the prototype's raw pointer; sigma for the `.stats` text comes from the caller; fsync of the outputs is an option (`SyncOutputs`, off).
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.7c and what was done (by the heading in this file; "from X" = the entry that wrote it):** **"handed to E4 (PH4) and 1b.7"** (from E3b): (a) the DIST primitive generalised: done in 1b.7a, used here by B1 and B3; (b)-(e) PH1 / PH2 / PH3 are 1b.7d; (f) resume, 40-bit positions and both strands: not built (1b.7e and later). **"handed to E5 (PH5) and 1b.7"** (from E4): (a) the base mode only, as the owner decided (the bounded mode is not built); (b) `ILcpChunkConsumer` is the hook: built and tested, PH5 hangs on it (`SA[i]` for NEXTSYM is not read); (c) B2 workers: two of four under the 160 MiB cap, as in E4; (d) the two output files are written at their exact final length and the writers take ranges of a `MappedOutputFile` (placement as sections is 1b.7e); (e) B1 and B3 single-threaded: kept. **"handed to E6 and 1b.7"** (from E5): (a) the scan inside the single B3 thread with no second thread: kept, layout F not built; (b) NEXTSYM: not built; (c) codec: Packed default, Tagged a parameter, layout A only; (d) a^n spills 16 B per entry, no run-length encoding (owner): the spill is byte-equal to the prototype's (155,189,248 B at 10^7); (e) windows, fsync interval, flush on release: parameters of `LcpChildTuning` and `LcpChildOptions.SyncOutputs`, CHILD at a section offset: `ChildScan.Create(n, file, start, options)` is tested (the CRC pass is 1b.7e); (f) 40-bit positions: not built. **"handed to E7, E9 and 1b.7"** (from E6): (c) the output files, `.stats`, release order and the final-length check as they are: kept (`CheckLengths`, the stats text is byte-equal to the prototype's); (d) the counters now include the CHILD windows (`AddWindow`, `AddMappedWrite`) and the reads through mappings of the LCP files; the 5.5 % that the explicit counters were below rchar + wchar is not attributed; (a), (b) (the whole pipeline's RSS slope and the scratch I/O of PH3): not 1b.7c (PH4's counted I/O is equal to the prototype's). **"handed to E8, E9 and 1b.7"** (from E7): (a) R follows n as the real regime (series B): `LcpChildRules` fixes R = 8,000,000 (P = 375 at 3x10^9); `ScaleN` / `RuleN` stay for the comparison; (c) PH4's scratch is 12 B/char in the base mode: pinned by a test and by the counted scratch peak (1,200,000,000 B at 10^8); (e) the lean settings are `LcpChildTuning` fields; (b) PH1's buckets, (d) the cache line: not 1b.7c. **"handed to 1b.7"** (from E9): PSI, the BWT and PH6: 1b.7e, except (f), the window size of every mapped writer: LCP and CHILD windows default to 1 MiB, as parameters. **"handed to the E8 run and 1b.7"** (from E8): (b) positions `uint`: the stage refuses n above `PackedTextFormat.MaxSymbols` = 2^32 - 4 with `NotSupportedException` (the three limits that were one apart are one now), (d) arm64: the PLCP loop uses the reader's scalar common prefix and `Prefetch` is a guarded hint (equal results, not run on arm64), (e) `e8-verify` is the acceptance check of the production outputs (11 of 11 at 10^8; the adapter for the container is 1b.7e), (g) PH4's per-chunk buffers and the arena cap: the rule above and the 160 MiB budget; (a), (c), (f), (h): not 1b.7c. **"handed to 1b.7b-e"** (from 1b.7a): (b) done (PH4 B1 / B2 / B3 and PH5 on `DistScratch` / `DistWriter` / `DistReader` and `SpillStack`, `ChunkDivider` in place of `ChunkOf`, no Phi file in the base mode, an `IOException` of the stack ends the build and removes the outputs: tested with the spill directory removed under the scan), (c) done (`BudgetedArena` in every task, two `ArenaBudget`s, one `IoCounters`, RssAnon through `RssReader` / `RssSampler`, "unavailable" reported as such); (a), (d), (e), (f): 1b.7b, later, 1b.7d, 1b.7e. **"handed to 1b.7c-e"** (from 1b.7b): (a) `Lce` clamps, and the PLCP loop relies on it (the three `PlcpScan` limit mutants are equivalent only because of it); (c) the three big outputs are created through `MappedOutputFile.Create` with `Auto` (the LCP files after B2, when the width is known; CHILD at the first block): done; (b), (d), (e), (f): 1b.7e, later.
- **Not verified / limits.** Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (simulated platform and scalar path only); a text of more than 10^8 symbols (the 3x10^9 figures, P = 375 chunks, 78.37 MB per B2 task, 48 GB of spill for a^n and the time of about 8 s per 10^8 symbols x30, are ESTIMATES; the E8 run on the Mac measured the prototype at that size); the Packed read cost beyond 100 Mbp; the child table of a text between 2^30 and 2^32 - 4 symbols (sparse files for the limit checks only); a build that places CHILD at a section offset of a larger file (`ChildScan.Create` with `start` is tested, the builder does not offer it); `e8-verify` above 2x10^8 symbols; `SyncOutputs` on a real device; a full disk during the build (the outputs are created with the reservation, a full disk in the scratch is an `IOException` of `DistScratch`); the time of the permutation bits on their own (G3 was not run with them switched off); the prototype on a suffix array with a repeated entry inside one chunk (by reading, not run); NEXTSYM, layout F, the bounded mode and the second B3 thread are not built; the CHILD CRC pass and the container placement belong to 1b.7e. Defects found: in the **prototype** (not fixed, not touched): its per-chunk tuple counts pass a repeated entry inside one chunk (by reading); in the **new code**: the builder left the LCP files behind when a later step failed (found by a failure test, fixed `0bcad96a`), and the first G3 harness timed the prototype without its disposal (a harness defect, a^n looked +19 %, corrected); the audit found **ten gaps of the tests** (round 1) and one more (round 3), all closed with tests, and no defect of the production logic.
- **Open points handed to 1b.7d-e:** (a) **1b.7e** calls `LcpChildBuilder.Build` with the SA file of PH3, the reader of the text and one `IoCounters` for the whole build (`LcpChildOptions.Counters`), and reads from `LcpChildResult` what the header needs (`Lcp.Kind`, `Lcp.OverflowCount` = `PARAM0` of `LCP`, `Scan.NodeCount`, `InternalCount`, `LrsDepth`, `LrsRank`, `MaxLcp`, `StatsText(sigma)`); the outputs are three files: a container that wants them as sections either copies them (4-5 B/char more I/O) or builds `LcpOutputWriter` / `ChildScan` over ranges of its own `MappedOutputFile` and calls `ExternalLcp` / `ChildScan` directly (the LCP section size needs the width, which is known after B2); (b) the two arena budgets (160 + 64 MiB) are created inside the builder from `ArenaCapBytes` and `ChildArenaCapBytes`; one shared `ArenaBudget` across PH1-PH5 is `ExtLcpOptions.Budget` and `ChildBuildOptions.Budget` set by the caller; (c) the RssAnon verdict is the whole process (the host counts); per-phase figures are `RssAnonPeakMb` and `RssVerdict` taken per call; (d) PH4 needs the packed text open as a `MappedTextReader` for the whole stage (8 random reads per symbol in `Phi`) and the SA file complete and closed for writing; (e) the scratch is `ScratchDir/ph4` and `ScratchDir/ph5`: 12 B/char at the peak of PH4 and 16 B x (max LCP + 1) of spill (a^n: 155 MB at 10^7, 48 GB at 3x10^9); (f) **1b.7d** (PH1-PH3) is not touched by this package: the SA file it writes is the input here, and its comparator uses `MappedTextReader.Lce`, which clamps (the orchestrator measured `Lce` on random pairs at +19 % over the prototype in 1b.7b; the PLCP loop of this package, which calls it, is not slower than the prototype's, so no unchecked variant was built here); (g) NEXTSYM needs `SA[i]` and random text reads (17.8 ns per rank, about as much as the scan): a separate pass over the SA file, or the hook with a second consumer; (h) the owner's open items: none new; G5 is reported (+15.1 %, below the flag).
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default (3,153 passed + 8 skipped, 1 m 28 s), Exhaustive (73 of 73, 8 m 55 s) and Fuzz (32 of 32, 8 m 37 s) because `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed, the **checked build** `-p:EsaBounds=true` with `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true` (3,154 passed + 7 skipped, 1 m 26 s) because the new code uses `Buf<T>`, `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (672 of 672, always for 1b.7), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (505 of 505, 10 m 10 s; it holds the new differential tests), build of `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench`, all on the head `5ff27456` (only documents changed afterwards); **skipped, with the rule:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change); no widening trigger fired (a build, a test and a timing run were never run in parallel; the new tests start no child process). Residual risk as stated in the policy (couplings through data files, reflection or child processes are not visible to project references): the differential and cost tests read `/tmp/stbench` files that nobody changed; the explicit G5 test reaches private constructors of `EsaIndex`, `ChildTable` and `PackedChild` by reflection and is not part of a run, so a change of those constructors would break it without a red default run
- **Cost of 1b.7c (wall clock between the first message and the last commit; tokens are an estimate):** about 3.7 hours of wall clock in one implementer context with one compaction (the machine clock: 15:27 to about 19:10 UTC; the first commit is 15:47), of which about 40 minutes of measurement (G2 at 10^8 with `e8-verify`, G3 twice because the first harness did not time the prototype's disposal (16 minutes per full run), G5), the 298-mutant audit in four rounds (about 20 s per mutant plus the writing of the lists and the gap tests) and about 33 minutes of the affected-set tests, run once at the end (1.5 + 9 + 9 + 1.5 + 0.1 + 10 minutes of test time); tokens are an ESTIMATE of about 1.2 M by the convention of the earlier packages (the transcript is 4.7 MB, about 4 bytes per token; the session counter was not read); the transcript's usage records show 0.46 M generated tokens, 8.7 M freshly cached input tokens and 106 M cache reads, which is a different metric and is not what the cap of the brief (7 h, 1.8 M) means to count; the cap was not reached by time or by the convention above, the forecast (3-5 h, 0.8-1.2 M, +-50 %) was met in time and sits at the upper edge in tokens
- **Orchestrator verification of 1b.7c (2026-11-18):** pushed head `f625f679`, tree clean, scope as stated (ten new files in `External`, `MappedTextReader.cs` +7 lines, nothing else of `src`); affected set run by me: Esa default 3,153 + 8 skipped, Exhaustive 73, Fuzz 32, checked build 3,154 + 7 skipped, Persistent 672, EmProto 505 (Vectors, Ukkonen, Mcp.Core skipped by the rule); own G2 at 10^8 on `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`: SHA-256 of `.pk .sa .lcp .ovf .cld` equal to the E7 references, `.stats` equal, `e8-verify` 11 of 11; own G3 (4 threads, cold cache, 3 runs per cell): production / prototype 0.906 (dna, scaled geometry), 0.909 (genome), 0.971 (dna, R = 8 M), outputs equal in all 18 runs, RssAnon of the test host 154.8 MB against 155.0 MB at R = 8 M (gate 320 MB); own fault injection: a repeated entry, an entry out of range and a wrong `SA[0]` are refused without leftovers, **two adjacent entries swapped (a permutation in the wrong order) is accepted silently, the verifier's `sa_order` must catch it (1b.7e)**; mutation audit recounted (298 distinct, 271 killed, 27 equivalent, 0 open) and 13 own mutants killed 13 of 13; G5 re-run: Packed **+16 % per child step** against Tagged, whole queries 0 to +22 % within the noise, below the 25 % flag, the codec decision stands; details in ADR-004 section 5a after the 1b.7c subsection. - 2026-11-18 1b.7d [x] `90b35ce2` (code and tests as in the commits after `31e0f475` (tests first); G2 / G3 / G5 rows `dfa6fce3`, `18e22f4e`; audit rounds ``60c89b6f` (lists, runner), `eb92fe4a` (round 1a and its tests), `e7001fe4` (round 1s), `b4907522` (round 2, the heavy pass, the arguments)`; - **Choices made** (ADR-004 section 5a has the reasons; none is a public API, a file format or a behaviour verified in E3-E9): path-based stage, the suffix array created before PH1 (a full disk is found first); the rank file by `Dist.Scatter` with a skipping mapper (existing-file edit `DistOptions.Files`); the reduced strings by `MappedTextReader.OpenReduced` (existing-file edit); the failure semantics of 1b.7c (outputs, rank file, scratch subdirectories removed; the original exception type kept where the prototype wrapped it in `InvalidOperationException`); no static counters (per-store numbering, `ScratchLedger`, `IoCounters` per build, a tag per build on every scratch file); new checks (the inversion ranks every sample position, the length of the rank file, `VerifyRanks` as an option); the RssAnon gate is a verdict; the parameter rule is the one of E6 (`ScaleRules`, `ScaleN` / `RuleN`), the same periods and seeds, so the bytes are the prototype's; the clamped `Lce` in the comparator; no BWT.
