- **Not verified / limits.** any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (simulated platform and scalar path only); a text of more than 10^8 symbols (the 3x10^9 figures are ESTIMATES; E8 measured the prototype at that size); **the external levels of the recursion at the real geometry** (at 10^8 the reduced string has 3.2 M symbols and fits N_base = 16 Mi, so the real-geometry cells end in the base case; the external levels run in the tests, in the scaled geometry and in cells A, and the real geometry reaches them above about 5x10^8 symbols); the page-cache floor of PH1 (185 text scans) and of PH3 under a limited page cache; `SyncOutput` on a real device; a full disk at a real size (an injected reservation source only); a text between 2^31 and 2^32 - 4 symbols (a sparse file for the limit checks only); both strands (40-bit positions), resume, the BWT; the unreachable degenerate retry of the partition (dead code: no test reaches even one retry). Defects found: in the **prototype** (not fixed there, avoided or fixed in production): worker failures wrapped in `InvalidOperationException`; the `pos-*.bin` files of a nested sort carry no tag of the build, so a failed PH1 leaves them behind, and the rank file is not removed on a failure; static counters shared by two builds in one process; nothing checks that the inverted suffix array ranks every sample position; `EmBuilder.Partition` does not dispose its writers when a step fails; the `int` of the retry (`oversample *= 2`) can overflow after 26 retries (unreachable); one view per slice of K x 4 bytes (133 kB at the scaled geometry) where the E9 rule asks for windows of at least 1 MiB. In the **new code**: none in the logic. In the **tests and harnesses**: the first oracle of PH1 assumed names = ranks of X-prefixes where the prototype groups by the 64-bit key and the X-prefix; the G2 harness wrote a three-line `.pk.meta` where `e8-verify` reads seven; the first G3 harness counted the rank file twice in the prototype's PH1 scratch peak; a thread-count identity test took 2.5 minutes (now 30 s); two wrong assertions of the explicit exhaustive class (4,094 binary strings instead of 8,190, a depth assertion for strings that cannot recurse); and the audit showed 82 gaps of the tests in round 1, all closed. the 276 mutants of the second half of the lists, stage 2 of the audit and the individual re-run of the 14 kills by the watchdog or a crash; the cost of the construction of the Singer cover for X = 993 (0.28 s, a fixed cost of every build, not reduced here: it could be computed once or cached, open point).
- **Open points handed to 1b.7e:** (a) `SaBuilder.Build(text, saPath, options)` is path based and creates the suffix array itself; as a container section it needs an overload over a `MappedOutputFile` and a range (`MappedSaSliceSink` takes the file object already) or a copy (4 B/char of I/O); (b) one `IoCounters` for the whole build (`SaBuildOptions.Counters`, `LcpChildOptions.Counters`), the result carries `Io`, `Ph1Io`, `Ph23Io`, the arena and scratch peaks, the RssAnon peaks per phase and for the call, `Reservation`, `RecursionLevels`, `Parameters`; (c) the budgets are per phase (160 MiB for PH1, 160 MiB for PH2 / PH3, then 160 + 64 MiB for PH4 / PH5): the process peak is the largest stage, not the sum; (d) PSI / BWT / PH6: PH3 does not produce the BWT, the primary row has to come from the SA file or from PH3 (one number, not built); (e) the text must stay open for PH1-PH4; the scratch of PH1-PH3 is `ScratchDir/ph1`, `ph3` and `ranks.bin` (8 B/char at the peak of PH3), removed when done; (f) the verdict is the whole process; (g) the owner's items: none new; the construction of the Singer cover (0.28 s per build) could be cached; PH2 / PH3 and PH4 / PH5 dominate the 22 s at 10^8 as before.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** the affected set of `src/SuffixTree.Esa` (the only project of `src` that changed: 15 new files and two edited ones in `External/`, `apps/` is untouched), run on the head `90b35ce2` (only documents changed afterwards): **`SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default 3,305 passed + 8 skipped** (3,153 + 8 before, +152 new; 6 m 18 s in the Debug configuration), **Exhaustive 76 of 76** (Release, 9 m 49 s), **Fuzz 34 of 34** (Release, 8 m 42 s), **checked build** `-p:EsaBounds=true` with `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true` **3,306 passed + 7 skipped** (Release, 4 m 16 s), **`SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` 672 of 672**, **`SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` 513 of 513** (505 + the 8 differential tests; Release, 16 m 6 s), **build of `apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench` succeeds**; **skipped, with the rule:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the Mcp.Core tests (their projects, `SuffixTree.Core` and the shared props did not change); no widening trigger fired (a build, a test and a timing run were never run at the same time; the new tests start no child process); the first attempt at the explicit classes in the Debug configuration ran for 30 minutes without ending (the families at 10^6) and was stopped, the classes were run in Release as the test header says

### S1b 2026-11-19 work package 1b.8 (the full differential fuzz over the in-memory and the file-backed trees, the Dispose race, the container corruption fuzz and the shared-index test in `tests/SuffixTree/SuffixTree.Esa.Tests`; one implementer context)

- **Tests (red first, exact account).** The race (`d01d77ae`): the parent test **red** (the child process died with an `AccessViolationException`), run alone the four race tests of the child class: **three crashed the process** (`CompactLcp.MappedValue`, `LcpPyramid.Fill`, `SaView.get_Item`), the fourth passed; the fix made all five green. `SesaTraverseDamageTests` (`01985b65`): **4 of 6 red** (the repeated suffix-array entry), then the fix, then four index-error cases and the four sweeps (**3 of 4 sweeps red** before the LCP fix). The pyramid defect: the regression replay of the case was red (an `InvalidOperationException` from `LeftmostBelow`), then the fix. **The harness itself was written together with its tests (it is test code; there is no stub to be red against): its tests are the fault injections** (a fault of the file-backed tree at `Structure` / `Trusted` is found and minimized to `aba`, a fault of the in-memory tree to at most 3 symbols), the coverage test and its removal test, the smoke slice, the regression replay; and the 71 mutants measure its power.
- **Defects found (all by this package's own tests or harness; each fixed with a test; the abandon rule never applied).** *Production:* (1) the **Dispose race** (a crash: three of four race tests killed the process with `AccessViolationException`), fixed by the reader count; (2) a **suffix array that is in range but not a permutation** (sealed damage of the SA section, kind `SealedWords`) made `FindMaximalRepeatedPairs` over a `Checked` file append pairs until `OutOfMemoryException`: the mapped `Traverse` refuses a repeated or out-of-range position with `InvalidDataException` (the first version of the check indexed its bitmap with the unchecked value, an `IndexOutOfRangeException` that the fuzz found one run later); (3) an **LCP[1] other than 0** (and a negative LCP value) ended in an `IndexOutOfRangeException` of a managed array in `LeftmostOccurrence`: now `InvalidDataException`; (4) a **damaged LCP overflow list** made the LCP pyramid throw `InvalidOperationException` at query time: found by the **first 30-minute run** (master seed 1), fixed, the case is in the corpus. *Harness (mine):* a children buffer too small for a root of 1,300 children (twice), a wrapper that hid a null visitor, a quadratic MEM output on a Fibonacci word (35 M triples for one query, a case that ran for minutes), a header flip of the section count that the harness could not seal, NUnit recording the caught `Assert.Fail` of a worker as a failure of the test (the workers run without the execution context now), a default test whose draw of kinds of damage changed when the kinds grew (it failed in the first G6 run, fixed). *Existing tests:* none wrong; **no mismatch between the in-memory tree, the file-backed trees, the Ukkonen tree and the v6 tree was found in any run**, and the two old open points (the unmapping race, the `Checked` mode through the facade) are closed.
- **Counts.** Esa default **3,763 passed + 10 skipped (3,729 + 10 before; 10 m 33 s, Debug)** (34 new default tests: the race parent 1, the shared-index tests 5, the harness tests 10, the coverage-table tests 3, `SesaTraverseDamageTests` 15), Exhaustive **82 of 82 (82 before; 17 m 39 s, Release)**, Fuzz **39 of 39 (38 before; 14 m 43 s, Release; the new one is the 60-second default budget of the full differential fuzz)**, checked build **3,764 passed + 9 skipped (3,730 + 9 before; 7 m 5 s, Release)**. Explicit classes: the five race tests in the child process (category `DisposeRaceChild`, started by the parent test), the budgeted fuzz (G2: two runs of 1,800 s), the cost drivers `FacadeGuardCostTests` and `ReferenceBuildCostTests` (category Cost), the regeneration of the coverage list; the regression corpus has 13 new cases (the corruption fuzz) and replays through the full harness in a default test. **G5 reported (the run of seed 2, 4 workers per lane):** checks per second of an engine's own check time: memory-default 30.8, memory-packed 49.1, memory-reference3 52.8, the four file-backed engines 25.6-25.9, Ukkonen 27.6, v6 23.4 (a check is one text against one engine, the digest of every member); cases per second of a lane: tiny 31.5, small 8.4, large 0.96, xl 0.056, corruption 557 damaged files (908 cases including the skipped), concurrency 29.6 repetitions of 4 threads. **The v6 limit:** v6 and Ukkonen are compared at every n of every band up to 5 x 10^5 (the 1b.6 tests stopped at n = 150, the cost of an exhaustive definition check, not of v6); beyond it the cost is the limit (v6 heap build 1.28 s and 69 MB at 10^6, 6.2 s and 275 MB at 4 x 10^6, 14.9 s and 687 MB at 10^7; Ukkonen 0.74 s / 161 MB, 4.7 s / 483 MB, 12.4 s / 966 MB; rows `rows_1b8_dispose_cost.tsv`, section refbuild) times the three trees of a case in each of four workers. **Skipped texts:** 6,589 texts of the differential lanes have a code unit above 255 and no file-backed engine (4,973 tiny, 1,483 small, 132 large, 1 xl; the in-memory engines, Ukkonen and v6 still ran on them), 113,702 cases of the corruption lane (no container can be built) are counted as skipped. **Harness audit counts:** coverage list 71 members, 71 rows, every member called on the in-memory and, where it applies, on the file-backed tree (the factories and `EsaOpenOptions` only on one side by nature); mutants 71 listed, 71 run, 0 not run.
- **Choices made** (the smaller, safer course; none changes a public signature, an ADR or a verified behaviour of 1b.7 except the one named): (1) **the reader count does not wait** (a waiting `Dispose` would deadlock a `Dispose` from inside a visitor and would hold the thread that disposes); the release is done by whoever leaves last; (2) **the count is striped by thread** after a four-thread measurement of the single count (about 100 ns per call): eight counts of 64 bytes; (3) the lazy results are wrapped **for a file-backed tree only** (an in-memory tree has no mapping: its `Text` is the same `StringTextSource` as before, which the existing tests pin); (4) the container of a case is built by the product with the tiny parameters of the builder up to 300 symbols (they make the buckets, runs and windows of the build many) and with a difference cover of 31 / 57 / 133 above (the default period of 1,024 costs 285 ms of fixed time per build, 10-45 ms with these); PSI on or off by the case (the default is not changed); (5) **the corruption lane builds its files with the fast test writer** of 1b.7f (`SesaTestFile.Image`, byte-identical in layout to the builder's, which 1b.7f tested) because it needs thousands of containers per minute; (6) a text with a code unit above 255 has no file-backed engine and is counted; (7) **the open results are compared by property** (ADR-001 section 7): the representative of the longest repeat on a tie, the text position of the longest common substring and of the anchors, the order of `FindAllOccurrences`; (8) **the queries are short above 120 symbols** (at most 400, above 2,000 at most 150): the output of MEM is as long as the number of pairs of matches and is quadratic on a repetitive text (35 M triples for one query on a Fibonacci word of 74,000 symbols); (9) the checks run on workers **without the execution context of the test**, because NUnit records every `Assert.Fail` in the current test even when the harness catches it as data; (10) a lane starts a case only if the mean cost of its cases says it will end inside the budget; (11) the audit uses 60 s and two workers per mutant, a heap cap of 3 GB (a mutant can make a list grow without end), `ESA_FUZZ_MAXFAIL=1`; (12) the v6 tree is compared at **every n of every band**, to 5 x 10^5 (see G5).
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.8 and what was done (by the heading of the entry that wrote them):** **"Open points handed to 1b.7 / 1b.8"** (from 1b.6): `facade_member_coverage.tsv` (every member of the interfaces, now with the file-backed tree and `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` / `EsaOpenOptions`, derived by reflection, kept complete by a test): **done**; `FacadeChecks.CheckEverything` as the per-case check: **done, on every engine**; `FacadeConcurrencyTests` with checksums on a file-backed tree: **done** (`FacadeSharedIndexTests`, `FullFuzzConcurrency`); v6 compared only up to n = 150: **done, at every n to 5 x 10^5** (its build is 1.3 s at 10^6, 6 s at 4 x 10^6, 15 s and 690 MB at 10^7); the unmapping race of `Dispose` (1b.6 open point (b), "document it or add a reader count"): **done, a reader count**; the `Checked` mode through the facade (1b.6 (c)): **done by the corruption fuzz**; `PrintTree` needs the pack (1b.6 (d)): unchanged. **"Open points handed to 1b.8-1b.10 and S2"** (from 1b.7f): the harness over `SuffixIndexFactory.Open` trees (`Checked` and `Trusted`, `Header` and `Structure`) and the 4-thread test on a mapped tree: **done**; a `Dispose` during a read (limits of 1b.7f): **done, tested and fixed**; the `IncludePsi` default: **not mine** (the harness builds with and without PSI and reads it at `Structure`; no decision); a cold-cache query measurement, the throughput on `dna_100m`, the review of the `Checked` rules and of the `Structure` pass against ADR-003: open for 1b.9 / 1b.10.
- **Not verified / limits.** (a) Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the guard pages need Linux `mmap` / `mprotect`; elsewhere the corruption lane runs without them). (b) A damaged file in `Trusted` mode (it may crash by contract; the corruption fuzz reads damaged files in `Checked` mode only, and `Trusted` only after a file has passed every level). (c) **The cost of the reader count with several threads on one tree:** the medians of the difference between the facade call and the index call at four threads are 70-127 ns for the striped count (54-123 ns for the single count) against -49 .. +24 ns before the change, single batches between -250 and +330 ns: 3-6 % of a 20-mer count at 1.7-2.2 us, not resolved better and not reduced by the stripes; the single-thread cost (12.5-12.6 ns) is resolved, the contended one is not; 1b.9 should measure the throughput at 4 threads. (d) The one-tree-per-process protocol of 1b.7f cannot resolve 5 %: seven medians of five repeats spread 40 % on this machine (paired ratios 0.61-1.55). (e) The harness texts above 5 x 10^5 symbols (the largest case of a run was 5 x 10^5; the v6 and Ukkonen digests, not their builds, are the cost); the corruption lane works on texts of at most 4,000 symbols and on containers of the test writer. (f) The strict levels of the corruption fuzz are those that 1b.7f established (and the header fields the format fixes); a damage that no level sees and that keeps every answer (a flipped reserved byte of a section entry, a build-info byte, a padding bit) is accepted by design and counted as "opened, answers equal". (g) A race is a probability: 80 rounds of four readers against `Dispose` at random moments, and the ordering argument in the XML documentation of `Enter`; no model checker. (h) The mutants are single-site and hand-made, the engine only: the builder, PSI and the reader of `e8-verify` are outside this audit; W01 and the 11 argued equivalents rest on arguments (`1b8_equivalent.py`).
- **Open points handed to 1b.9-1b.10 and S2:** **1b.9:** the throughput of the file-backed and in-memory tree at four threads (the reader count is one flag read and two atomic updates on a private cache line per call); the cold open and the throughput on `dna_100m`. **1b.10:** a review of the reader-count protocol (the fence argument of `Enter` / `Leave` / `ReleaseWhenIdle`), of the three new refusals of the mapped `Traverse` and pyramid, and of the harness itself (the digest normalizations, the strict levels). **S2:** the harness over a `Structure` pass at scale (the corruption lane is small texts), a `Resume`, the readers of NEXTSYM / BUCKET / ISA / RMQ.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** the code did not change after `9a9ac926` (documents and rows only), and all four sets ran on it: **run:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default, Exhaustive, Fuzz and the checked build (`analysis/run_esa_checked.sh`: `-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) with the numbers under Counts (3,763 + 10 skipped, 82, 39, 3,764 + 9 skipped), **no result of a test that existed before changed**; the 30-minute budget run (G2) ran on `03287f14`, which differs from `9a9ac926` by two commits that touch only default-set tests (`FullFuzzTests.cs`, `FacadeSharedIndexTests.cs`) and rows files. **Two things went wrong in G6 and were handled:** the first default run (20:00 UTC, head `b4b27bb0`) failed one test of my own (`EveryKindOfDamage_IsDrawn`: the draw of the kinds of damage changed when the kinds of damage were added, two kinds were not in its 40-case slice; it now runs a corruption slice of its own) and I made the shared-index texts smaller (the default Esa run took 11 m 1 s, the shared-index tests about 74 s of it, now 39 s), then re-ran the default set on `9a9ac926`; **the container restarted at about 20:40 UTC during the Exhaustive run, so Exhaustive, Fuzz and the checked build were run again from 20:45 UTC on the same head (the default run and the 30-minute run were finished before the restart).** **Skipped, with reasons:** `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (no file of `SuffixTree.Persistent` or of its tests changed), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (no file of `apps/` or the shared builder changed), the Bench build (no csproj changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` / Ukkonen / `SuffixTree.Mcp.Core.Tests` / Vectors (`SuffixTree.Core` and `SuffixTree` untouched); no full-solution run.
- **Cost of 1b.8 (wall clock between the first message and the last commit; tokens are an estimate):** about **5.2 hours** of wall clock by the machine clock (16:17 to about 21:30 UTC), counted from the first message with the caps unchanged (7 hours or 2.0 M tokens): about 75 % of the time cap, a little above the forecast of 3-5 hours (the restart and the second default run of G6 cost about 30 minutes); **one container restart (about 20:40 UTC, during the Exhaustive run of G6; the sets after it were run again) and two context summaries** (the first at about 19:30 UTC, during the second 30-minute run; the second at the restart). Where the time went: orientation, the race test (red, `d01d77ae` 16:34) and the reader count (`9ed6257f` 17:06) about 50 minutes; the three defects of the damaged-file reads (17:06-17:17) and the first version of the harness (`4f745cc5` 17:17, the xl band and the argument probes `94af3451` 17:31) about 1.5 hours together with the cost measurements of the first version of the count (16:41 and 16:51: the protocol of the brief cannot resolve 5 %, an in-process instrument was written); **the mutation audit about 1 hour of machine time (17:33-18:36, four rounds, 71 mutants, with the harness extensions of rounds 2 and 3 written while the rounds ran)**; the striped count and its measurements (18:43-18:55); **the two 30-minute runs, 31 minutes each (18:55-19:27 and 19:28-19:59)**, the first of which found the defect of the LCP pyramid; **G6 about 1 hour 25 minutes** (20:00-21:26, including the failed first default run, its fix and a second default run of 11 minutes, and the about 15 minutes of the interrupted Exhaustive run lost to the restart); the documents were written in parallel with the runs, about 40 minutes. Tokens: an estimate of about **1.3 M** (no counter reaches the implementer; the usage records of the transcript sum to 0.49 M output tokens and 8.8 M tokens of context written, which is not what the cap counts), inside the forecast of 0.9-1.5 M and below the cap of 2.0 M; the 80 % rule (5.6 hours) did not fire.
- **Orchestrator verification of 1b.8 (2026-11-19):** pushed head `165467c8`, tree clean, scope as stated (six existing production files, +234/-63, no new production file; the harness and three Facade test classes; no csproj, `apps/`, `wiki/` or ADR-001/002/003 change); affected set run by me: Esa default 3,763 + 10 skipped, Exhaustive 82, Fuzz 39, checked build 3,764 + 9 skipped, Persistent 672, EmProto 533, Bench build clean; **the Dispose race reproduced independently: 62 of 150 processes crashed before the reader count (31bc0646), 0 of 150 after**, the cost of the count not resolvable at one thread (noise +-20 %) or four threads (+3.4 %); **own 30-minute run of the harness with master seed 3 on the idle machine: 336,314 cases, 0 failures**, 194,373 damaged-file cases (96,345 batteries between guard pages, 1,141,130 refusals as `InvalidDataException`), 28,880 thread runs with equal checksums; harness audit recounted (71 = 56 + 2 + 11 + 2 + 0 open) and **32 own random operator mutants of nine engine files: 21 killed, 7 equivalent, 4 small gaps of the harness** (section-end bound of a mapped child cell, tie-break of equal longest repeats, equality of the corrupt-leaf-depth guard twice); details in ADR-004 section 5a after the 1b.8 subsection. **Next: 1b.9a (the file-backed read path: find and fix the 29-37 % gap to memory, owner requirement of 2026-11-19), then 1b.9, 1b.11, 1b.10; manual approval before S2.**

### S1b 2026-11-20 work package 1b.9a (the file-backed read path: the cause of the 24-35 % gap to the in-memory tree and the fix, in `SuffixTree.Esa`; owner requirement of 2026-11-19; one implementer context)

- 2026-11-20 1b.9a [x] `eb97842c` (tests first `0176ba88` the descent, `2ad34830` CHILD codec 2; the specialised descent `34ef5a3b`, CHILD codec 2 `c940c097`; the harness `5d25ddec` and the commits after it; the audit `analysis/mutation/*1b9a*`; the rows `analysis/em/rows_1b9a_*.tsv`; this entry in the commit after them). **Outcome in one paragraph:** **The cause is found and fixed; the fixed gate is met; two reported items are not.** The file-backed tree was 24-35 % slower than the in-memory tree because of the **code of its read path** (the typed views `SaView` / `CompactLcp` / `MappedChild` / `MappedTextReader`, each with an array-or-pointer test, a checked-mode test and a switch on the LCP kind, passed by copy through the descent: **about 25 of the 26 points**) and, behind it, the **layout of the child table** (the container's `Packed` codec makes a descent touch 18 % more cache lines and 15 % more pages than the in-memory `Tagged` layout: about 4 points); **not** by the memory system (page size and TLB: 0.3-1.2 points; the packed text decode: 0-1 points when done inline, about 4 through the old reader; the `Checked` checks: 1.2-1.5 points). The fix is software and managed only: **`MappedSearch`** (the descent over raw pointers, one loop per combination of access mode, LCP width and CHILD codec, a 64-bit window compare against the packed pattern, the checks of `Checked` mode unchanged) and **CHILD codec 2** (the tagged layout in the container, now the builder's default; ADR-003 amendment). Result at 10 Mbp DNA, `Checked` against the in-memory tree: **0.5 / 3.1 / -1.2 % slower** (41 processes per variant; 0 / 1 / 3 % in the in-process A/B), 100 Mbp 2.3 / 3.7 / 4.0 %, five symbols 5.9 / 3.5 / 6.6 %, cold `Header` open 0.49 ms. **Not met: the protein-like text** (16-19 % by medians, 4-8 % in process; one cause of 1-3 points isolated, the rest not) **and the in-memory tree against `e4a0b2da`** (4-5 % slower, already so at `e57cdae0`; the reader count of 1b.8 is most of it).
- **Diagnosis (hypothesis by hypothesis; the table of the isolated effects, the instruments and the numbers are in ADR-004 section 5a "1b.9a").** In-process A/B, engine level, throughput relative to the in-memory tree, count 20-mers / locate 8-mers / absent 24-mers: the file as shipped **0.742 / 0.796 / 0.724** (24-35 % slower; the 29-37 % of 1b.7f at the facade level); **(4) checks:** the same file in `Trusted` 0.757 / 0.810 / 0.736 (1.2-1.5 points); **(2) packed text:** the text as a byte per symbol, 0.770 / 0.865 / 0.766 through the old reader (about 4 points) but **-1 / -1.4 / 0 points** when decoded inline (load and shift per symbol, the pattern packed the same way and compared in 64-bit windows); **(3) views and branches:** raw pointers over the same mapped arrays, byte text, no checks, still the packed child table: **1.025 / 1.054 / 1.004** (the 25 points); **(1) page size / TLB:** the same code over the mapped arrays 0.889 / 0.981 / 0.888, over resident pinned copies 0.895 / 0.978 / 0.876 (0.6, 0.3, 1.2 points; a dependent random load over 128 MB costs 146-169 ns mapped, 151-168 ns in a managed array, 148-169 ns in `NativeMemory`; managed arrays are not huge-page backed here, `AnonHugePages` 0 kB, nor is the mapping, `FilePmdMapped` 0 kB); **(5) access order:** a 20-mer count reads 127.8 values, 64.5 cache lines and 49.9 pages in the in-memory layout and 181.2 / 76.3 / 57.7 in the container as written (+18 % lines, +15.5 % pages, the extra from the LCP array that tells the kind of a packed cell), equal to the in-memory numbers with the tagged layout; a software prefetch of the next sibling was not resolved (+3.4 / -0.7 / +1.0 points); **(6) layout (found by 5, not on the list):** the packed child table in the in-memory engine 0.857 / 0.952 / 0.847, tagged in the lean mapped descent +4.0 % on count. Instruments: no `perf` (a VM without a PMU): a stopwatch harness with the JIT warmed by time, one tree per process or several in one process with rotated passes, `/proc/self/smaps_rollup`, a dependent-load micro-benchmark, and a replay of the descent that counts reads, 64-byte lines and 4 KB pages per query.
- **What was built.** *Production:* **new** `MappedSearch.cs` (333 lines: the descent over a container as one loop over raw pointers, specialised at compile time by access mode, LCP width and CHILD codec; the 64-bit window compare against the packed pattern; the checks of `Checked` mode); **edited** (16 existing files, +119 / -35; 11 under `src/SuffixTree/Algorithms/SuffixTree.Esa`, 5 under `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto`): `ChildLayouts.cs` (+26 / -6: `MappedChild` reads the tagged cell, `IsTagged`, `Pointer`), `ChildTable.cs` (+3 / -3: `FromMapped` reports the layout of the file), `EsaIndex.Search.cs` (+4 / -3: `MatchMapped` runs `MappedSearch`; `DescendMapped` stays as the reference), `EsaIndex.cs` (+3: the `MappedSearch` of a file-backed index), `External/MappedTextReader.cs` (+3: `Pointer`), `External/Sesa/SesaFormat.cs` (+6: the codec constants), `SesaHeaderReader.cs` (+14 / -2: `CheckChildCodec`), `SesaBuilder.cs` (+23 / -7: the option `ChildCodec`, `ChildCodecFor`, the `BUILD_INFO` text), `SectionLcpChildBuilder.cs` (+2 / -1: either codec), `SesaFile.cs` (+2 / -2), `SesaVerifier.cs` (+14 / -5: the `Structure` level compares the table in the layout of its codec), `apps/SuffixTree.EmProto` `Verify/OutputVerifier.cs` (+8 / -4), `Verify/VerifyModels.cs` (+3), `Verify/VerifySources.cs` (+5), `Experiments/E8.cs` (+2 / -1), `Cli.cs` (+1 / -1) (`e8-verify` takes the codec of a container from its header; `--child-codec packed|tagged` for a prefix run); no csproj changed. *Tests:* `Search/MappedSearchTests.cs` (new, 34 cases), `External/SesaChildCodecTests.cs` (new, 21 cases) and edits of 11 existing test files and `TEST_HIERARCHY.md` (the writer takes the codec as a parameter; the hostile tests, the corruption fuzz and the reader fuzz alternate codecs; the full differential harness builds one case in three with codec 1; five existing tests changed because the builder default changed, listed under Tests). *Harness and scripts (outside the shipped code):* `analysis/em/readbench/` (`ReadBench`: one tree per process, several trees or several builds in one process, `Experiments*.cs`), `run_1b9a_matrix.sh`, `run_1b9a_gate.sh`, `summarize_1b9a.py`, the rows `rows_1b9a_*.tsv`; the mutation audit `analysis/mutation/make_1b9a_mutants.py`, `run_1b9a_mutants.py`, `run_1b9a_audit.sh`, `1b9a_*_listed.json`, `1b9a_equivalent.py`, `summarize_1b9a.py`, `summary_1b9a.tsv`, `results_1b9a_*.tsv`. *Documents:* ADR-003 (amendment of 2026-11-19: CHILD codec 2), ADR-004 section 5a "1b.9a", this entry, the Overview row, the plan row, `tests/SuffixTree/TEST_HIERARCHY.md`.
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** The descent (`0176ba88`): `MappedSearchTests` (30 cases) written against a stub of `MappedSearch` that throws: **30 of 30 red**; the implementation (`34ef5a3b`) made them green at once, and a first run of the 3,809 neighbours (the reader, the facade, the factory classes: 2,257 passed, 5 skipped) did not change. CHILD codec 2 (`2ad34830`): the tests and the stubs (the codec constants, the builder option, `ChildCodecFor` and `CheckChildCodec` that throw, a `MappedChild` that never reports the tagged codec, `IVerifySource.ChildTagged` that is null): **76 of 3,824 Esa default tests red** (the new `SesaChildCodecTests`, the codec loops of `MappedSearchTests`, the hostile tests on a tagged image, and the builder tests whose expectation is the tagged default) and **1 of 19 `ContainerVerifyTests` red**; the implementation (`c940c097`) made 71 of the 76 green at once and left **5 existing tests that asserted what the builder wrote before** (the codec of the CHILD entry in `SesaAuditTests`, the BUILD_INFO text, the counted mapped writes of two builds, the pinned `Stable` figures of a text with an overflow list, and `AnUnknownCodecOfARequiredSection`, which named codec 2 as the unknown one): changed to the new default, each with the reason in the commit message; no other existing test changed its result. **Tests that were added after the code:** the stage test for a text of 2^30 symbols (`ebcb48fe`, a gap that the review of the checks showed; green when written) and the tests of the first audit round (`58a27162`): `AnLcpValueAtTheEdgeOfItsRange_InCheckedMode_EndsAsTheReferenceDoes` (2 cases), `ACellOutsideTheChildTable_InCheckedMode_IsInvalidData_ForTheNextLIndexOfBothLayouts` (2 cases) and the text `a^70000` in `EveryRepresentationOfTheLcpArray_EqualsTheMemoryIndex`: each was written for a mutant that survived the unit tests and the budgeted harness, was green on the head and killed the mutant in round 2.
- **Counts.** Final head: `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default (Debug) **3,819 passed + 10 skipped (3,829**: 3,824 before the stage test and the four cases of the audit tests); Fuzz **39 of 39** (Release, 14 m 23 s); the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`) **3,820 passed + 9 skipped** (7 m 15 s); on `c940c097`, whose production code is that of the final head: Exhaustive **82 of 82**, `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` **535 of 535**, the Bench build clean; the 10-minute budget run of the 1b.8 harness: master seed 4, 600 s, 4 workers, head `39fc4739`: **117904 cases in 905 s (the test process: 907 s), 0 failures** (tiny 4790 cases, small 1394 cases, large 111 cases, xl 5 cases, corruption 111549 cases, concurrency 55 cases); a first run on `39fc4739` stopped at the 300 s watchdog of the xl band on a slow case (`akb`, 478,991 symbols), which finishes at both `e57cdae0` and the head when the xl lane runs alone (2 workers, 20 cases and 0 failures each, `rows_1b9a_fuzz_xl_compare.txt`): a slow case of the harness, not a defect of this package; the limit is now a parameter of `run_1b8_fuzz.sh` (`FUZZ_HANG_S`, 900 s for this run). New tests: `MappedSearchTests` 34 cases, `SesaChildCodecTests` 21 cases; 11 existing test files edited. Mutation audit: 67 listed, 67 run, 0 not run: 54 killed by the unit tests in round 1, 5 in round 2, 8 argued equivalent, 0 open. Gate rows: 41 processes per variant at 10 Mbp DNA, 21 for the five-symbol and the protein-like text, 22 at 100 Mbp (memory and `Checked`), 15 per build for the in-memory comparison, the in-process A/B 6 + 3 + 3 + 6 processes (+ 5 for the builds), 10 cold opens. Production: 1 new file (333 lines) and 16 edited (+119 / -35).
- **Choices made** (the smaller, safer course; none changes a public signature; the one change of a verified behaviour is the default of the builder, named first): (1) **The default of the builder is now CHILD codec 2** (tagged) for a text of at most 2^30 - 1 symbols and codec 1 above it; `SesaBuildOptions.ChildCodec` chooses the other one; the reader reads both, so every file of 1b.7e / 1b.7f stays valid; the amendment of ADR-003 is a default taken by the orchestrator under the owner's requirement and is reversible (the option and the reader of codec 1 stay). The measured reason: the packed layout makes the descent touch 18 % more cache lines and 15 % more pages, and the lean mapped descent is 4 % faster with the tagged one; a format change was the second choice after the code-only fix, taken because the first alone left count / absent 9-10 % under the in-memory tree in the in-process A/B, not for margin on the gate (which the first alone met in separate processes). (2) **`EsaIndex.DescendMapped` stays** as the readable reference of `MappedSearch` (the tests compare the two on valid and damaged files) instead of being deleted. (3) **Twelve loops by struct type arguments**, each instantiated on first use, instead of one loop with runtime flags: a flag that is the same for a whole file costs a branch per read and the data show what that is worth (point 3 of the diagnosis). (4) **The packed pattern is built lazily**, when the first stretch needs a comparison (a pattern that fails at the first levels never pays for it). (5) **No resident open mode, no unpacked TEXT section, no prefetch, no `madvise`** (the data do not justify the first three; the fourth is the rule of the project). (6) **The test writer keeps `Packed` as its default** (`SesaTestFile.Image(..., codec)`) so that the hundreds of tests that damage cells at specific offsets keep their meaning; the new and the parametrised tests name the codec; the corruption fuzz takes it from a bit of the damage seed that its draws do not use (the regression corpus keeps its damage), the reader fuzz alternates it, the full differential harness builds one case in three with codec 1. (7) **`e8-verify` takes the codec of a container from its header**; for a prefix run `--child-codec packed|tagged` (default packed, unchanged). (8) **The isolated effects are measured in one process** (the in-process A/B of `ReadBench`), the gate in separate processes with the order rotated, because the first resolves 1-2 % and the second 15 % per process; both are reported. (9) **The experiments of the diagnosis stay in `analysis/em/readbench`** (compiled only with `-p:Experiments=true`; they reach internals through the friend assembly name `SuffixTree.Esa.Bench`, and the raw ones read codec 1, so they run on a container of 1b.7f). (10) The `madvise` experiment is **outside the repository** (a P/Invoke copy of the harness in `/tmp`); only its number is recorded. (11) **Two throw-away builds of the head were measured and not shipped** (the 16-bit symbol read, +1-3 points on the protein-like text; no reader count for in-memory trees, +3-4 points in memory): either one is a change of the hot code after the sets and the audit had run on it, and the second changes a verified contract of 1b.8; both are items for 1b.9. (12) The audit lists **67 mutants** of the new and the changed code, and two of them were re-written after the first round (see the audit paragraph).
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.9a and what was done (by the heading of the entry that wrote them):** **"Open points handed to 1b.8-1b.10 and S2" (from 1b.7f):** the throughput on `dna_100m`: **done** (22 processes per variant and the in-process A/B); the cold-cache **query** measurement: **not done** (the cold open was measured, 0.49 ms; the queries are warm by the protocol); `Checked` against `Trusted` "no difference that five medians resolve": **explained** (the checks cost 1.2-1.5 points in process, below the spread between processes); the 1b.7f flag "**throughput of the file-backed tree more than 25 % slower than in memory**": **done, cause found and fixed** (this entry). **"Open points handed to 1b.9-1b.10 and S2" (from 1b.8):** the throughput at four threads: **open** (nothing here changes the reader count; the in-memory part of its cost is measured below); the cold open and the throughput on `dna_100m`: **done**; the limit (d) of 1b.8 "the one-tree-per-process protocol cannot resolve 5 %": **addressed** by the in-process A/B (1-2 %) and by 41 processes per variant at 10 Mbp. **CHILD `Tagged` has no codec id in format 1.0** (from 1b.7e): **done, codec 2**.
- **Not verified / limits.** (a) Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64: the window decode (`ReverseEndianness`, an unaligned 64-bit load, a byte read behind it) is plain managed code and was run on x86-64 only; the guard-page tests need Linux. (b) **The separate-process spread is 25 % on this machine and bimodal** (the processes of one variant fall into a group near 700-730 thousand count/s and a group near 810-930, whichever the CPU: pinned runs of the same variant gave 568-841); a median of 41 processes has a standard error of about 4 % (the 95 % interval of the slowdown is about -13 .. +13 points), so the verdict by medians can move by more than the effects of this package; the in-process A/B is the precise instrument and is reported next to it. (c) A text of 2^30 symbols or more with codec 2 is refused by the header check and by the builder; the **checks** were run on the functions (`CheckChildCodec`, `ChildCodecFor`, the stage on a sparse text of 2^30 symbols), not on a container of that length. (d) `e8-verify` and the `Structure` level of codec 2 were run at 10^8 (rows), not at 3x10^9. (e) The measurement of 100 Mbp used the scaled geometry of the builder (E6 parameters); the process with the in-memory tree at 100 Mbp had 1.52 GB resident. (f) No test of a read-ahead or a cold page cache for the queries (the throughput is warm by the protocol). (g) The `madvise` number is one throw-away experiment on this kernel (6.18.44, THP `madvise`, no `CONFIG_READ_ONLY_THP_FOR_FS`, ext4) and says nothing about another one. (h) The mutants are single-site and hand-made, for the code of this package only. (i) **The protein-like text is not within 10 %** by the medians (and the cause is only partly isolated), and **the in-memory tree is 4-5 % slower than at `e4a0b2da`** (not caused here): both are stated in the outcome; neither is claimed. (j) The throughput at four threads was not measured.
- **Open points handed to 1b.9 and to the owner:** **1b.9:** the S1 gate numbers are to be measured on the tagged default (the builder writes codec 2; the container is 4.0 B/char for the child table as before); the throughput at four threads; the cold-cache query measurement; **the decision about the default (codec 2) is the owner's and reversible**; **(1) the protein-like text:** the 16-bit single-symbol read (six lines, +1-3 points) and the packing of the pattern, then a re-run of the gate on it; **(2) the in-memory tree:** the reader count of 1b.8 costs 3-4 points of an in-memory search (the measured copy skips it for in-memory trees only); either keep the count for the file-backed tree only and say so in the contract, or reduce its cost (one flag read and two atomic updates per call today); the rest of the 4-5 % (0-2 points) is not isolated. **For the owner:** `madvise(MADV_HUGEPAGE)` through P/Invoke touches the rule "managed only": measured outside the shipped code on the mapped container, **it has no effect on this kernel** (0.986 / 1.004 / 0.978; the kernel does not map huge pages for a read-only file mapping: `THPeligible` 0, no `CONFIG_READ_ONLY_THP_FOR_FS`), so no decision about the rule is needed for it. **For S2 / later:** a section that interleaves the suffix array and the child table (the descent reads `SA[a]` and `CLD[a]` at the same ranks: 38 of the 64 cache lines of a 20-mer count are these two arrays; interleaved they would share lines) would cut the lines per query further; it needs a new section and a reader and is not measured; the same replay (`ExperimentsCount`) is the tool to estimate it first.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** **the production code of the final head is that of `c940c097`** (`git diff c940c097..HEAD -- src apps` is empty); **run on the final head:** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default in Debug (**3,819 passed + 10 skipped**, 10 m 17 s), Fuzz (**39 of 39**, Release, 14 m 23 s), the checked build (**3,820 passed + 9 skipped**, 7 m 15 s), the 10-minute budget run of the 1b.8 harness (master seed 4, 600 s, 4 workers, head `39fc4739`: **117904 cases in 905 s (the test process: 907 s), 0 failures** (tiny 4790 cases, small 1394 cases, large 111 cases, xl 5 cases, corruption 111549 cases, concurrency 55 cases); a first run on `39fc4739` stopped at the 300 s watchdog of the xl band on a slow case (`akb`, 478,991 symbols), which finishes at both `e57cdae0` and the head when the xl lane runs alone (2 workers, 20 cases and 0 failures each, `rows_1b9a_fuzz_xl_compare.txt`): a slow case of the harness, not a defect of this package; the limit is now a parameter of `run_1b8_fuzz.sh` (`FUZZ_HANG_S`, 900 s for this run)); **run on `c940c097` and not repeated** (the production code is the same and no test of these sets changed since): Exhaustive (**82 of 82**, 17 m 25 s, Release), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (**535 of 535**, 15 m 44 s: the container writer and `apps/` changed), the Bench build (builds). **Skipped, with reasons:** `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (no file of `SuffixTree.Persistent` or of its tests changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` / Ukkonen / `SuffixTree.Mcp.Core.Tests` / Vectors (`SuffixTree.Core` and `SuffixTree` untouched); no full-solution run.
- **Cost of 1b.9a (wall clock between the first message and the last commit; tokens are an estimate):** about **6.4 hours** of wall clock by the machine clock (23:29 UTC on 2026-11-19 to about 05:55 UTC on 2026-11-20), counted from the first message with the caps unchanged (8 hours or 2.2 M tokens), against a forecast of 3-6 hours and 0.9-1.6 M tokens; the 80 % rule (6.4 hours) fired when the documents were being written: every code, test, audit and run result was committed and pushed (`eb97842c`) before that, so nothing but the documents was left; by the token reckoning below, the 80 % mark of the token cap (1.76 M) was passed at about the same time. **One context summary** (at about 03:00 UTC, after the diagnosis and the gate matrices of 10 Mbp). Where the time went: orientation and the first isolated experiments about 1 hour; the specialised descent and its tests (`0176ba88` 00:07, `34ef5a3b` 00:28) about 1 hour; the measurement harness and the diagnosis table (the in-process A/B, the replay counter, the page-size controls) about 1 hour 15 minutes; CHILD codec 2 (ADR-003 amendment, reader, builder, verifier, `e8-verify`, tests first `2ad34830` 00:55, `c940c097` 01:07) about 1 hour; the affected sets (default 10 m, Exhaustive 17 m, Fuzz 14 m, checked 7 m, EmProto 16 m) about 1 hour of machine time; the gate matrices (41 + 21 + 21 processes at 10 Mbp, 22 at 100 Mbp, the in-process pairs, the open cost, the in-memory builds) and the throw-away experiments about 1 hour 15 minutes; the mutation audit about 40 minutes of machine time (two worktrees; one second round of 5 mutants), the second default / Fuzz / checked runs and the 10-minute harness run (with the slow xl case) about 1 hour 15 minutes; the documents were written while the runs went on. Tokens: an estimate of about **1.9 M** (no counter reaches the implementer; the usage records of the transcript sum to the output tokens and the context written given below, which is not what the cap counts; the same reckoning as 1b.8: the output tokens plus a tenth of the context written), above the forecast of 0.9-1.6 M (the output of this package is 1.1 M tokens: the diagnosis, the measurement harness and long documents) and below the cap of 2.2 M; the usage records of the transcript sum to 1.1 M output tokens and 9.2 M tokens of context written.

### S1b 2026-11-20 work package 1b.9b (the two instruments reconciled, the in-memory tree restored, ten test gaps closed, the reader count at four threads, in `SuffixTree.Esa` and its tests; owner rule "no debts before S2"; one implementer context, one container restart)

- 2026-11-20 1b.9b [x] `60e5064f` (tests first `d39c158b`, the single-symbol read and the harness `60e5064f`, the audit `analysis/mutation/*1b9b*`, the rows `analysis/em/rows_1b9b_*.tsv`; this entry in the commit after them). **Outcome in one paragraph:** **The reconciliation, the in-memory restoration and the test gaps are done; one gate (G3) is not met.** (1) *The instruments:* the cross-process instrument cannot resolve what it was asked to: the throughput of a process falls into one of two groups 1.4x apart whatever the variant, the round or the tiering, so a ratio of two medians of 21 processes moves by 5-15 % for the same code (the same build against itself: 0.995 / 1.050 / 0.978, interval 0.74-1.39); the in-process instrument (+-3 points) puts the file-backed `Checked` tree at **0.91-1.06 of the in-memory tree on every one of the six texts** (DNA, 5 symbols, uniform and realistic protein, 90 symbols, 256 bytes; count / locate / absent), the realistic protein at 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971 after the single-symbol read of the mapped descent became one 16-bit load (+4 points), and at 100 Mbp the file-backed tree is the faster one (1.09 / 1.00 / 1.07); the cross-process medians stay 8-16 % below 1 on DNA, 5 symbols and 90 symbols with intervals that contain 1 (paired and stratified estimates 0.88-1.02): unresolved, with the cause isolated; the orchestrator's protocol reproduces the direction of its "file faster" reading by medians (1.17 / 1.09 / 1.30) and removes it by pairing (0.97-0.99); its 2.19 is not reproduced. (2) *The in-memory tree:* **0.990-1.010 of `e4a0b2da`** in process (12 processes, DNA and realistic protein; the head before was 0.96-0.99): the reader count is gone for in-memory trees, **because the race was real and had another cause** (a test written first crashes the host against the behaviour of `e4a0b2da`: `Dispose` freed the pins of the navigation pack under a reader) and `Dispose` of an in-memory tree now releases nothing (the pins go with the finalizer); the other points were the LCP indexer that no longer inlined (+1 point). The specialised pointer descent is **not adopted** for the in-memory tree (-0.2 to +5.4 points against the generic one on all six texts; the packed text copy loses). (3) Ten test gaps (`B102`, `B118`, `VE7`, `VE9`, `VE12`, the cell bound, the tie-break, the leaf-depth guard twice, `S03`) have tests, each with named mutants; the harness compares the longest-repeat representative between the two ESA engines and has a new damage kind for the leaf-depth equality. (4) *Four threads:* the in-memory facade costs nothing; **the reader count of the file-backed tree costs 7.6 % of a DNA count (gate 5 %: not met), 3.6 % on the realistic protein, 0-4 % on locate and absent**: recorded with its cause (a fence per call that serialises the overlap of queries) and its remedy (an asymmetric protocol) for 1b.10 or the owner. (5) Hygiene done (47 sidecars folded, the binary TSV fixed at its writers, `ReadBench.csproj` not in the solution). Gates: **G1 PASS, G2 PASS in process and unresolved with an isolated cause cross-process, G3 FAIL (one number), G4 PASS, G5 PASS, G6 PASS.**
- **Instrument reconciliation (ADR-004 section 5a "1b.9b" has the definition and the isolated experiments).** File-backed `Checked` / in-memory throughput, count / locate / absent; cross-process = 21 processes per variant, ratio of medians (paired by round; mode-stratified, exploratory); in process = 5 processes of 9 passes, median of the per-pass ratio:

| Text | cross-process | in process (`Checked`; `Trusted`) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNA (4) | 0.917 / 0.878 / 0.920 (paired 0.943 / 0.954 / 0.979; stratified 0.947 / 0.965 / 0.931; intervals 0.77-1.11) | 0.964 / 0.961 / 0.932 (`Trusted` 1.023 / 0.993 / 0.972) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 8-12 % below 1, intervals contain 1, stratified 0.93-0.97: unresolved by that instrument |
| 5 symbols | 0.842 / 0.863 / 0.869 (0.882 / 0.922 / 0.893; 0.951 / 1.018 / 0.950; 0.77-1.14) | 0.975 / 0.959 / 0.969 (1.038 / 1.012 / 1.026) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 13-16 % below 1, intervals contain 1, stratified 0.95-1.02: unresolved |
| protein uniform (20) | 1.014 / 1.124 / 1.093 (0.958 / 0.956 / 0.956; 0.955 / 0.968 / 0.961; 0.78-1.26) | 0.996 / 0.918 / 0.908 (0.968 / 0.941 / 0.928) | within 10 % on both (the smallest in-process figure of the table, 0.908) |
| protein realistic (20) | 1.072 / 1.100 / 1.101 (1.026 / 1.034 / 0.997; 1.053 / 0.959 / 1.112; 0.91-1.15) | 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971 (1.002 / 0.962 / 0.940) | within 10 % on both (the file-backed tree is the faster one in this cross-process run: the same noise) |
| text (90) | 0.860 / 0.871 / 0.945 (0.978 / 0.961 / 0.962; 0.959 / 0.965 / 0.984; 0.74-1.18) | 0.997 / 0.967 / 0.946 (1.009 / 0.977 / 0.957) | in process within 10 %; cross-process medians 13-14 % below 1 on count and locate, paired 0.96-0.98, interval contains 1: unresolved |
| bytes (256) | 1.008 / 0.936 / 0.953 (1.017 / 1.014 / 1.025; 1.044 / 1.042 / 1.025; 0.80-1.20) | 1.043 / 1.063 / 1.040 (1.009 / 1.013 / 1.013) | within 10 % on both; the file-backed tree is the faster one |

  **Why they disagree:** the throughput of a cross-process run falls into one of two groups about 1.4x apart whatever the variant, the round or the CPU, so a ratio of two medians of 21 processes moves by +-10-15 % for the same code (the same build against itself, DNA, 21 processes: **0.995 / 1.050 / 0.978**, interval 0.74-1.39; the per-round ratios of two variants range over 0.66-1.50); the in-process instrument (+-3 points) is the one that decides. The numbers that disagreed earlier (1b.9a: protein-like text 16-19 % slower; the orchestrator: 1.33 on DNA and **2.19** on the uniform protein) are inside that floor except the 2.19, which no mix of the two groups gives (their in-memory DNA throughput was 30 % below the lower group seen here: another machine state; **not reproduced, not isolated**). The text width is not the cause (the in-memory search reads a byte per symbol; a byte copy or a packed copy in front of the specialised descent changes nothing or loses); the trees sharing a process do not bias the in-process figure (it agrees with the stratified cross-process estimate within 3 points). The single-symbol read of the mapped descent (one 16-bit load) is **+4 points on the realistic protein** (0.902 / 0.927 / 0.935 before, 0.945 / 0.970 / 0.971 after). 100 Mbp DNA: `Checked` / in memory in process (4 processes) **1.09 / 1.00 / 1.07**, cross-process (7 processes) 1.25 / 1.24 / 1.19 by medians (paired 1.11 / 1.01 / 1.05): the file-backed tree is not slower than the in-memory tree at 100 Mbp.
- **What was built.** *Production (5 existing files, +66 / -19, none new):* `EsaSuffixTree.cs` (+19 / -7: an in-memory tree takes no reader count and its `Dispose` releases nothing, the scope of a call still holds the tree; the remarks and the summary of `Dispose` say so), `CompactLcp.cs` (+20 / -9: the slow arm of the indexer and the range error are out of line, so that the indexer is inlined into the descent again), `External/Sesa/SesaBuilder.cs` (+8 / -1: `CheckBuildInfoFits`), `Memory/NativeArena.cs` (+9: `GuardedCreated`), `MappedSearch.cs` (+10 / -2: `Symbol` is one 16-bit load). *Tests (new):* `External/SesaGap1b9bTests.cs`, `External/SesaReaderGap1b9bTests.cs`, `Facade/FacadeLongestRepeatTieTests.cs`, the in-memory race test in `Facade/FacadeDisposeRaceTests.cs`; edited: `FacadeLifetimeTests.cs` (the two release tests), `Fuzz/FullFuzzCheck.cs` + `FuzzDigest.cs` (the `lrs-representative` line and `LrsRuleViolation`), `Fuzz/CorruptionFuzz.cs` (the damage kind `SealedLeafDepth`), `External/SesaReaderGapTests.cs` (`LcpSections` internal), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests/External/SesaBuildCostTests.cs` (the CRC rows go into the rows file). *Analysis:* `analysis/em/readbench/` (`Generate.cs` the six texts, `Program.cs` the pattern lengths, `@text` alphabets, the four-thread instrument `mt:`, the engine-level prototypes `ix-mem-b` / `ix-mem-p` in `ExperimentsRaw.cs`, `README.md` with the benchmark definition), `run_1b9b_bench.sh`, `summarize_1b9b.py`, `analysis/mutation/{make,run,summarize}_1b9b_*.py`, `1b9b_equivalent.py`.
- **Tests (red first, exact account).** The in-memory race test was written first and run in three states (worktree): at the head (reader count for every tree) **7 of 7 child tests green, twice**; against the behaviour of `e4a0b2da` (no count, `Dispose` releases the pack at once) **the test host crashed in 3 of 3 runs** (the stress test; the five older child tests passed before it); with the fix **green** (every run). The deterministic variant never failed against the old behaviour and was dropped. The gap tests were written against the head, where they are green by construction (each closes a missing direction of an existing check); that they fail against a mutant is the audit: 43 mutants listed, 43 run, 0 not run: 39 killed in round 1, 2 in round 2 (two tests added for the survivors `A03` and `F05`: a non-atomic guard counter, and a caller on stripe 0 of the file-backed reader count that never left it, a real gap of 1b.8), 2 argued equivalent (`B06`, `F04`), 0 open. A harness barrier that waited for a thread that never came (a test bug of my own) hung the first run of the child tests for 10 minutes; found at once, fixed.
- **Counts.** **New tests:** `SesaGap1b9bTests` (14 cases), `SesaReaderGap1b9bTests` (18 cases), `FacadeLongestRepeatTieTests` (28 cases), the in-memory race test (explicit, in the child process), one new damage kind of the corruption lane and the `lrs-representative` / `lrs-rule` checks of the harness; 2 tests of 1b.6 rewritten; **+60 tests in the default run (59 at the run of the sets, one added for the survivor `1b9b_A03`)**, the numbers of the sets are in "Test sets run and skipped". Benchmark and analysis: 45 rows files `rows_1b9b_*.tsv`, 6 generators, the four-thread instrument, the prototypes of the in-memory descent. Mutation audit: 43 listed, 43 run, 0 not run; 41 killed (39 + 2), 2 argued equivalent, 0 open. Production: 5 existing files edited (+66 / -19), none new.
- **Choices made** (the smaller, safer course; the one change of a verified behaviour is named first): (1) **No reader count for an in-memory tree, and `Dispose` of it releases nothing**, rather than a cheaper count: once nothing that a reader uses is released, a count of any kind is cost without a purpose (3-4 points); this changes one behaviour of 1b.6 (two tests rewritten, named in ADR-004) and the contract statements stay (a call in flight completes, a later call throws). (2) **The specialised descent is not adopted for the in-memory tree** (the rule of the brief: 10 % on count and absent for every text; measured -0.2 to +5.4 points); no production code was written for it, the prototype stays in `analysis/em/readbench` (`ix-mem-b`, `ix-mem-p`, `-p:Experiments=true`). (3) **The tie-break stays implementation-specific by contract** (ADR-001 section 7 is not changed, so no ADR-001 edit); the two ESA engines keep one rule, the lexicographically smallest tied substring, which the harness now compares between them and with the rule; Ukkonen and v6 are not held to it. (4) **Two seams in production code for tests** (a process-wide counter of guarded arenas; the BUILD_INFO room check as a function) instead of reflection or a copy of the builder. (5) **`S03` is tested at the reader struct**, because at the descent the same value is refused a statement later by the check of the child depth (the argument of `1b9a_M40` stays true for the descent). (6) **A new damage kind in the corruption lane**, appended at the end of the enumeration, which changes the kind drawn for a given master seed (the regression corpus names its kind and is unaffected; the numbers of earlier harness runs are not reproducible by seed). (7) **The 16-bit single-symbol read** of the mapped descent, which 1b.9a measured and did not ship, is shipped (a change of the hot code of the file-backed tree, covered by the existing read-path tests and the harness, four mutants added). (8) **The CRC sidecar rows are comment lines** (`#crc`) behind the row of their run. (9) **The cross-process instrument is reported with its noise floor** (the null experiment, the interval, the paired median, and a mode-stratified estimate marked exploratory); the in-process instrument decides where the two disagree. (10) **Cross-process rows for four texts are those of the build before this package (`433195b6`, 21 processes each)**, the in-memory change being far below the resolution of that instrument; DNA and the realistic protein were re-run on the final build. (11) **The reader count of the file-backed tree was not redesigned** (the asymmetric protocol needs a proof and a re-run of the 1b.8 race tests; recorded as the one gate not met). (12) **The deterministic variant of the in-memory race test was dropped** because it never failed against the old behaviour. (13) **The jit experiment ran with 8 processes per setting** (it was cut from 12 for time) and the 4-thread DNA run was extended to 6 processes instead.
- **Earlier open points that concern 1b.9b and what was done (by the heading of the entry that wrote them):** **1b.9a "Open points handed to 1b.9 and to the owner": (1) the protein-like text:** the 16-bit read **done** (+4 points), the packing of the pattern **not done** (it is built lazily; not measured), the gate re-run on all six texts in process: **no text beyond 10 %**; **(2) the in-memory tree:** the count for in-memory trees **removed and its cause fixed** (the pins), the other 0-2 points **found** (the LCP indexer no longer inlined), the paired run against `e4a0b2da` **0.980-1.008** (see Not verified); **the throughput at four threads: done** (below); **"does not reproduce the implementer's protein-like result and is not explained" (orchestrator verification of 1b.9a):** explained as noise (null experiment), the 2.19 not reproduced. **1b.9a "Not verified" (i), (j):** (i) the protein-like text is within 10 % in process; the in-memory tree is back at `e4a0b2da`; (j) the four-thread measurement is made. **1b.7e `B102`, `B118`; 1b.7f `VE7`, `VE9`, `VE12`; 1b.8 four (the bound of a mapped child cell, the tie-break, the leaf-depth guard twice); 1b.9a `S03`:** the ten tests (table in ADR-004). **1b.8 "limit (d): the one-tree-per-process protocol cannot resolve 5 %":** shown by the null experiment (the floor is +-5-10 % on the point estimate); the in-process instrument resolves 3 points. **Hygiene items of the orchestrator (47 sidecars, a binary TSV, `ReadBench.csproj` under `docs`):** done; the commit title with a colon stays.
- **Defects found.** **One, old and real: `Dispose` of an in-memory tree freed the GC handles of the navigation pack while a reader could hold pointers into the pinned arrays** (since 1b.6; masked, by accident, by the reader count of 1b.8, which covered in-memory trees too); fixed at the cause. No other test closed a gap by finding a defect: the ten tests are green on the head.
- **Not verified / limits.** (a) Any run on macOS, Windows or arm64 (the finalizer-based release of the pins and the process-wide behaviour of the collector were run on x86-64 Linux only). (b) **The in-memory race test is a stress test**: it crashed the host in 3 of 3 runs against the old behaviour and passed in every run of the head and of the fix (about 20 runs), which is evidence of detection power and not a proof of absence; a window that the collector does not hit is not tested. (c) **The cross-process instrument cannot resolve what the gates ask of it**: its 95 % intervals are +-25-35 % and the null experiment (the same build twice) moves the ratio of medians by 2-5 % and the per-round ratio by 0.66-1.50; the gates are decided by the in-process instrument (+-3 points, 5 processes); the cross-process rows of four of the six texts are those of the build before this package (21 processes each), and for DNA, five symbols and 90 symbols the medians stay 8-16 % below 1 with intervals that contain 1; no number of processes that fits a session would change that (the stratified estimate, which is exploratory, says 0.93-1.02). (d) **The cause of the two throughput groups is not isolated**: it is not the variant, the round, the CPU (1b.9a pinned processes) or the tiering (8 processes each with `TieredPGO=0` and `TieredCompilation=0` keep the spread, 540-760 thousand count/s on the uniform protein); the page placement of the process or the host is the remaining candidate and was not tested; the orchestrator's 2.19 was not reproduced and not explained. (e) The texts are generated (the realistic protein is not a proteome, the 90-symbol text is not a corpus); 100 Mbp is DNA only, one container. (f) The in-memory comparison with `e4a0b2da` is in process, 8-12 processes, +-1-1.5 points: the gate figure 0.99 is at the resolution of the instrument. (g) The specialised descent was measured at 10 Mbp, with an 8-bit LCP base array and the tagged child table only; the 16-bit LCP and the packed layout were not prototyped. (h) The four-thread instrument measures wall time of barrier-released phases (3 repetitions): the first barrier and the stragglers are in it; it has 4 threads on 4 vCPUs and no spare core. (i) The mutants are single-site and hand-made, for the code of this package and for the code whose gaps it closed. (j) The harness run is 10 minutes, one master seed. (k) The packing of the pattern in `MappedSearch` (the other item of the 1b.9a list) was not measured.
- **Open points handed to 1b.9 and to the owner.** **For the owner (one decision, one gate not met):** **G3 is not met on one number**: the reader count of the file-backed tree costs **7.6 % of a count of present 20-mers on DNA at four threads** (6 processes, 0.911-0.938 per process; 3.6 % on the realistic protein; 0-4 % on locate and absent; the in-memory tree has no count and no cost). The cost is not the arithmetic of two atomic operations (about 15 ns of a 1.2 microsecond call) but their fence: a `lock`-prefixed instruction does not let the loads of the next query start before the previous one has finished, which costs about one memory latency (90 ns) per call where independent queries would otherwise overlap. The one remedy that removes it is an asymmetric protocol: a reader writes a count of a thread-exclusive slot with plain stores and reads the flag with no fence, and `Dispose` raises the flag, calls `Interlocked.MemoryBarrierProcessWide()` (a managed API, rare path) and then reads the counts; threads beyond the slots take the present atomic path. It needs a proof and the race tests of 1b.8 re-run, which did not fit this package; it is the item "the review of the reader-count protocol" of 1b.10, and the cost is the price of the present protocol until then. Either the owner accepts 7.6 % on that one metric for the file-backed tree (4 threads), or 1b.10 implements the asymmetric protocol. **For 1b.9:** the S1 gate numbers are to be measured on the build of this package (the in-memory tree is back at `e4a0b2da` speed, the file-backed tree is within 10 % in process on every text and faster at 100 Mbp); the benchmark definition of `analysis/em/readbench/README.md` is the one to use; the cross-process instrument needs the null experiment next to it. **For 1b.9c-1b.9f, 1b.10, 1b.11:** nothing of this package blocks them; 1b.10 should read the lifetime remarks of `EsaSuffixTree` (in-memory: no count, no release at `Dispose`; file-backed: the count) and the new damage kind of the corruption lane.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** **run on the final head** (production and test code of `60e5064f`; the later commits change documents and rows only): `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default in Debug (**3,878 passed + 10 skipped**, 10 m 39 s; 3,819 + 10 before: +59 tests), Exhaustive (**82 of 82**, Release, 17 m 46 s), Fuzz (**39 of 39**, Release, 14 m 41 s), the checked build (`-p:EsaBounds=true`, `ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true`: **3,879 passed + 9 skipped**, 7 m 29 s), `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (**535 of 535**, Release, 15 m 55 s: the builder and an EmProto test changed), the Bench build (clean), the 10-minute budget run of the 1b.8 harness (`run_1b8_fuzz.sh`, master seed 5, budget 600 s, 4 workers, head `60e5064f`: **110,905 cases in 766 s, 0 failures**: tiny 4,821, small 1,298, large 99, xl 7, corruption 104,616 cases (63,862 run, 40,754 skipped; the new kind `SealedLeafDepth` 5,337 cases, 4,674 refused at `Structure`), concurrency 64 cases with 11,040 thread runs and equal checksums; `rows_1b9b_fuzz.tsv`). The child tests of the Dispose race: 6 of 6 (5 of 1b.8 and the new in-memory test). **Skipped, with reasons:** `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (no file of `SuffixTree.Persistent` or its tests changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` / Ukkonen / `SuffixTree.Mcp.Core.Tests` / Vectors (`SuffixTree.Core` and `SuffixTree` untouched); no full-solution run.

Where the time went: orientation, the generators, the driver and the containers about 15 minutes; the cross-process baseline of six texts (21 processes per variant, 40 minutes of machine time) while the gap tests, the in-memory race test and the hygiene were written without a build; the first build, the race tests in three states (head, `e4a0b2da` behaviour, fix) and the ablation about 40 minutes; the LCP indexer and the 16-bit read with their paired runs about 30 minutes; the final timing blocks (in process, e4a0b2da paired 12 processes, four threads, null, cross-process, 100 Mbp, the orchestrator protocol) about 1 hour 40 minutes of machine time, during which the documents were drafted; the test sets (default 10 m, Exhaustive 18 m, Fuzz 15 m, harness 13 m, checked 8 m, EmProto 16 m) about 1 hour 20 minutes; the mutation audit 49 minutes (43 mutants, two re-runs); the documents (ADR-004 section, this entry, the register) about 40 minutes of work spread over the waits. Tokens: an estimate of about **0.4 M** (no counter reaches the implementer; the output tokens plus a tenth of the context written, the reckoning of 1b.8 and 1b.9a), below the forecast of 0.9-1.5 M because most of the clock was machine time and waiting, and below the cap of 2.0 M.

### S1b 2026-11-21 work package 1b.9d (the sparse tail of the container reserved, every mapped write site of the build listed and closed, the production chain under a page-cache limit, in `SuffixTree.Esa` (`MappedOutputFile`, `Dist`, `IoCounters`, one line of `SesaBuilder`), tests and the Bench command `sesa`; owner rule "no debts before S2"; one implementer context, no context compaction, no container restart)

- 2026-11-21 1b.9d [x] `116e015c` (tests first `eb32ee12`, the fix `a7207055`, measurements `48312730`, `6ae8ea5a`, tests for the survivors `c63ac759`, audit `116e015c`, `analysis/em/*1b9d*`, `analysis/mutation/*1b9d*`; this entry in the commit after them). **Outcome in one paragraph:** (1) **The window was real and is closed.** At 10^8 (`u32_100m`, LCP of four bytes, the container grows by 300 MB behind its reserved length) a tmpfs of 3.8 GB whose blocks a competing thread takes from the hook `lcp-placed` on ends the old build with **SIGBUS (exit 135), with and without PSI, the temporary file and its scratch left behind**; with the reservation the build without PSI ends (14.2 s, **the container byte-identical to the one built on the ordinary disk**) and the build with PSI meets the competitor at its next file, the BWT scratch, for the blocks that the build itself has freed: it ended with the `IOException` "Failed to create `bwt.bin` ... because the disk was full" and **nothing left** in the first run and with success in the re-run on the head (both are allowed, a signal is not). `MappedOutputFile.Grow` writes zeros into the range that it adds (`IReservationSource.ReserveRange`, default `ZeroFill`, 1 MiB `RandomAccess.Write`: managed, portable, the BCL refuses `PreallocationSize` for an existing file, tried) and gives the length back on any failure; `Dist`'s target is reserved too (it was the one other unreserved site: 18 sites listed in the ADR table, 2 were not reserved, both closed; the rest by `Create`, `Auto` = `fallocate`, or not mapped writes). (2) **Cost:** about 1 ms per MiB of growth, **0 for a build whose LCP does not grow (every build of `dna_100m`), 0.002 s of reservation at 10^8 for `genome_100m` (11 MB), 0.028 s for 179 MB, 0.052 s for 300 MB (0.3 % of the build) plus up to 0.3 s on the stores that follow**, the device bytes unchanged; reserving the worst case at the creation instead costs no time and 3 (n + 1) bytes of disk in every build (9 GB at 3x10^9): the owner's choice, point 1 below. The in-process A/B at 10^8 does not resolve 1-2 % (the null cell varies by +-7 % per pair: 1.069 [0.947-1.112] warm; 0.985 [0.933-1.078] cold with six balanced pairs; `u32_100`: 0.989 [0.926-1.107]); RssAnon of the single-process builds 262-303 MB against 320. (3) **Tests:** 26 new tests, 13 of the first 21 red against `a5def355` (9 by assertion, 4 by the death of the test host, which is the signal itself), then green; 6 older places changed. **Mutation audit: 54 mutants, 42 + 7 + 1 + 2 + 2 killed, closed by a test, killed by the death of the host and again by an assertion, argued equivalent, 0 open.** (4) **The production chain under a page-cache limit (E7 protocol, `dna_100m` and `genome_100m`, 4 threads, default parameters, 10^8 with the memory ratio of 3x10^9):** `dna_100m` unlimited 25.7 s; **effective cache 0.71: 1.73x [1.40-2.12]; 0.50: 5.05x [4.51-5.23]** (130 s, three runs each; the 3x line is at about 0.6); `genome_100m` unlimited 28.7 s, **0.80: 3.7x, 0.69: 10.7x, 0.55: 19.4x**; PH1 is unaffected, PH2 / PH3 is the floor (35-340 GB of device reads), the output side does not thrash (1.03 pages dirtied per byte written); a constant limit kills the build (95 and 84 MB: OOM after PH3, as E7's C65); every limited run that finished gave the byte-identical container. **The gate C of E7 (3x at 0.5x) is not met (5.05x; the prototype 3.6x); the abandon rule of 4.6 does not fire; the E3 rule (above 5x the design needs a minimum page cache) is reached below 0.55 (`dna`) and 0.7 (genome-like), the documented floor of about 1x stands.** The production chain is 1.3-1.6x slower than the prototype under the same cache pressure (not investigated). ADR-004 section 5a "1b.9d" has the tables.
- **Defects found.** In the build: the sparse growth (the subject); the sparse default target of `Dist` (not on the path of the build). Outside the scope, reported: `MappedFileStorageProvider` of `SuffixTree.Persistent` has the same window; the BCL ignores `fallocate` silently on a file system without it. In the tooling (mine): the first runner of the cache cells raised the limit at once (4 cells discarded, re-run), the first full-volume experiment (a ballast written once) proved nothing (B3 frees 8n bytes while it writes 3n), a test of 1b.7e would have written 5 GB (it ran, slowly, once).
- **Not verified / limits.** Native macOS, Windows and arm64 (the creation of every output there is still a free-space check and a sparse file); file systems other than ext4 and tmpfs (btrfs, ZFS, APFS, network and FUSE volumes, a Docker Desktop bind mount); a second process or a quota as the competitor; the window on a real device and at 3x10^9 (the figures at 3x10^9 are ESTIMATES by size); a difference of 1-2 % in the whole build (bounded from the inside, not resolved); the cause of the production chain's extra slowness under cache pressure; single-site hand-made mutants by the implementer.
- **Open points handed to the owner.** (1) Zero fill (Z, implemented) or the worst case at the creation (W: no time, +3 (n + 1) bytes of disk; the two could be an option). (2) A platform without `fallocate`: reserve every output by writing zeros (about 1 s per GiB) or document the window. (3) `MappedFileStorageProvider` has the same window (its own package). (4) The production chain under cache pressure is slower than the prototype and genome-like text needs more than 0.8 for 3x: look for the cause, or leave the documented floor.
- **Test sets run and skipped (policy of this file, level 2):** `SuffixTree.Esa.Tests` default in Debug (`dotnet test` in the project directory): **3,989 passed + 10 skipped** (3,963 + 10 at 1b.9c: +26 new tests), 12 m 16 s; Exhaustive (`dotnet test -c Release --filter "Category=Exhaustive"`): **82 of 82**, 17 m 26 s; Fuzz (`dotnet test -c Release --filter "Category=Fuzz"`): **39 of 39**, 14 m 50 s (both because `src/SuffixTree.Esa` changed); the checked build (`ESA_EXPECT_BOUNDS=true dotnet test -p:EsaBounds=true`, Debug; run although the new lines carry no `ESA_BOUNDS` conditional, because `Dist.cs` holds code that the checked build compiles differently): **3,990 passed + 9 skipped** (3,964 + 9 at 1b.9c), 12 m 3 s; `SuffixTree.Persistent.Tests` (`dotnet test`): **672 of 672**, 30 s; `SuffixTree.EmProto.Tests` (`dotnet test -c Release`): **535 of 535**, 16 m 14 s; the Bench application builds clean (`dotnet build apps/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench/SuffixTree.Esa.Bench.csproj -c Release --no-incremental`: 0 warnings, 0 errors). All on the head `116e015c` (the commit after it changes documents and result files only), one after the other with nothing else running. **Not run, with the reason:** Vectors (no SA or LCP kernel changed), `SuffixTree.Tests` (Ukkonen) and the MCP tests (no file of theirs or of what they test changed).
- **Cost of 1b.9d (wall clock by the machine clock; tokens are an estimate):** about **4.1 hours** of wall clock by the machine clock (00:50 UTC to about 04:55 UTC on 2026-11-21), against a forecast of 3-5 hours and a cap of 7 hours (80 % = 5.6 hours, not reached); no context compaction, no container restart; tokens about 1.2 M by my own estimate (forecast 0.9-1.5 M, cap 2.0 M, 80 % = 1.6 M not reached); where the time went: orientation and design 00:50-01:20, tests first and the code 01:20-01:40, measurements 01:40-02:50 (about 50 minutes of machine time for the cache cells), the mutation audit 02:50-03:23, the affected set 03:24-04:40, the re-run of the figures on the pushed head and the documents 04:40-04:55.
